# CompanyHub Operations Reference

Facts about hosts, background jobs, timers, sign-in, and Supabase.
Line numbers are from branch fix/2026-10-06-auth-identity at its head after the 2026-10-06 re-review fixes; sections 1, 2 and 5 from commit 6eb7a5f9 (main).
No secrets are in this file. Only env var NAMES are listed.

## 1. Hosts

| Host | Branch | Use |
|---|---|---|
| GitHub Pages (MattControlServiceCompany.github.io/companydashboard) | `main` | Production for routine deploys. Test host. No sync. (scripts/stamp-version.py:46-56) |
| Netlify (cscdashboard.netlify.app) | `stable` | Stable host. Only host that runs functions and sync. (app/ch-auth.js:70-73) |

- Promote to Netlify: `git push origin main:stable`.
- Cost: 15 credits for each Netlify deploy. Limit: 300 credits each month. The count resets on the 2nd.
  (Source: Netlify account rules told by Matt. Not in the code.)
- Netlify does not build on pushes to `main`. (scripts/stamp-version.py:46-52)
- Build config: no build command, publish root `.`. (netlify.toml:4-6)
- Release bump and the ?v= refs (2026-11-03, branch feat/2026-11-03-per-file-version): `python scripts/stamp-version.py` sets
  the site version (CH_VERSION in site-ui.js and index.html) and rewrites EVERY `?v=` on the script and CSS refs in
  energy-department.html, index.html and ems-leads.html. Each `?v=` is now the first 8 hex chars of the sha256 of THAT file
  (`ref_hash`, scripts/stamp-version.py; line endings read as LF). An unchanged file keeps its URL, so the browser downloads again
  only the files that changed. The old all-files number (`?v=217`) is gone. Runs by hand at release time, no schedule, no env var.
  The hash step runs after the CH_VERSION rewrite, so site-ui.js and index.html refs change on every release by design.
  How to check: `python scripts/stamp-version.py --check-refs` (exit 1 on any wrong hash; also run by `node scripts/ci-version-check.js`
  in the Release Checks workflow) and `node test-per-file-version-refs.js` (twice with no change = zero ref changes; one changed file
  = only the refs of that file change, one per page that loads it). `--refs-only` rewrites the hashes without a version change.
- Gate speed (2026-11-06, branch 2026-11-06-q3-expert-fixes): `check` in tools/gate-single-physics.js compiles the REQUIRED_FUNCS patterns once per run (`REQUIRED_FUNC_RES`), not once per line. Same results, about half the time. The gate kills one feature test after 240 s (scripts/regression-gate.js `runTest`), and tools/test-physics-single-source.js runs the check about 30 times. Check: `node tools/test-physics-single-source.js` (56 passed).
- Report PDF merged cells (2026-11-09, v260): app/report-pdf.js `_rptPdfFlattenTables` finds a cell's neighbours by grid slot (rowspan/colspan aware) and moves each row background onto the row's cells, because html2canvas paints a row background over the lower half of a merged cell. How to check: `node test-report-pdf-merged-cells.js`.
- Back/Forward after a release (2026-11-03, branch fix/2026-11-03-hard-refresh): app/report-engine.js (next to the `focus` handler in the init
  block) listens for `pageshow`. When `event.persisted` is true (Firefox page cache restore, old code still in memory) it runs
  `_checkForVersionUpdate()` at once, without the 60 s gap. That function makes one no-store GET of site-ui.js and compares CH_VERSION
  with RELEASE_NOTES[0].v. Same version or a failed fetch = nothing. A new version = the existing safe auto reload
  (`chSafeToReload`, once per version per tab). A Back/Forward that loads the page again is covered by the check on load.
  netlify.toml cannot do this: `netlify dev` overwrites a Cache-Control header rule with the static server's own
  (netlify-cli dist/utils/proxy.js). How to check: `node test-pageshow-version-check.js`. No env var, no schedule.

## 2. Netlify functions (netlify/functions)

| Function | What it does | Trigger | Env var names |
|---|---|---|---|
| kv-sync | Shared data store. GET manifest/keys/key. PUT with version check. Keeps change history. (kv-sync.js:210-245) | HTTP `/.netlify/functions/kv-sync` (app/db.js:10). No schedule. | SUPABASE_URL, SUPABASE_SECRET_KEY, AUTHORIZED_USERS, CH_TEST_JWKS_URL (test only) (kv-sync.js:78-79,91,125) |
| pdf-sync | Stores and returns PDF files. Single or chunked PUT. (pdf-sync.js:323-342) | HTTP. No schedule. | SUPABASE_URL, SUPABASE_SECRET_KEY, AUTHORIZED_USERS, ALLOWED_ORIGIN (optional), CH_TEST_JWKS_URL (test only) (pdf-sync.js:120-121,135,155,226) |
| calendar-fetch | Downloads one calendar feed (.ics) and returns a snapshot of its events. Stateless: it never reads or writes the database. Address must be https, host calendar.google.com, path ending .ics (lib/calendar/snapshot.mjs `checkFeedUrl`). Reader code: netlify/functions/lib/calendar/normalize.mjs (recurring events: 365 days back, 730 forward). Sign-in check: netlify/lib/auth.js `verifyAuth` (one copy, also used by kv-sync and pdf-sync). | HTTP POST `/.netlify/functions/calendar-fetch`, sent only when the user clicks Sync feed on the Project page, District Calendar tab (app/project-calendar.js `sync`). No schedule, no timer, no call on page open. About 2 MB download per call (the feed has no ETag). | SUPABASE_URL, AUTHORIZED_USERS, CH_TEST_JWKS_URL (test only) |
| supabase-keepalive | One read of one row in table `kv`. Stops Supabase from pausing. Returns 200 on success, 500 on failure. Logs HTTP status and duration. (supabase-keepalive.js:1-40) | Schedule `@daily` (netlify.toml:26-27). `@daily` = 00:00 UTC = 19:00 Central (CDT, summer) or 18:00 Central (CST, winter). | SUPABASE_URL, SUPABASE_SECRET_KEY |

How to check that a function works:
- Netlify dashboard, site cscdashboard, Observability, then Functions. Open the function and read the invocation log.
- keepalive: look for the log line `[supabase-keepalive] OK: HTTP 200 <n>ms` once each day. A line with `FAIL` means a problem
  (supabase-keepalive.js:11,21,25,28).
- kv-sync and pdf-sync: a 401 means a bad token. A 403 means the email is not in AUTHORIZED_USERS. A 502 means a Supabase failure.
  (kv-sync.js:152,160; pdf-sync.js header)
- Sync PUT bodies over 1 MB are gzip. app/db.js (_maybeGzipBody) sends JSON `{"gz":"<base64 of gzip(value JSON)>"}` with content-type application/json. The runtime never sees a binary body (netlify dev and the Functions runtime broke binary bodies). kv-sync.js `getRawBody` is the one decode path: it gunzips `gz` when present, else reads plain JSON. Netlify synchronous Functions accept a request body up to 6 MB (Netlify Functions limits), so 1.8 MB of gzip (about 2.4 MB as base64) fits. A failed queue entry keeps the server's error text. Check: test-kv-sync-gzip-body.js.
- Netlify scheduled functions only run on the published (production) deploy.

## 3. Client timers and polls

| Timer | What it does | Interval | When it stops |
|---|---|---|---|
| Queue drain (app/db.js `_drainQueueOnce` :1779, back-off timer `_scheduleDrain` :1812, consts at :33-35) | Retries unsynced data writes through `_putWithAuth` (one refresh-and-retry on 401, see section 4). It runs on page load, on `online`, after sign-in (`chAuthStateChanged`) and after a write that failed (`_enqueueWrite`). While this user has a queued item that a retry can still send, ONE back-off timer retries: 15 s, then doubling to 5 min; a drain that sends something resets it to 15 s. | 15 s up to 5 min, only while items are queued | The timer does not exist when the queue is empty, when the only items belong to another user, or when every item failed for good (HTTP 400, 413, 422). Sends nothing while sync is off. Check: test-sync-golive-blockers.js CREDIT4-CREDIT6. |
| Remote-change check (poll timer, VISIBLE TAB ONLY; `_armRemotePoll` and `_remoteCheckIfDue` in app/db.js call `_checkForRemoteChanges`; listeners and the first `_armRemotePoll` in `_startBackgroundSync`) | Asks the server whether the other user changed data, through `CH_AUTH.withAuthRetry`. It runs on page load (`_hydrate`), every 60 s while the tab is visible (`REMOTE_POLL_MS`, a `setTimeout` chain that is cleared when the tab hides and armed again on `visibilitychange`; restored 2026-11-02 after the go-live test saw no update for 270 s), when the tab becomes visible and when the window gains focus. It never runs while `document.hidden`, and at most once per 50 s (`REMOTE_CHECK_MIN_MS`) counted from the last manifest GET of any kind (`_lastManifestAt`, set in `_fetchManifestWithTimeout`). A key counts as changed by ONE rule, `_serverRowNeedsRoutinePull` (app/db.js, above `_hydrateInner`), shared with `_hydrate`: the server version is newer than the local stamp, OR the key is brand new to this browser (no stamp, nothing cached). So a key another user creates after this page loaded (for example the first task) is picked up by the next check without a reload. Changed keys are applied by `_hydrate` (merge, archive what loses), then `dbRemoteApplied` makes app/sync-ui.js send `chRefreshFromStore`; `chRefreshFromStore` in app/core.js (and the EMS Leads listeners in energy-department.html and ems-leads.html) reads the store into the page lists and draws the open view again (it first sends `chDropStoreCaches`; a module that keeps results derived from the store in memory clears them in its own listener: app/equipment-matrix.js, app/pricing-estimator.js (end of file), app/report-preview.js; `loadCoreLists(true)` also empties `dcEvents` when the store list is empty). The refresh is read-only: `loadCoreLists(true)` and `loadUtilityData(true)` run no migration, heal or backfill and write nothing, and they keep the current record objects (`chReconcile` in app/core.js, matched by id), so a save that runs after a refresh writes into the current record and both edits stay. No reload, no banner, no user action. app/sync-ui.js (`_refreshWhenSafe`) protects the user's work with one dirty mark per open view: a trusted input or change event inside `.view.active` (not inside a dialog) marks it dirty (`_markDirty`); the page lists still read the store at once (`chRefreshFromStore` with `draw: false`, lists only, no redraw), but a dirty view is not drawn again and the redraw stays pending with no timer. The mark clears and the pending redraw runs on `dataUpdated` (app/db.js `set`, sent on every local write) or `chViewChanged` (app/core.js `chViewChanged()`, called from `sv`, `showList`, `openDetail` and `sPTab`); a user switch (`chAuthStateChanged`) drops both. Values that script fills in never mark the view dirty. A save from a dirty view goes through the version-checked write and the conflict rule, unchanged. An open dialog or modal (`OPEN_DIALOG_SELECTOR`) queues the redraw (local 1 s check, no server call) until it closes; typing inside the dialog does not mark the view dirty. Scroll positions (page and panels), expanded rows and selected rows (by `data-id` or id) are kept. The Equipment Matrix also keeps its view (Audit, Raw, Summary, Counts, Sequence), edit mode, sort and column choices: its `chDropStoreCaches` listener (app/equipment-matrix.js) marks the open project in `_emKeepViewOnce` (only while the matrix tab is open) and the FIRST `emRenderMatrix` for the same project skips the reset to Audit View and clears the mark (`_emTakeKeepView`); no clock, so a redraw held back by edit mode keeps the view however long it waits. A tab switch or project change (`chViewChanged`, ignored while `window._chRefreshing` is set by the `chRefreshFromStore` listener in app/core.js) clears the mark. Filters are cleared. Check: tools/test-matrix-sync-write-and-view.js. Check: test-auto-sync-remote-refresh.js (dirty view: 0 redraws over 6 s; redraw after the save, a tab change and navigation; a script-set value does not hold it) and test-sync-golive-blockers.js POLL2 and REV2. A user switch or sign-out (`chAuthStateChanged`) clears the waits and the conflict-resolved delay timer. A key with a pending local edit is not announced: the edit stays, and the next check tries again (version-checked writes and the conflict rule are unchanged). Conflict history wording comes from `describeConflictEntry` in app/sync-ui.js (plain sentences, no internal codes; it names the record when the entry has one, for example `Equipment matrix, AHU-1 (Project name)`). The check sends no PUT. Writes still find a conflict by the 409 answer. Check: test-auto-sync-remote-refresh.js (headless browser: the open view redraws with no banner and no reload; conflict history wording) and test-sync-golive-blockers.js POLL1/POLL2 (the check itself) and CREDIT1-CREDIT3 (idle hidden tab = 0 calls; visible = exactly 1 manifest GET, none inside 50 s) and SYNCDEF1 (visible tab sees the change in under 65 s without a focus event; hidden 6 h = 0 calls). | none (events only) | Quiet while sync is off. |
| PDF queue drain (app/core.js `_pdfDrainQueueOnce`, const at :110) | Retries unsynced PDF writes through `CH_AUTH.withAuthRetry` (`_pdfWithAuthRetry`); sends only the verified user's own entries (`DB.entryBelongsTo`). The drain stops at once when the signed-in user changes during it (`_pdfUserChanged`, checked before each entry and after each await, same rule as the db.js drain): nothing more is sent, removed or stamped for the other user. | 15 s | Never. Also runs on `online` and on sign-in (`chAuthStateChanged`). |
| Tombstone retry (app/db.js:1134) | Retries failed delete sync, one-shot, with back-off | Variable delay | Clears when it fires or is replaced (db.js:1133). Skips if sync is off. |
| Session refresh (app/ch-auth.js:366, const at :49) | Refreshes the sign-in token if near expiry | 5 min (talks to Supabase Auth only when the token is within 5 min of expiry, about once an hour) | Never. Does not start when sync is off (ch-auth.js:363). |
| Version check (NO timer; `_checkForVersionUpdate` app/report-engine.js, listeners in the page-load init just above it) | Sends ONE small GET for the first 4 KB of `site-ui.js` (`Range: bytes=0-4095`, `cache: 'no-store'`), reads `CH_VERSION` from it ONLY when the answer status is 200 or 206 (any other status is ignored, no update bar) and compares it with the version this tab loaded (`RELEASE_NOTES[0].v`). If they differ it shows the sidebar link "New version: reload" and the page reloads by itself when `chSafeToReload` (app/report-engine.js) is true: no queued or in-flight write (`DB.getQueueDepth`, `DB.hasPendingWrites`), no unsaved extracted bills, no running extraction (app/tooltips-help.js), no open dialog (`window.chDialogIsOpen`) and no edited view (`window.chViewIsDirty`, both app/sync-ui.js). It shows the toast "Updating to the new version", waits 2 s (one `setTimeout`, checks again, then saves the page state and reloads). If it is not safe, the link stays and the same check runs again on focus or visible (no server call) and on `dataUpdated` and `chViewChanged`. One automatic reload per version per tab session (`sessionStorage` `ch_auto_reload_ver`), so a mismatch cannot loop. No ETag is used (a local server gives the same ETag every time). | none (events only): on page load, and when the tab becomes visible or gets focus, at least 60 s apart (`_CH_VER_MIN_GAP_MS`) | Never runs while `document.hidden`. Check: test-sync-golive-blockers.js CREDIT7 and CREDIT8. |
| Clock (app/site-functions.js:34; site-ui.js:774) | Updates the clock text | 15 s | Never. |
| Bill dump (app/bill-analysis.js:12768) | Copies the open bill to localStorage for debug | 2 s | Never. |
| OCR abort poll (app/bill-analysis.js:13989) | Checks the abort flag and OCR time budget | 250 ms | `clearInterval` when OCR call ends (:14011,:14015). |
| Stored-bill repair pass (app/auto-repair.js `scheduleAutoRepair`; see the section "Automatic repair of stored bills from their PDF page") | After each page load: waits 4 s, then `requestIdleCallback`, then re-reads stored PDF pages one PDF at a time, 50 ms pause between PDFs. Local only (no server call); runs only when `DB.isReady()`. | One run per page load | Ends when no bill is left without a marker for the current `EXTRACTOR_RULES_VERSION` (a missing PDF or a read error is tried once per load). |
| Bill review render (app/bill-corrections-review.js:1830) | Redraws the review modal | 400 ms | Cleared on next open (:1829) and on close (:1838). |

Many short `setTimeout` calls (toasts, URL cleanup, UI yield) are one-shot. They are not listed.

### Netlify credit cost (Matt, 2026-10-07)

Netlify Free plan: Function compute is 10 credits per GB-hour, plus requests. The old 60 s manifest poll cost about 1.1
credits a day for ONE open tab, 24 hours a day, even when nobody looked at it (1440 Function calls a day; about 0.0008
credits a call, from that figure). That poll was removed 2026-10-07. On 2026-11-02 a poll came back for a VISIBLE tab
only (app/db.js `_armRemotePoll`, one manifest GET a minute, cleared when the tab hides): 480 calls for 8 visible hours,
about 0.4 credits a day, and 0 for a hidden or closed tab. The text below about 5 min between checks now reads 50 s.

- No idle calls. A tab that is hidden or left alone calls no Netlify Function: 0 calls a day. Calls come only from the user:
  page load (manifest + the changed keys), tab visible again or window focus (one manifest GET at most every 5 min), and
  each save (one PUT). A queued write that failed retries on the back-off timer only until it is sent.
- Estimate per user per day, working about 8 hours: load 1 to 10 calls, visible-tab checks 10 to 40 (maximum 96 at 5 min
  apart), saves 50 to 200 PUTs. The version check adds about 0 (4 KB static GETs on focus). About 100 to 300 calls, or roughly 0.1 to 0.25 credits a day. This is an estimate, not a
  measurement. Check the real number in the Netlify dashboard (Observability, Functions, kv-sync, invocations per day).
- Every remaining timer that can call a Netlify Function or Supabase:

| Timer | Calls | How often | Credits a day (estimate) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Data queue back-off (app/db.js:1812) | kv-sync PUT | Only while this user has a sendable queued item: 15 s, 30 s, 60 s ... 5 min. None when empty. Worst case (server answers 5xx all day) 288 calls | 0 normally; at most about 0.2 |
| PDF queue drain (app/core.js:423, every 15 s) | pdf-sync, only when the PDF queue holds this user's items; the function returns at once when empty (`_pdfDrainQueueOnce`) | 0 calls when empty | 0 normally |
| Session refresh (app/ch-auth.js:511, every 5 min) | Supabase Auth token endpoint (not a Netlify Function), only when the token is within 5 min of expiry | about 24 a day per open tab, hidden or not | 0 Netlify credits (Supabase) |
| Version check (no timer; app/report-engine.js `_checkForVersionUpdate`) | 4 KB Range GET of static file `site-ui.js` (not a Function). Runs on load and on visible or focus, at least 60 s apart, never while hidden | A tab left open all day: one 4 KB GET per focus, about 0.1 MB a day | About 0 credits a day. Before this change: up to 96 MB a day per visible tab, about 2 credits |
| supabase-keepalive (netlify.toml:27, `@daily`) | One Supabase read | 1 a day | about 0 |

### Sync fixes 2026-11-02 (go-live test v194)
- Conflict redraw: after a same-field conflict is resolved (`_resolveCollectionConflict` for a list, `_saveOverServer` for any other key), app/db.js `_announceResolvedView` sends `dbRemoteApplied` (reason `conflict-resolved`); app/sync-ui.js redraws the open view when safe (no reload), after 2.5 s so the toast shows first. Check: SYNCDEF2, SYNCDEF2b.
- A merge of a collection key that equals the server row sends nothing (`_sameCanonical`); a merge of a background history list (calendar, BAS, audit log; `_isBackgroundList`) shows no "merged with yours" message. Check: SYNCDEF3d, SYNCDEF3e.
- Per-user flag `ch_qs_seen` is written under `<user id>::ch_qs_seen`; two users never share the row. Check: SYNCDEF3c.

## 4. Sign-in and allowlist

- Sign-in screen: index.html. It calls `CH_AUTH.signIn(email, password)` (app/ch-auth.js:198). Supabase Auth checks the password.
- Sync mode is `on` only on exactly cscdashboard.netlify.app and only when signed in. Every other host is `off`. (app/ch-auth.js:70-95; `_isNetlifyHost` :70, `backendMode` :82)
- Server check: every kv-sync and pdf-sync request must carry a valid Supabase token (checked against Supabase JWKS).
  The email in the token must be in env var AUTHORIZED_USERS (comma-separated list). (kv-sync.js:134-163, 125-129)
- To add or remove a user: change AUTHORIZED_USERS in Netlify site settings, then redeploy.
- Demo login is off on the sync host. The button is hidden and `loginDemo()` returns at once. (index.html:1188-1190, 1241-1243)
- Startup: app/db.js waits for the first token refresh, `CH_AUTH.ready()`, for at most 8 s (`_authReady`, db.js:2051-2059)
  before its first server request, so a stored token that already expired is never sent. A slower refresh does not block
  loading from the local copy.
- The ONE rule for a server answer of 401/403 is `CH_AUTH.withAuthRetry` (app/ch-auth.js:327; `_onServerRefusal` :297):
  401 = refresh the token once and send the same request again; a second 401, or any 403, ends the session (sync off,
  signed-out bar, every timer quiet). A refresh that comes back as a different user never retries the request. The
  remote-change check, the data queue drain, the live write and the PDF queue drain all go through it.
- A failed token refresh (app/ch-auth.js `_startRefresh`, rule `_refreshRefused`) ends the session only when Supabase
  REFUSED the refresh token: HTTP 400, 401 or 403. A network error or a 5xx (Supabase paused) keeps the session; the
  5-minute timer, or the next 401 on a request, tries again. How to check: with the network off, the signed-out bar
  must not appear; the offline banner does.
- Closing the browser signs the user out (2026-10-07; app/ch-auth.js `_store`, `_askPeers`, `_acceptPeerSession`,
  `_withRefreshLock`). The session `ch_sb_session` (access + refresh token) lives in per-tab sessionStorage, never
  localStorage. A reload keeps it. A new tab has none, so it asks the open tabs over BroadcastChannel `ch_auth` (message
  `need`; a signed-in tab answers `session`) and waits at most 400 ms (`CH_AUTH.settled()`; index.html and app/core.js wait
  for it before they choose between the app and the sign-in screen). No answer = signed out. Every sign-in, refresh and
  sign-out is broadcast (`session` / `signout`), so a sign-out in one tab signs out all tabs, and a different user
  signing in on one tab is followed by the others. Only one tab refreshes at a time (`navigator.locks`
  `ch_auth_refresh`); a tab that waited for the lock uses the session a peer already refreshed and makes no token call.
  A refused refresh first asks the peers once (400 ms) for a newer session before the tab signs out. Needs no env var.
  An old localStorage `ch_sb_session` is deleted at load and never used, so each user signs in once after this release.
  The offline queue (`ch_sync_queue`, IndexedDB), the per-user cache, `ch_last_user` and `ch_local_identity` are NOT
  touched on close: signed out = sync off, queued edits wait; the same user signing in again sends them once; a
  different user never sends them (`_entryBelongsTo`). Do not add any clear on pagehide/beforeunload. Limits: if the
  browser restores tabs on start (Chrome "Continue where you left off", Ctrl+Shift+T, Edge restore) it restores
  sessionStorage too and the user stays signed in; page code cannot stop that. The server-side refresh token stays valid
  until sign-out or revoke (Supabase Free has no inactivity limit; not checked in the dashboard); the client now keeps it
  only while a tab is open. How to check: node test-sync-golive-blockers.js ("8a:" to "8l:" tests); in DevTools
  Application, `ch_sb_session` is under Session Storage and absent from Local Storage; close all windows, open the site:
  the sign-in screen shows.
- A token refresh result belongs to the session it started from (app/ch-auth.js `_refresh`; the one check is
  `_sameStoredSession`: same user id and same refresh token in storage). The tab's session `ch_sb_session` is kept equal to
  the other tabs' by the broadcasts above. After the Supabase answer it is read again. If another tab changed it meanwhile (a different user signed
  in, or signed out) the result is dropped: nothing is saved, nothing is applied, and this tab follows the stored session
  (`chAuthStateChanged` fires, db.js bumps its identity epoch). A REFUSED refresh (400/401/403) also ends only the session
  it was for; a session another tab stored meanwhile is kept. How to check: node test-sync-golive-blockers.js, the four
  "7a:" tests (real ch-auth.js with a held token request).
- A 401 on a data write or on the queue drain: one token refresh, then the same request again (`CH_AUTH.withAuthRetry`,
  called from `_putWithAuth` in app/db.js, the one place a refused write is reported). The "server refused this sign-in"
  bar (`dbAuthRejected`) shows only when the final answer is still 401/403 and the session ended. If the refresh could not
  reach Supabase, the session is kept, the offline banner shows, and the write stays queued for the next drain.
  How to check: in DevTools Network, a PUT answered 401 is followed by a `/auth/v1/token` call and a second PUT (200);
  no red bar appears.

## 5. Supabase

- Free project. It pauses after about 7 days with no activity. (Pause window is a Supabase plan rule, not in the code.)
- A pause took the site down on 2026-10-06. (supabase-keepalive.js:3)
- Prevention: the `supabase-keepalive` function (section 2) runs each day and reads one row from table `kv`.
- If the project is paused: restore it in the Supabase dashboard, then check the keepalive log.

## 6. Sync write rules (app/db.js)

- No write before the first full pull (2026-10-21, branch 2026-10-21-sync-safety-client). In backend mode 'on', nothing
  goes to the server until `_hydrateInner` finished every read for the signed-in user (`_pulledUid`, rule
  `_hasFullPull`, app/db.js; public `DB.hasFullPull`). ONE gated send: `_sendKvPut` returns `held-no-pull` for every
  caller (live write, drain, merge-push inside the pull, first-connect upload, conflict resend) and each caller queues
  the value (`_enqueueWrite`). A merge-push made during a pull therefore waits until the whole pull is complete. The
  only bypass is the named flag `allowBeforeFullPull: true` in the payload, set by `restorePush` alone (an explicit
  user restore of one key at a known server version); it is never sent on the wire. A failed manifest, batch read,
  drift read, deletion-record read or an identity change leaves the gate shut. A successful pull releases the queue
  and fires the window event `dbFullPull` once per sign-in. `warmCache` waits for a pull when a READ failed
  (`_pullReadsFailed`): `_holdForFirstPull` retries after 1, 2, 4, 8 s, then every 15 s, so the page does not start on
  an empty cache; a browser that already holds data (`_cacheHasContent`) starts after 3 tries and retries in the
  background every 30 s (`_scheduleBackgroundPull`) and on `online`. A 401/403 answer (`_pullRefused`) ends the wait
  (the "server refused" banner shows). While it waits, each retry fires `dbOfflineBanner` with `waiting:true`; the
  existing small offline banner in app/sync-ui.js (`offlineBannerText`) then reads "Could not load <what failed> from
  the server. Nothing is saved until it loads. Retrying automatically." with the technical detail on hover. No new
  timer when sync is off. Check: node test-sync-golive-blockers.js (SYNC-SAFETY 1, 2, 5 tests).
- Queued write versus the pull (app/db.js `_queuedWriteBlocksPull`, `_resolveStaleQueuedWrites`). A queued write
  stops the pull from taking the server copy of its key only while the server version is not newer than the version
  the write was based on (`entry.baseVersion`; none recorded counts as older). A newer server copy wins: the write
  leaves the queue and goes to the normal conflict path (`_handleConflict`: list merge or later-save-wins; the
  replaced value archived). Only the signed-in user's OWN waiting write counts, for shared
  keys too (`_queuedEntryFor`, `_hasQueuedWrite`): another user's write never blocks or opens a conflict for this
  user. Check: SYNC-SAFETY 3 tests, IDENTITY 2.
- Same-field conflict, no dialog (2026-11-05, app/db.js `_handleConflict`, `_saveOverServer`, `_applyLaterSaveWinsRecords`). When
  a write meets a newer server row (HTTP 409, a stale queued write, or a first-load difference) nobody is asked. Lists
  (`_collectionCfg` keys) merge per record; for a record changed on both sides, and for every other key, the LATER SAVE
  WINS: this write is sent again at the server version just seen with `explicitOverwrite`, cause "Sync conflict: later
  save wins". The server value it replaces is copied to the local Conflict history (reason `write-conflict-later-save-wins`
  or `record-conflict`, `losingSide: 'server'`) and kv-sync.js copies the replaced row to `kv_history`. The user sees only
  one of two toasts, each defined once in app/db.js (`TOAST_SAVED_OVER_EARLIER`, `TOAST_MERGED`): when the later save replaced a value (same field, list or whole key) "Saved. Another user's earlier change to this item is kept in history."; only a merge with no clash says "Changes saved by another user were merged with yours. Nothing was lost." A newer 409 on the re-send runs
  the same rule again (bounded by `CONFLICT_RESEND_CAP` = 4, then the retry queue). A deletion on either side follows the
  same rule. A key with no version stamp whose local value differs from the server (first load) keeps the server copy and
  copies the local value to Conflict history (`_reconcileIncoming`, reason `hydration-server-wins`); nothing is sent.
  The conflict dialog (`SyncConflictUI.showConflictModal`, `#ch-conflict-overlay`) and the held-draft feature
  (Decide later, `DB.getHeldDrafts`) are removed. A queue entry saved with `held:true` by an older build is now a
  normal queued write: it is sent, meets the same rule, and the replaced value is archived. Check:
  node tools/test-conflict-auto-resolve.js, node test-sync-golive-blockers.js (M2 same field, SYNC-SAFETY 3, SYNCDEF2/2b).
- Identity safety (2026-10-21, app/db.js). A queued or stale write made as user A is never sent as user B:
  `_sendKvPut` refuses an entry whose owner is not the signed-in user, the drain sends only own entries, every
  sign-in needs its own full pull (`_pulledUid` is reset in `_handleAuthIdentityChange`, so A returning after B must
  pull again before any send). `_clearPerUserLocalState` also removes from the page and the version map any shared
  key that holds another user's UNSENT edit; `_reapplyQueuedPerUserValues` puts it back, with
  `entry.baseVersion` as the stamp, when its author signs in again (a newer server copy then goes to the conflict
  path for A). Before this, B saw A's unsent value on the page and a stale edit of A opened a conflict for B
  with A's value. Check: IDENTITY 2-6 tests.
- Edit made while signed out (2026-11-05, app/db.js `_handleAuthIdentityChange`, `_checkDurableIdentityMarker`).
  Cause of the lost edit (found with a local two-user run): the sign-in page (index.html) loads db.js but never runs
  `warmCache`, so its in-memory queue is empty. The sign-in event ran `_handleAuthIdentityChange`, which wrote that empty
  queue (`_persistSyncQueue`) and the identity marker over the stored ones. Now the handler does nothing until the stored
  state is loaded (`_stateLoaded`, set by `_loadSyncQueue`); the next page's `warmCache` reads the real queue. SAME user
  signed in again: the edit stays queued and goes out through the normal conflict path (list merge or later save wins;
  the replaced server value is archived). `_checkDurableIdentityMarker` also calls `_reapplyQueuedPerUserValues` for the
  signed-in user, which puts back the base-version stamp that the sign-out removed, so the edit is sent with its base.
  DIFFERENT user signed in: the edit stays queued under its author, is never sent under the new identity, and the new user
  sees the server copy; it is sent when the author signs in again. Check: node test-sync-golive-blockers.js, tests 8m and 8n
  (a fake IndexedDB records that the sign-in page writes neither `ch_sync_queue` nor `ch_local_identity`), IDENTITY 2-3, 7d.
  A sign-out blanks the stored identity marker, so the marker alone cannot show that a different user arrived. At every
  load `_checkDurableIdentityMarker` calls `_clearForeignQueuedSharedKeys(currentUid)`: each shared key with a queue entry
  owned by someone else is removed from `_cache`, `_replicaVersions`, `_syncBase` and storage (`_persistStamp`,
  `_rawDelete`), so the signed-in user loads the server copy. `_uploadFirstConnect` skips such keys
  (`_hasForeignQueuedWrite`). Check: test 8p (edit after a real sign-out, then another user loads, then the author returns).
  A queued edit of a list key (a key with a merge config, `_collectionCfg`) stores its merge base WITH the entry
  (`entry.base`, set in `_enqueueWrite` from `_syncBase`). `_reapplyQueuedPerUserValues` puts it back into `_syncBase`
  together with the stamp version `entry.baseVersion` (one pair, then `_persistStamp`) when the author returns, so the
  3-way merge uses the author's own base, not a copy another user synced meanwhile. An entry with no base (older build)
  removes `_syncBase` for the key: the merge has no base, so it is a union and deletes nothing. If the browser cannot
  store `ch_sync_queue` (localStorage fallback full), `_rawSet` shows the same error toast as a failed save. Safety net: `_archiveServerRecordsDropped` copies every server record that a merge removes without a field
  conflict to the conflict history (reason `merge-removed-server-record`), and the re-send uses explicitOverwrite so
  kv-sync.js also keeps the replaced row in kv_history. A history entry for a replaced SERVER value is owned by the
  person who wrote it (`_ownerOfServerValue`, from `updatedBy`), not by the signed-in user. Check: node
  test-sync-golive-blockers.js, tests 8q, 8r, 8s, 8t.
- Legacy utility blob (2026-10-21, app/utility-data.js), backend mode 'on' only (`_backendOn`); the GitHub site runs
  as before. `_legacyBlobToMigrate(customerId)` is the ONE rule for "copy the old `en_utility_<projectId>` blob into
  `en_utility_<customerId>`": the customer key does not exist at all (an existing blob, empty or not, is never
  replaced) and a project of that customer has a legacy blob with buildings. `_selfHealCustomersAndScope` applies it to
  already-healed projects; `saveUtilityData` never writes an empty auto-created blob (no load snapshot) while that rule
  finds a legacy copy. `loadUtilityData` writes nothing (no heal, no migration, no backfill) before the full pull
  (`_loadWritesAllowed`); it runs again, complete, on `dbFullPull` (`_loadWritesDeferred`). Check: SYNC-SAFETY 2 and 3
  utility tests.
- Signed-in name (2026-10-21, app/core.js, app/ch-auth.js). On the Netlify site, a page with a live session and no saved
  `ch_user` shows the session email (`CH_AUTH.sessionUser`); the GitHub site is unchanged. Check: SYNC-SAFETY 3 tests.
- A `DB.set` sends a PUT only when the canonical hash of the value differs from the hash stamped at the last sync
  (`_valueChanged`, one rule). Opening a pane or loading a page with no edit sends nothing.
- One PUT in flight per key (`_replicateWrite`). A write that arrives while one is in flight waits and is sent after
  the answer; the newest waiting value wins. Two PUTs for one key never overlap.
- How to check: open the browser DevTools Network tab on the sync host, filter `kv-sync`, reload the page and open the
  Building Performance and Savings Projection panes. Expected: zero PUT requests.
- Nothing is saved without a user edit: project progress is computed on read (app/core.js `projectProgress`, never
  stored on the Dashboard tab); recurring-meeting agendas and their tasks get ids from the project id and meeting date
  (app/csv-import.js `stableNumericId`), so two browsers generate one identical record; the Building Performance and
  Savings Projection panes save only from their inputs and buttons (`bpSave`, `bspSave`), never on open, and
  `getBspCfg` is the one reader of the Savings Projection settings with defaults. How to check: open a project
  Dashboard tab and both panes with the Network tab filtered on `kv-sync`: zero PUT.
  Same rule for zoom: opening the Equipment Matrix only reads `en_em_zoom` and paints it (`emApplyZoom`, app/equipment-matrix.js);
  only the zoom buttons save (`emSetZoom`). `setTableZoom` (app/site-functions.js) saves to local storage only on a click, not on a re-apply.
  Raw per-user keys (`RAW_PER_USER_LOCAL_KEYS`: `ch_theme`, `ch_settings`, view and order prefs) stay raw local keys, never sent to the server. On an identity change app/db.js `_clearPerUserLocalState(prevUid, nextUid)`
  parks the leaving user's values under the local key `ch_user_local::<userId>::<key>` (never in a backup: `isNeverBackupKey` lists the prefix) and puts back the next user's own values, so A keeps Light and the tip settings after sign-out/sign-in or A->B->A and B
  does not get A's. Quick Start seen flag `ch_qs_seen` and dismissed tips `ch_dismissed_tips` are not raw: app/tooltips-help.js reads and writes them with DB.get/DB.set (per-user, synced), and `chFirstVisit()` (app/tooltips-help.js) is the ONE first-sign-in rule: app shown, first server pull done (`DB.hasFullPull()`), `ch_qs_seen` not set; it sets the flag at once. `autoQuickStart` (overlay) and the tip bubbles (`_chShowFirstVisitTips`, `attachBaselineSaveTip`) both call it, so a later load shows neither; `chAuthStateChanged` resets the in-page latch. How to check: node test-quickstart-after-signin.js. How to check: node test-sync-golive-blockers.js (theme test), node test-em-zoom-no-write-on-open.js.
  Table prefs: the Cost Estimate table keeps column widths, hidden columns and a schema marker in `ch_tbl_*_pricing_tbl_<projectId>` (app/pricing-estimator.js).
  Render never writes them: `_pricingMigrateColSchema` returns when nothing is stored; `_pricingSetColWidths`/`_pricingSetHiddenCols` (user resize/hide/show only) also stamp the schema marker.
  How to check: node test-pricing-tblprefs-no-write-on-render.js (6 pass); Network tab, filter `kv-sync`, open JOCO Cost Estimate: zero PUT.
  Phase over allowance is a data notice: `console.warn` (not error) in `_pricingComputeRecommendedTimeline`.
- Version stamps are one local record per key, `ch_rv::<key>` = `{ stamp: {version, hash}, base? }` (app/db.js
  `_persistStamp`, the one writer; `_setSynced` the one stamper). A tab writes only the record of the key it synced, so a
  second tab can never overwrite the first tab's stamps (the old whole-map `ch_replica_state`/`ch_sync_base` are split once
  on the first load and removed). These records never sync and are never in a backup (the one prefix is `SyncClassification.RV_PREFIX`; `isNeverBackupKey` covers it for `siteBackup` and `RestoreMerge.isEngineKey`).
  How to check: DevTools, Application, IndexedDB, CompanyHub store: keys `ch_rv::en_projects` etc.; no `ch_replica_state`.
- Auth/session keys (`ch_sb_session`, the refresh token) are never in a backup file and a restore never writes them. The one
  list is the `neverBackup` entries of `PER_USER_CH_ENGINE_EXCLUSIONS` (app/sync-classification.js), read through
  `SyncClassification.isNeverBackupKey`. `siteBackup` (app/site-functions.js) drops them and `RestoreMerge.isEngineKey`
  (app/restore-merge.js) skips them. How to check: node test-sync-golive-blockers.js, the "(e):" test; open a new backup
  file and search for `ch_sb_session`: no match.
- Queue entries with no owner tag (saved by v2026.10.06.83 or older) are never sent (`_retireOwnerlessEntries`, runs when
  the queue loads). Each goes to Conflict history as `queue-entry-no-owner` with its value, leaves the queue, and its
  version stamp is dropped so the next load compares the local copy with the server. How to check: after one load the
  "N unsynced changes" bar is gone and Sync status, Conflict history lists the entries.
- Conflict history (`en_conflict_archive`, local only) is one list per browser. Every entry carries an owner tag
  (`_appendConflictArchive`, same shape as a queue entry; it stores a COPY of the losing value, so the merge that follows cannot change an archived version). The one visibility rule is `_archiveEntryVisible`, applied by
  `DB.getConflictArchive` (the one reader: the viewer and the export button call it): an entry for a shared
  key is shown to everyone on this browser; an entry for a per-user key (`ch_*` prefs, zoom levels) only to its owner.
  Another user's per-user entry, or one saved by a build before owner tags, stays in storage, is never shown and is never
  removed by this user (`clearConflictArchive` removes only entries the user can see). app/sync-ui.js closes the panel on `chAuthStateChanged`.
  There is no sidebar link and no count in the sidebar (2026-11-05: sync needs no user action). The list opens only from
  Sync status, button "Past conflicts (N)" (`_renderArchiveEntry`, shown when N > 0). How to check: node test-sync-golive-blockers.js, the "7c:" tests.
  Reset (`siteResetData`, site-functions.js) exports only the entries this user can see (`DB.getConflictArchive`), never
  another user's. The wipe KEEPS the entries this user cannot see (`DB.getConflictArchiveOthers`, written back after
  `DB.clear()`): they are neither exported nor deleted. The backup (`DB.getAllForExport`, then `siteBackup`) holds the same
  visible list only, and never `ch_sync_queue` (unsent edits may belong to another user). An old per-user queue entry with no
  owner tag is archived with NO owner (`_retireOwnerlessEntries`): shown to nobody, never sent, kept. How to check:
  node test-sync-golive-blockers.js, the "F2/F3:" and "F4:" tests.
- Identity guards on the write path (app/db.js). `_sendKvPut` is the ONE sender: if the edit's owner id is not the signed-in
  user at send time it returns `stale-identity` (no PUT, no stamp), so a value waiting behind another PUT or a hash check is
  never sent with the next user's token or key. A successful live write (`_writeOne`) removes older queued entries for the
  same key and owner, so the drain cannot replay an old value. A per-user edit whose author signs out while it is in flight (`stale-identity` in `_writeOne`, app/db.js) is queued under the AUTHOR's id (`payload.owner`), never sent as another user, and drains when that author signs in again. A live server row with no value (or null) is unreadable:
  `_reconcileIncoming` keeps the local copy and its stamp and raises `dbHydrateFailed`. `_reconcileIncoming` also returns without applying when the local value changed, or a write got queued, during its hash await: an edit made in that gap is never replaced (test REV3). A refusal (403 or second 401) in a
  tab whose cached user differs from the stored session takes over the stored session (`_onServerRefusal`), it never clears
  it. How to check: node test-sync-golive-blockers.js, the "F1:", "F5:", "F6:" and "later successful write" tests.
- Savings %: `getBspCfg` (app/utility-data.js) is the ONE reader of a building's `savingsPct` (default 11, a stored 0 stays 0).
  utility-data.js, report-engine.js and graphics-setpoints.js all call it.
- Local per-user stores on a shared browser (app/db.js). The one "a write for this key is still waiting" rule is
  `_hasQueuedWrite` (hydration skip, first-connect upload skip, `_valueChanged`): a queued entry counts for a shared key
  whoever owns it (same server row), and for a per-user key only when it is the signed-in user's own (`_entryBelongsTo`),
  so another user's queued pref never blocks this user's own row from loading. On an identity change
  (`_clearPerUserLocalState`, live or hard refresh) the per-user cache values, raw localStorage prefs, AND every per-user
  version stamp `ch_rv::<key>` (also one with no cached value) are removed; queue entries are never deleted. Merge bases
  and `ch_deleted_items` hold collection keys only (shared). `ch_last_user` holds the last user's id and email (the
  "N unsynced changes by <email>" bar reads it). How to check: node test-sync-golive-blockers.js, the "7d:" tests.
- The auth session `ch_sb_session` (access and refresh tokens, app/ch-auth.js) is classified local-only at the one place
  that decides what syncs (app/sync-classification.js `PER_USER_CH_ENGINE_EXCLUSIONS`). It never gets a wire key, is never
  uploaded by the first-connect upload, and is never removed by the identity-change sweep. It is now in sessionStorage; the rule stays so a
  localStorage copy can never sync. This matters in localStorage-fallback mode (IndexedDB unavailable), where db.js loads every localStorage key into its cache. How to
  check: node test-sync-golive-blockers.js, the "(a):" test; `SyncClassification.classifyKey('ch_sb_session')` is
  `local-only`.

- Restore has 5 modes. "Merge, keep whichever copy is newer" (`'newer-wins'`) is the same as backup-wins but keeps a strictly newer server record. The next paragraph describes backup-wins.

- Restore mode "Make this backup the server copy" (`'backup-wins'`, app/restore-merge.js `mergeValue` and `plan`, dialog in
  app/site-functions.js `_restoreDialog`). It reuses `_restoreApply` and `DB.restorePush` (no second upload path): a key that
  is absent on the server is inserted at version 1 (`baseVersion` null); a key that differs is replaced at the server version
  just read, with `explicitOverwrite`; kv-sync.js keeps the old copy in `kv_history` for every changing write (see "kv_history on every write"). A key whose
  content already equals the backup is not sent. Rules: a plain key or map takes the backup value; EVERY record collection
  key (`en_projects`, `en_customers`, `en_tasks`, `en_dc_events`, `ems_leads_v1`, the audit log, report history,
  `en_utility_cust_<id>` with its buildings, meters and bills, `en_eqmatrix_cmaps_*`) is a union by id in which the BACKUP
  record wins field by field on a matched id (Matt worked only on the GitHub site since 2026-08-19, so the backup is newer);
  records only the server holds are kept, never deleted. The one exception is `en_presented_savings` (frozen client figures):
  add-only. The old server copy of every changed key stays in `kv_history`. legacy `en_utility_<id>` keys are
  skipped; nothing is deleted; a tombstoned key stays deleted unless ticked; a per-user key goes only to the signed-in user
  (`DB.restoreScope`). A key that would still remove records (a plain key whose nested lists lose items) is HELD: listed with
  the count, unticked, not sent until the user ticks it (`opts.allowRemoval`, `item.held`). After the apply the dialog shows
  one row per key: OK with the action ("inserted as v1", "overwrote vN with vN+1, old copy kept in history"), Failed with the
  HTTP status (`DB.restorePush` returns `httpStatus`: 409 for a conflict, the server code otherwise), Skipped or Not sent with
  the reason. A second run with the same file finds server = backup and sends nothing. How to check:
  node test-restore-merge.js ("backup-wins" tests), node test-sync-golive-blockers.js ("migration R1-R3"),
  node tools/test-backend-mode-default.js (scenarios G1-G3: a synthetic backup with 5 missing keys incl. one 2.3 MB key,
  differing keys and collection keys with server-only records).
- Unstamped differing key (app/db.js `_hydrateInner` drift loop). A key with no `ch_rv` stamp, a real local value and a
  live server row that differs takes the server value; the local value is copied to Conflict history first
  (`_reconcileIncoming`). No dialog (the `_driftNeedsAsk` rule is removed, 2026-11-05). Same content is adopted silently.
  Collection keys merge per record. How to check: node test-sync-golive-blockers.js, "migration H1-H6".
- Failures name the key. The first-connect upload keeps `{key, status, httpStatus}` for every failed key (a 409 counts as
  failed) in `DB.getUploadProgress().failures`; the toast and the Sync status panel list up to five keys as "key (HTTP 502)"
  (`DB.describeFailure`, the one text function). A queued write that keeps failing is listed by `DB.getQueueFailures()` with
  its HTTP status (400, 413 and 422 are marked permanent); the Sync status panel shows them under the queue count.
  How to check: node test-sync-golive-blockers.js, "migration U1/U2" and "migration D1".
- One-time utility fix code (dates, sewer usage, therms/MMBtu, facKW, rates) is removed from app/utility-data.js
  (2026-10-21); the shared data already holds the fixes, so a new browser never re-runs them. Their old marker keys are no
  longer listed in app/sync-classification.js. The one on-load pass left there is the facKW fill, which has no marker.
- Settings (`ch_settings`, `ch_projTabOrder*`, `ch_defaultProjTab`, `ch_tbl_*`) are per-user keys (the `ch_` rule in
  `PER_USER`, app/sync-classification.js). `bcr_result_*` (saved bill review results) is shared; `bcr_pdftext_*` (PDF text
  cache) is local-only. How to check: node test-sync-classification-settings.js.

## 6c. Go-live cutover (one time): make the Netlify site hold the GitHub-site data

Why: Matt worked only on the GitHub site since 2026-08-19. The server holds the August seed. The GitHub site never syncs.
Matt's steps, in this order:

1. On the GitHub site: Backup. Save the file. This is the fresh backup. Do not edit data on the GitHub site after this.
2. Open the Netlify site (cscdashboard.netlify.app). Sign in as yourself first. Do not sign in as another user first.
   Per-user settings go to the signed-in user.
3. No conflict window opens. A setting that differs on load keeps the server value; this browser's value goes to
   Conflict history. The old value is also kept in the server history.
4. Open Restore. Choose the backup file from step 1.
5. Choose "Make this backup the server copy". Read the table. Read the list "would remove records that the backup does not
   have". Leave those items unticked. Tick one only after you compare it.
6. Click Restore. A safety copy of the current server values downloads first. Keep that file.
7. Read the result list. Every row must say OK or Skipped. A Failed row shows the key name and the HTTP status. Send that
   list to the developer.
8. Click "Close and reload". Open Restore again, choose the same file and the same mode. The button must say
   "Nothing to change". If it lists items, read them.

Nothing is deleted by this restore. On a record in both, the backup value wins, unless the server copy has a strictly newer updated-at stamp (kept, counted in the dialog); a record only on the server stays. A key the backup lacks is not touched. Old server values are in `kv_history`
(kv-sync.js `snapshotHistory`). To undo one key, read its row in `kv_history` in the Supabase dashboard.

## 6d. Backup importer: scripts/migrate-seed.js (Matt runs it; agents never hold the key)

What it is: a Node script (scripts/migrate-seed.js) that loads a backup file into the Supabase `kv` table and the `pdf_blobs`
bucket. It talks to Supabase directly, so it costs 0 Netlify credits and works while Netlify has no deploy credits.
The old whole-key `--force`, `--live` and `--dry-run` flags are gone. Merge rule: it calls `RestoreMerge.mergeValue(key,
server, backup, 'newer-wins')` (app/restore-merge.js), the same function as Restore "Merge, keep whichever copy is newer". A
record only on the server stays. A record in both is replaced by the backup record whole in the importer only (it passes `{ replaceWhole: true }` as the sixth argument of `mergeValue`; app Restore does not, and keeps every field the backup lacks) (a field only the server record has, such as a stale `updatedAt`, is dropped; the nested list and the derived meter cache fields are kept; the old value goes to `kv_history`; the dry run counts it as changed and the diff file names the lost fields). On top: a backup record is dropped when the server
copy has a strictly newer `updatedAt`/`updated_at`/`modifiedAt`/`lastModified`. No timestamp = the backup wins. This
"newer wins" rule lives INSIDE `mergeValue` (app/restore-merge.js, `recordTime`/`curIsNewer`, 'newer-wins' mode only; 'backup-wins' is an unconditional overwrite that lists the newer server records it overwrites). It runs at
every level (building, meter, bill, pricing entry, plain-key object). The app Restore "Merge, keep whichever copy is newer" uses the
same function, so the app and the importer give the same result, except that the importer replaces a matched record whole. A key is HELD (no write, listed in the diff file) when the
server has a whole item the backup lacks: a list item, a map entry, or a plain field of any type in a plain-key object. A field lost inside a matched record is not a hold (see above). Name a held key in `--allow-removal`
to write it.

Modes:
- Default = dry run. No write. Console shows counts only (keys and records: new / changed / server-only / identical). The full
  diff (record ids, every record the backup overwrites) goes to `<report-dir>/YYYY-MM-DD-HHMM-supabase-import-diff.json`
  (default `AI/_context/live-data/diffs/`). The diff names nested records (level + id path), the field names that change, are
  added or are lost, and why a key is held. Field names only, never values. The script refuses any report or snapshot folder inside the repo.
- Server for the diff: `--server-file <image.json>` (offline, no key) or `--target prod|staging` (GET only).
- `--apply --target prod|staging` writes. Order: read server rows, save them to
  `AI/_context/live-data/snapshots/YYYY-MM-DD-HHMM-supabase-snapshot.json` (stop if that fails), then per key one `kv_history`
  row (the old server row) and a version-checked PATCH (a key that changed on the server meanwhile is reported as a conflict,
  never overwritten), then new keys at version 1, then PDFs (write-once, same-hash re-run is a no-op).
- Target safety. Needs ONLY the target-specific values: URL = env `SUPABASE_URL_PROD` or `SUPABASE_URL_STAGING`; key = env
  `SUPABASE_SECRET_KEY_PROD` / `SUPABASE_SECRET_KEY_STAGING`, else the Windows Credential Manager generic credential
  `companyhub-supabase-<target>-key`. The generic `SUPABASE_URL` and `SUPABASE_SECRET_KEY` are NEVER read. There is no `--url` and no
  `--cred-target`. A missing value stops the run (resolveCreds, scripts/migrate-seed.js). A URL ending in `/rest/v1/` is cut to the bare origin (normalizeSupabaseUrl). The PDF stat treats HTTP 404, and HTTP 400 with a Storage body of statusCode 404 (NoSuchKey), as missing; any other 400 stops the run (pdfStorageStat). The log prints the target name and the host
  (`Server: WRITING to target=prod host=<host>`), never the key. No prompt. The key is never printed or stored.
- `kv_history` rows written here are not trimmed (kv-sync.js trims on its own writes: 20 rows or 30 days a key).
- Failed writes (applyPlan, applyPdfFiles, fetchWithRetry, scripts/migrate-seed.js). Each kv, kv_history and PDF write is tried up to 4 times (RETRY.attempts) with 1 s, 2 s, 4 s waits and a 180 s timeout per try. The same timeout and retry apply to the reads (kv row read, key list, Storage stat and download), so a hung read cannot stop the run silently. Retried: a network error, a timeout, HTTP 408, 429, 500, 502, 503, 504 and 520-524 (isRetryableStatus). Any other status (a 4xx, 501, 505) is not retried. A retry that gets 409 with the same hash on the server counts as inserted (the first try wrote the row and lost the answer); if the re-read itself fails, the FAILED line says so. A PATCH that matches 0 rows, or gets a 2xx answer that is not JSON, reads the key: the same hash on the server counts as updated (patchKvRow); a different hash is `conflict-version-changed`. The history snapshot is idempotent: before the write and before every retry the importer looks for a kv_history row with the same key and version (historyRowExists) and writes none if one exists, so a lost answer or a re-run after a failed PATCH adds no duplicate row. kv_history has no unique constraint; no schema change was made. A write that still fails is listed by name: `FAILED key <key> status=error http=<status> reason=<server message>` (or `FAILED pdf <canonical key> ...`). The key name and a shortened server error are printed (scrubServerText: the error code and the first 80 characters of the message). The secret key is never printed. A server error can quote a fragment of the row, so the cut is to 80 characters; it is not a guarantee that no data text can appear. Conflicts and history failures are listed too. If N > 0 the last line is `APPLY INCOMPLETE: N failed` and the process exits with code 1; `APPLY COMPLETE` prints only when 0 failed. If keys were HELD (not written) the last line is `APPLY COMPLETE WITH N HELD key(s) NOT WRITTEN` and the re-run will still show them as changed. Run the same command again: it writes only what is missing. A kv value over KV_VALUE_MAX_BYTES (25 MiB of JSON) is shown as `OVERSIZE` in the dry run and is not sent by --apply (it fails by name). That limit is an importer guard, not a Supabase number: the largest value proven to write is the 9 MB Equipment Matrix key. The app reads and writes that value through netlify/functions/kv-sync.js, which gzips bodies to stay under Netlify's ~6 MB function cap (comments at kv-sync.js:44-48 and 178-181, measured 2026-07-19: 9.03 MB to about 0.35 MB), so a large importer value does not need a smaller limit for the app. An empty backup value (`empty-skipped`) adds 0 to the record counts.
How to check: `node test-migrate-seed.js` (38 tests, in-memory fake Supabase, no network). Then the same command without
`--apply` must show 0 new and 0 changed.

Nov 2 runbook, data step (after the Netlify deploy and the live acceptance run):
1. On the GitHub site: Backup. Do not edit there after that. The newest PDF export also (a PDF export newer than 20260930 does not exist yet).
2. Check out the exact commit that is deployed. Run the dry run: `node scripts/migrate-seed.js --file <backup> --pdf-dir <pdf folder> --target prod`. Read the counts. Open the diff file. Held keys: compare each, then decide.
3. Run it with `--apply`. The snapshot file name is printed; keep it. Rows written: see the "Data written" line.
4. Run the dry run again. It must show new=0 and changed=0 (PDFs: to upload=0).
5. In the Netlify app: Restore the same file in "Make this backup the server copy" mode. The button must say "Nothing to change" (6c step 8).
Rehearse the same steps on a staging project first (a second free Supabase project with netlify/db/schema.sql applied and a private
`pdf_blobs` bucket). Undo one key: read its row in `kv_history`, or copy it from the snapshot file.
Schema grants: netlify/db/schema.sql gives `service_role` (only) explicit grants on `public.kv`, `public.kv_history` and the sequence `public.kv_history_id_seq`, because Supabase stopped automatic Data API grants on new public tables on 2026-10-30 (existing tables are unaffected). Run the file in the SQL editor of any new project (staging included). `node tools/gate-schema-grants.js` (in the regression gate) fails if a `create table` in netlify/db/*.sql has no RLS or no `service_role` grant, or if any grant targets anon.
The app Restore path (6c) is on main only, not in stable (v87), until the Nov 2 deploy.

## 6b. Bill fields (merged 2026-10-06, fix/2026-10-05-hidden-fields)

- Retired stored bill fields. Bill rows no longer store copies or roll-ups next to the real fields (thermCost, kwCost,
  kwhCost, otherCost, taxCost, total*Rate, facKWCost, kwh on gas bills, fromPDF). `getBillOwnUnitRate` is removed.
  `getStoredRate(bill, type)` (computations/rates.js:155) is the ONE rate function every page calls: it computes cost over
  usage and reads the old stored rate only when the bill has no cost or no usage. `getExtractedRate(parsed, type)`
  (computations/rates.js:300) is a different value: the preview rate of a freshly extracted PDF, before the bill is saved.
  `billHasPdf` (computations/rates.js) is the one "has a PDF" answer. The Edit Bill modal round-trips no hidden inputs.
- Bill-save-fields gate (computations/bill-save-fields.gate.js). Fails when a bill-row builder in app/bill-analysis.js
  writes a key that is not a BILL_SCHEMA key (app/csv-import.js) or on the short META list, when `openBillModal` renders a
  hidden `bl-` input, or when `billHasPdf` is not the one PDF answer. How to check: node computations/bill-save-fields.gate.js
  (exit 0); node tools/test-rate-single-source.js.
- No-grey-text gate (tools/test-no-grey-text.js, run by the regression gate as a feature test). Rule (Matt, 2026-10-22): all text is #ffffff on dark and #000000 on light; no grey, navy or slate text; de-emphasis by size, weight or italic, never color or opacity. The test loads index.html, ems-leads.html and energy-department.html (home, projects, every project tab, synthetic data) headless in dark and light and fails on any visible text whose computed color is not #000/#fff and not a saturated accent (HSL saturation >= 0.45, lightness 0.2 to 0.78), on placeholders that are not, and on text faded by opacity. No allow-list. Tokens: --text, --text2, --text3 are one value per theme in each page style block (site-ui.css was never linked by any page and is deleted). The gate judges each text against the fill it sits on (dark fill #fff, light fill #000), puts a disabled button and a select on every screen, and runs Dark, Light, System-dark and System-light plus a live OS flip. Chart text: SharedCharts.textColor() (lib/shared-charts.js) reads --text. Check: `node tools/test-no-grey-text.js --verbose` (needs NODE_PATH to the Playwright copy).
- Sticky no-bleed gate (tools/test-sticky-no-bleed.js, run by the regression gate by glob; Matt, 2026-10-29: text must never scroll behind other text). Standard in lib/theme.css (all pages): every position:sticky/fixed box over scrolled content has an opaque background token (never rgba/transparent; a tint goes in background-image), a z-index from --z-sticky-col (20) or --z-sticky-head (25), and an offset flush with its scroll container (inside .view use top: var(--sticky-top-view), which cancels the 16px view padding). Source is the Utility Data Bills table (`.ud-bill-tbl th.sticky-col`, commits deadbe5b, a8c5f2e5). The test opens index.html, ems-leads.html and energy-department.html (home, PDF / OCR with a 12-bill synthetic file at 1280, 960 and 640 px, projects, every project tab with 36-bill synthetic meters), scrolls every scroll container down and sideways, and fails (page | selector | reason) when scrolled text paints above a sticky/fixed box, shows through it, or shows in a strip above it. It also injects known-bad probe boxes (must be flagged) and a good one (must pass), and reads source: no position:sticky with a translucent background. No timer, no network, no env var (CH_GATE_BROWSER=firefox selects Firefox). Check: `node tools/test-sticky-no-bleed.js --verbose` (about 40 s).
- Readability contrast gate (tools/test-readability-light-contrast.js, run by the regression gate by glob; Matt, 2026-10-29, readability items 4, 5, 6, 19). Light theme defines `--green` #166534 and `--amber` #92400e (energy-department.html light block). Projected Savings card text (app/core.js) uses `--violet-text` / `--violet-label` (dark: #c084fc / #a78bfa as before; light: #6d28d9). Equipment Matrix coverage and readiness text (app/equipment-matrix.js) uses `var(--green)` / `var(--warn)` in the stats bar and totals, and `var(--amber)` on the tinted likely-match cells (`--warn` is 4.3:1 there). The pinned Total column of the Utility Data bills table has a left box-shadow (`.ud-bill-tbl .sticky-col-right:not(.td-actions)`). The test reads the source, then opens energy-department.html headless (bundled Chromium, synthetic project, non-GET and Netlify/Supabase requests blocked), light theme: every visible text colored by those tokens on Dashboard, Equipment Matrix and Utility Data must be >= 4.5:1 on its composited fill, plus fixed probes with the matrix cell styles; and every pinned Total cell has a box-shadow in light and dark. No timer, no env var except NODE_PATH. Check: `node tools/test-readability-light-contrast.js` (about 20 s, prints PASS).
- Quarter label (app/report-engine.js `_rptQuarterLabel(q, year)`, Matt 2026-11-02). The one formatter for a quarter name in the quarterly report: "Q3 2026", never "Third Quarter". Cover title, page footer, chart bars, table headers and page titles call it. No timer, no env var. Check: `node tools/test-report-quarter-number-labels.js`.
- Page number position (2026-11-05): "Page N of M" sits in the white area below the letterhead wave, bottom right. Word: `_DOCX_FOOTER_DISTANCE_TWIPS` 200 (footer distance 10pt, set on the section in `_docxAssemble`), `_DOCX_FOOTER_RIGHT_OUTDENT_TWIPS` 360, a 1pt paragraph under the number (`_docxApplyPageNumberFooters`, app/docx-writer.js). Agenda PDF: `PDF_PAGE_NUM_BASELINE_FROM_BOTTOM_PT` in `buildMeetingPDF` (app/csv-import.js). Quarterly report PDF: `RPT_PAGENUM_DIV` (app/report-engine.js). No timer, no env var. Check: `node tools/test-report-word-layout.js` section 5, then look at the last page crop.
- Theme and System mode (lib/theme.js, lib/theme.css; linked in the head of index.html, energy-department.html, ems-leads.html (css only)). The stored choice is dark, light or system in ch_settings.theme and ch_theme (per-user keys, same path as before; existing Dark or Light users are unchanged, default stays dark). `chTheme.effective(pref)` (lib/theme.js) is the ONE function that decides the theme on screen; `data-theme` on html is always the effective dark or light. System follows the prefers-color-scheme media query live, no reload, and fires the `ch-theme-change` window event; lib/shared-charts.js listens and re-reads the chart text color. `chTheme.apply(pref)` saves and paints (siteApplyTheme calls it); `chTheme.applyAccent(hex)` sets the accent and `--on-accent` (white on a dark accent, black on a light accent; .btn-em uses it). lib/theme.css holds the rules every page needs: color-scheme per theme, explicit select colors from the tokens, and the shared disabled-button cue (dashed border, not-allowed cursor, pole text). Check: node tools/test-no-grey-text.js --verbose; in Settings choose System and change the OS color mode.
- The regression gate needs a backup that has presented records (`en_presented_savings`); the oracle checks the presented client figures from it.
- The regression gate uses the backup named in the oracle (`_input.backupFile` in regression-oracle.json, file in Downloads), not the newest one. If it is missing the gate fails. `--data` overrides. When you rebuild the oracle on a new backup, set `_input.backupFile` to that file (`--capture-baseline` sets it only if empty).
- Regression gate (scripts/regression-gate.js). Run: `node scripts/regression-gate.js` (about 830 s on 2026-11-09, exit 0 = pass). It
  checks pages and numbers against the oracle, runs scripts/verify-report-reconciliation.js, and runs EVERY `tools/test-*.js`
  and repo-root `test-*.js` / `test-*.mjs` as its own child process (a non-zero exit is a FAIL). The static gates, the reconciliation and the
  feature tests share one job pool: 3 child processes at a time, the known slow jobs first (`SLOW_FIRST`); a feature test stops after 240 s, a gate or the reconciliation after 600 s. New test files
  are found by glob. Tests that fail or hang on main, or need the internet, are listed in `TEST_EXCLUDE` in the gate with the
  reason and show as INFO; remove an entry when the test is fixed. Playwright comes from the env var
  `CH_PLAYWRIGHT_NODE_MODULES` (default `C:/Users/Matt Miller/AI/_context/tools/playwright-runtime/node_modules`, a permanent copy of the same Playwright version so the installed Chromium matches);
  the gate passes it to child tests as NODE_PATH. No junction or npm install is needed in the primary checkout or a worktree.
  Scratch folder (env var `CH_TMP_ROOT`, required): every test and tool puts browser profiles and scratch files under the folder named by
  `CH_TMP_ROOT`. The ONE owner is `tools/tmp-root.js` (`tmpRoot`, `mkTmpDir`, `tmpPath`, `profilePath`, `rmTmp`). It throws a clear error when
  the variable is not set; there is no default and no fallback to `C:/Temp` or the OS temp folder. Set it for the shell before you run any
  test, for example `$env:CH_TMP_ROOT = '<context dir>\temp\<dated-folder>\run'` (PowerShell); the folder is made when needed. Delete it
  when you finish with `python -I "<context dir>\tools\2026-11-03-clean-temp.py" <path>`. `rmTmp` deletes with `fs.rmSync` and 20 retries
  at 500 ms, so a Windows EPERM or EBUSY (a browser still holds a file) is retried before it fails. `tools/test-ocr-hidden-tab.js` also needs
  `CH_PDFJS_CACHE`: a folder with `pdf.min.js` and `pdf.worker.min.js` (pdf.js 3.11.174, saved once from cdnjs; the test never downloads and
  never writes into that folder).
  Keep the `CH_TMP_ROOT` path short (2026-11-08): the browser profile is `<root>/<tag>/<tag>-<task>-profile-<ms>-<pid>`. When that path is about 180 characters or more (for example a root inside a long dated worktree folder), Chromium cannot store page data on Windows (path limit 260), and `tools/test-matrix-sync-write-and-view.js` fails with `waiting for locator('#em-raw-btn')` while it passes alone. Use a short root such as `<context dir>	emp\<date>-g`. Check: the same test passes with a short root and fails with a long root.
  Temp-leak guard: the gate needs `CH_TMP_ROOT`. It makes a run tag `ch-gate-<pid>-<start ms>`, makes ONE run folder `<CH_TMP_ROOT>/<tag>`,
  sets `CH_TMP_ROOT` to that run folder for every child test, and passes the tag in the env var `CH_GATE_TAG`. `tools/launch-browser.js`
  (`launchBrowser(task, opts)`) puts the profile in the run folder (`<tag>-<task>-profile-...`) and `ctx.close()` there also deletes the
  profile. The gate's own work folder is `<tag>-work`. After the tests the gate lists the run folder: any entry left in it is a FAIL and the
  gate deletes it, then removes the empty run folder. Profiles of other processes live in other folders under the root and are never
  looked at, so they never count as a leak of this run.
  Flag-badge onclick gate (tools/gate-flag-badge-onclick-escape.js, in the gate's static list; run alone: `node tools/gate-flag-badge-onclick-escape.js`). Loads `udFlagBadgeHtml` (app/utility-data.js), feeds ids with `&quot;`, quotes and ampersands, decodes the onclick like a browser and fails if script runs or the decoded arguments differ from the ids. `udFlagBadgeHtml` builds each id with `_escHtml(JSON.stringify(id))`.
  Real-id gate (scripts/gate-real-ids.js, called from the gate's leak-guard group; run alone: `node scripts/gate-real-ids.js`).
  It runs `git grep -IcwF -f <list>` over all tracked files. Any hit = FAIL, printed as file name and line count only (never the
  value). The private list (one real account or meter id per line, never committed) is read from the env var `CH_REAL_IDS_FILE`.
  It is set as a Windows user environment variable on the work machine (`[Environment]::SetEnvironmentVariable('CH_REAL_IDS_FILE', <path in _context/reference/private>, 'User')`), like the other gate settings.
  Unset or missing file = SKIP (INFO, exit 0). A new shell is needed to see a new user variable.
  Browser tests must use `launchBrowser`. Other scratch files must use `tools/tmp-root.js`; `os.tmpdir()` and `C:/Temp` are not used in `tools/`, `scripts/` or the repo-root `test-*.js` files.
- Check routine (Matt, 2026-11-09). Run the gate on a snapshot worktree (detached at the commit), never on a tree you edit; one full gate at a time.
  While you work: `node scripts/regression-gate.js --fast` (about 100 s). It checks syntax of the files changed against origin/main plus untracked
  files, every oracle number, the pages of 1 project and the fast static gates. It skips the reconciliation, the feature tests and the slow gates
  (`SLOW_GATES`), and its last line says "REGRESSION GATE (FAST)". It is not a release check.
  Before a release, both: (1) the report check, `python -I "<context dir>\tools\<report check script>" <repo> <seed backup json> <work dir under _context\temp>`
  (the script name is in memory reference_frozen_snapshots). It exports the quarterly report data and the report PDF from <repo> and compares them with the newest frozen snapshots in
  `<context dir>\ground-truth\2026-11-09-frozen-snapshots` (write-once, SHA-256 manifest, hash-chained INDEX.jsonl). Any baseline change, any Q1/Q2 change,
  any page layout change or a frozen figure that is gone = "REPORT CHECK FAIL" and exit 1. Other changes are listed in compare-data.xlsx and compare-pdf.xlsx.
  A presented report or a baseline change that Matt approves = a new dated snapshot (`2026-11-09-frozen-snapshot.py freeze`); never edit a snapshot.
  (2) the full gate. Progress lines print while it runs: the end of each section, each FAIL at once, and a count every 20 feature tests.
- Bill CSV header matching (app/csv-import.js `ci()` inside `parseBillCsv`). One rule for every column: exact header first,
  then whole-word match. A header with a cost, id or date word is claimed only by an alias of the same class, so
  "Energy Cost" is not kWh, "Bill ID" is not cost and "Total" is not an end date. How to check:
  node tools/test-csv-header-match.js (exit 0); node tools/test-csv-partial-reimport.js.

## 6d. HTML escape (2026-10-07, branch 2026-10-07-shared-escape)

- `_escHtml(s)` (lib/formatting.js) is the ONE HTML escape. It encodes & < > " ' and turns null/undefined into an empty string.
  Every page calls it. index.html, energy-department.html and ems-leads.html load lib/formatting.js before app/sync-ui.js.
  The 20 private escapers (_esc, esc, emsEsc, baEsc, _bcrEsc, ...) are removed. Do not add a new one.
  The two Word XML escapers (_docxEscapeXml in app/docx-writer.js, _sooXmlEsc in app/soo-generator.js) write XML, not HTML, and stay.
  How to check: node tools/test-single-html-escape.js (exit 0). It fails on a private copy or a page that does not load the module.

## 6e. PDF viewer after reload (2026-10-08, branch 2026-10-08-pdf-view-after-reload)

- At extraction, `_storeExtractionPdf(b64)` (app/bill-analysis.js) keeps the source PDF ONCE in the bill PDF store with `bpaStoreBlob` (key en_pdf_shared_<hash16>, same store as attached bill PDFs). Only the key goes into sessionStorage `ch_extraction_state` (pdfKey) and `ch_queue_state` (results[].pdfKey). The base64 never goes into sessionStorage.
  Compact PDF Storage keeps these blobs: the key is already the canonical hash key. Same file = same key, so no duplicate copy.
- The extraction copy is local only: `pdfStore(id, b64, {localOnly:true})` (app/core.js) skips the server upload and records the key in localStorage `ch_pdf_local_only`. No upload at extraction, so no Netlify cost for unsaved PDFs. Any save that points a bill at that key calls `pdfEnsureUploaded(key)` (via `bpaStoreBlob` deps.ensureUploaded) and queues one upload. Normal save paths store under their own key with plain `pdfStore` (upload as before). Check: node tools/test-pdf-upload-gating.js.
- After a reload, `_reloadExtractionPdf()` loads the PDF from the key (called from initUtilityTool in app/report-engine.js). A missing PDF drops the key: values show, no View PDF button.
- `_showExtractionToolbar()` is the ONE function that shows View PDF and the debug button; the More list holds the debug button and the Side by side switch.
- PDF dock tabs (2026-10-19, app/bill-analysis.js `_renderPdfDockTab`, `setPdfDockTab`): the dock has two tabs, PDF and OCR text. The OCR text tab shows `_pdfOcrTextForSelectedBill()`: the text the reader used (`window._pdfRawText`), cut to the selected bill's page or page range, monospace, scrolling in the dock. It is the ONE reader of that text. The Extraction Output JSON panel, its toggle and the Raw Text button are removed; Save Debug still saves the full debug file with the JSON. Messages that the old panel held (progress, errors, non-bill text) show in `#pdfStatusBox` through `_pdfStatus(html)`. A batch run sets `data-queue-busy` on `#pdfBody` (`_pdfSetQueueBusy`), which hides the dock and the top row. The OCR text is not kept after a page reload (`rawText` is not saved in session storage); the tab then says to run the file again. No timer, no network, no env var. How to check: open PDF / OCR, extract a bill, click View PDF, click the OCR text tab; or run node tools/test-pdf-view-after-reload.js.
- PDF / OCR building, utility and period choice (2026-10-20, app/bill-analysis.js `_pdfGroupBills`, `_pdfBuildingName`, `_pdfBillIssues`, `_pdfNavModel`, `_pdfSelectorsHtml`): `_pdfGroupBills(bills, warnings, multi)` is the ONE grouping. It gives one group per account (the building) with the bill count, the number that need review, and the utility types inside it (bill count and warning count per type). `_pdfNavModel(bills)` is the ONE model of the open file (groups, types, periods newest first, the month of each bill through `billMonth`). The top row reads it: a building choice (a list when the file has 2+ accounts, plain text when it has one; each name ends with "(N to review)" when any of its bills do), a utility choice (only the selected building's types), and a billing period choice (month, "(to review)" on a bill that needs a look). A bill needs review when `_pdfBillIssues` says so (a field warning or error, charge lines that do not add up to Total Current Charges, a gap before the period, or a held bill). No whole-PDF warning or period count shows in this view, and the extraction method badge is removed (`showExtractionBadge` is deleted). The OCR text tab hides every internal %%NAME%% marker line (%%PAGE_n%%, %%POSITION_READ%%, %%POSITION_READ_END%%, %%POSITION_HELD%%) with one filter in `_pdfOcrTextForSelectedBill`. No timer, no network, no env var. How to check: node tools/test-bldgname-needs-review.js (exit 0).
- PDF / OCR page layout (2026-10-20, branch 2026-10-20-pdf-ocr-layout; app/bill-analysis.js, energy-department.html): when a bill is open, the values are in the left column and the PDF dock is in the right column, side by side, open by default (below 800 px wide the dock sits above the values; `_clampPdfDockWidth` keeps 400 px for the values). View PDF (`#pdfViewBtn`) sits in the page header. `_showExtractionToolbar()` opens the dock by itself unless Side by side is off (More list). Above the values there is ONE top row (`#pdfTopBar`, drawn by `_pdfRenderTopBar`, the same place for every file kind): building, utility and period choices, then status chips, then the one Save control, then More. Chips (count 0 = hidden): "N to review" (red), "N already saved - N differ" (amber), "N warning(s)" (amber), "N fixed" (green). A chip opens a list that overlays the page (`#pdfTopPop`, no push-down; closes on a click outside or Escape). The lists come from ONE place: `renderPDFFields` builds the review, warning and fixed lists of the selected bill (held reasons from `_gateReasons`, the held total correction, sum mismatch, a charge over the total, errors and warnings, the billing gap once, the values the reader fixed with Revert) and stores them in `window._pdfBillChips`; `_pdfDupChip` builds the duplicate list from `_pdfDupView` (one line for each bill that differs, "Building - Utility - Month: Field saved X, new Y", opening to a compare table and Overwrite / Merge / Attach PDF only / Skip through `_dupBannerAction`; one line for all that match the saved copy).
