The old full-width banners (Duplicate Bills Found, flagged for review, sum mismatch, line over total, total correction held, second billing gap, duplicate strip, Data Analysis, Auto-corrected notice, auto-assign banner) and the bottom save bar are removed. A flagged value shows an outline (red for review, amber for a warning), the reason under it and a "Use $X" button when a held proposal exists (`_proposed_<field>` or the held total correction); a fixed value shows a small "fixed" mark and its hover text gives the method. A "Use" click and a typed edit both go through `_pdfApplyFieldEdit` (the ONE edit path). The Save control by file kind (`_pdfSaveKind`, `_pdfSaveControl`): one bill = Save (`savePDFData`); many periods of one building = Save all N periods (`savePDFAllBills`), the other ways to save (Overwrite all, Merge all, Attach PDF only, one utility only, Save all to the matched meter, Save including held bills) are in the More list; many buildings in one file = no Save button in the top row (see the review bar below), the Overwrite all / Merge all / Attach PDFs only / Save including held bills rows are in the More list; a batch = Save all (N) (`saveQueuedBills`), with Overwrite all / Merge all / Skip all for already saved bills in the More list; batch counts come from `_pdfQueueStats`. The "Saves to" line (`#pdfSaveRow`, text from `_pdfSavesToText`) is the first line inside the values; Change opens the project, building and meter choices that the save code reads. No timer, no network, no env var. How to check: open PDF / OCR, extract a bill; run node tools/test-clear-pdf-resets-all.js, node tools/test-bldgname-needs-review.js and node tools/test-pdf-view-after-reload.js.
- PDF / OCR multi-building file review (2026-10-29, branch feat/2026-10-29-ocr-review-bar; app/bill-analysis.js, energy-department.html): the separate Review and Save window (`#pdfMultiBldgPanel`, `#pdfSavePop`) is removed. Two parts replace it, both drawn by `_mbRenderReview` from `_pdfRenderTopBar` (so they redraw on every bill switch and every change): (1) `#pdfBillDest`, a box above the selected bill's values (`_mbBillBoxHtml`): "Bill N of M", the status ("N issues" or "Ready to save"), the Saves to line, the destination issue from `_pdfDestIssue` (No matching meter, No matching building or meter, Meter found by address only, More than one meter could match) with Create meter / Create building + meter (`_mbCreateForRow`) and a meter picker (`_buildDestCell`), Include in Save All, Save this bill, Skip. Its left edge is red (an error), amber (values to check) or green (no issue). (2) `#pdfReviewBar`, a bar at the end of the values that sticks to the bottom of the window (`position: sticky`, its own place in the flow, so it never covers the last row): the counts "N bills - N ready - N with issues", one link per issue (`_mbGoToIssue` opens that bill and goes to the field or the box), and Save All (N) (`confirmMultiBuildingSave`, disabled with the reason from `_mbClassifyRows`). Single source: `_pdfBillIssues` now includes the destination issue; `_pdfBillIssueItems` lists every issue of a bill and is the ONE list that the bar links, the box count and `_pdfBillStatus` (utility buttons, period labels) read, so a bill with an issue is never green. Same account or meter number: Create meter on one bill (`_mbSetCreateTarget` -> `_mbLinkSameMeter`) sets every unmatched bill of the file that `_billIdentityHitsMeter` says goes to the same meter to "Goes to new meter (from bill N)" and checks it; Save All then makes one meter (the duplicate guard in `_autoCreateMeterAndSaveBill` uses the same `_billIdentityHitsMeter`). A charge row with no quantity and no rate (Customer Charge) starts at the left edge. No timer, no network, no env var. How to check: node tools/test-ocr-review-bar.js (exit 0; SHOTS=<folder> saves dark and light screenshots at 1920 and 640 px), node tools/test-save-all-selected.js, node tools/test-bldgname-needs-review.js.
- PDF / OCR extracted values (2026-10-21, branch 2026-10-21-extracted-values-v136; app/bill-analysis.js `renderPDFFields`, energy-department.html `.ef-grid`, `.ef-item`, `.ef-bar`): the values are boxed cells (label on top, value below) in a 2-column `.ef-grid`, in the section order of the commodity layout (`FIELD_LAYOUT`: Account Info, Billing Period & Meter, Meter 1/2 only when present, Charges). A charge row reads quantity | rate | charge | running total; the Total Current Charges row shows the running check (green match, red difference). The values pane sits beside the PDF, so `#extractedFieldsGrid` is a container (`container-type: inline-size`): under 560 px of PANE width (not window width) the grid is one column. A field shows ONCE as an editable cell (`_shownKeys` in `buildCell`); a second place that needs the number (a charge row quantity) shows a read-only copy. An empty optional value is hidden unless it is flagged, differs from the saved bill, is likely missed, or is required (`_REQUIRED_FIELDS`). Cell edge and icon: red `is-error` (missing or held), amber `is-warn`, green `is-fixed` (the reader fixed it; hover box has Revert), orange `is-size`; the hover box lists the reasons. A held fix shows a `Use $X` button in the cell (`_pdfApplyFieldEdit`, the one edit path, also used by typing). Header `#extractedFieldsHdr`: the title line, the red or amber Data analysis bar (N missing = the to-review count, N warning = the warning count) with a link per flagged value, and Manual Assign. The bar and the top-row chips are built from the SAME `_issueList` in `renderPDFFields` (`_chipReview`, `_chipWarn`), so their counts always match. `_pushIssue` merges one problem into one line (billing period problems are one line). No timer, no network, no env var. How to check: extract a bill in PDF / OCR; node tools/test-extracted-values-grid.js and node tools/test-clear-pdf-resets-all.js pass.
- A batch (2+ files) saves BOTH `ch_queue_state` and `ch_extraction_state`. Restore (initUtilityTool) takes the queue first; `_restoreQueueState` then drops the extraction copy. Each batch result has its own pdfKey. `ch_queue_state` queueRows keep only resultIdx, billIdx, checked, _saved, _held (renderQueueResults rebuilds bill/result), so the state is small.
  How to check: node tools/test-pdf-view-after-reload.js (exit 0). It uses the real file input, one file and a 2-file batch, and reloads twice.

## One month rule: billMonth (2026-10-13, branch 2026-10-13-phase3-quick-annual-entry)
- What: `billMonth(bill, meterBills)` (computations/normalization.js) is the one answer to "which calendar month does this whole bill belong to". It is the private `_normMonthChain` over the bill's OWN meter: a continuous chain of bills takes the next month in turn, the first bill of a chain and the first bill after a gap take their majority-days month. A bill with one date, or start equal to end, is in the month of that date. A bill with no date returns an empty string and is left out. `buildMoMap` uses it for every field (kWh, Therms, kW, kW cost, rates, dollars), so one month never holds kWh from one bill and kW from another. The old end-month rule (`bill.end` cut to year-month) and the D-1 comment are gone.
- Readers on it: `buildMoMap` and the propane delivery month (computations/normalization.js), `_hvlMeterMoMap` and `_solarGetBaselineBills` (app/calculators.js), `getActualForMonth` (app/budget.js), `_wdBuildMeterBaseline` (the Client B report engine file, app/report-engine-*.js; the end-month helper is deleted), Appendix D rows (app/report-engine.js), the setpoint graphics (app/graphics-setpoints.js), the Scorecard last-12-months EUI (app/scorecard.js) and the meter data-quality month count (computations/data-quality.js).
- Effect on numbers (2026-10-12 backup): on three electric meters whose Jan 29 to Mar 2 bill ended in March, Feb is no longer empty with Mar doubled, so the setpoint report finds a 12-month electric baseline there. Gas and the electric meters with bills near the 5th or 15th move their baseline window by one month (the year of service Jan 15 to Jan 15 instead of Dec 15 to Dec 15), so baseline Therms and dollars fall. Savings dollars do not change (savings already used the chain month). Details: _context/research/2026-10-13-month-rule/2026-10-13-findings.md.
- Check: `node tools/gate-single-month-rule.js` (fails on any other month cut near a bill date, on any call to `normMonth(` (retired name), on any call to `_normMonthChain(` outside billMonth, or if `_wdAssignedYm` returns; a new allowed site needs a reason that is not "it is a bill month") and `node tools/test-month-rule-single.js` (25 checks: chain, one-date and no-date bills, buildMoMap fields from the same bills, Budget, raw-bill report rows, data-quality count, gate-fails-on-a-copy). Both run in the regression gate (the test by glob). Run time: on every page that shows a bill month (no timer, no network). Tests that load app code in a vm get the real rule from `tools/month-rule-src.js`.

## Automatic repair of stored bills from their PDF page (2026-10-15, branch 2026-10-15-auto-repair)

- What: a background pass re-reads the stored PDF page of each bill that has a flag or a misread, and of the bills next to it, and repairs the bill from the fresh read. Entry: `scheduleAutoRepair` (app/auto-repair.js), called at the end of the read-chain block of `loadUtilityData` (app/utility-data.js). It runs in the user's browser (no time zone, no server, no env var, no Netlify call). It waits 4 s after load, then `requestIdleCallback` (15 s limit), then works one PDF at a time with a 50 ms pause between PDFs. `window._autoRepairStats` holds the counts; the console line is `[auto repair] Re-read N bills from PDF: M repaired, K cannot be repaired, H values held {raw reasons}`; the toast is one plain line (`N bills re-read: M fixed, K need review`) shown when something was repaired. Audit notes use `reasonLabel()` (computations/bill-repair.js), the one place a reason becomes plain words; stored markers keep the raw reason. The fallback `showToast` (app/site-functions.js) sets `#toastMsg`, keeps the X button and ignores a stored `ch_toast_duration` of 0 or less (third argument `0` = stays until X). How to check: node test-onload-toast.js.
- Which bills (`repairEligibleBills`, computations/bill-repair.js): a bill with a pdfKey and no marker for the current `EXTRACTOR_RULES_VERSION` (app/energy-savings.js, next to `_pickFileRule`) that has a counted flag (`BILL_COUNTED_FLAG_RULES`), a read-chain flag (`read_continuity`, `read_rollback`, `read_usage_mismatch`), blank reads or reads that do not add up (electric), or is the bill before or after one of those. Bump `EXTRACTOR_RULES_VERSION` by 1 whenever a reader rule changes; every bill is then read again once. Marker: `row._pdfRepair = { v, at, status: 'repaired' | 'nochange' | 'declined', reason }` ends the bill for that version. Two markers carry no `v` and are tried again on a later load: `{ status: 'waiting', reason: 'the PDF is not on this browser' }` (once per load, until the PDF is restored) and `{ status: 'error', reason, tries }` (a read that threw; after `AUTO_REPAIR_MAX_TRIES` = 3 loads the bill is declined for the version with "N tries" in the reason). `dup: true` on a marker means the row is one of a duplicate group; alone it carries no version. The work list is built ONCE per run (one `repairEligibleBills` per meter), then only the meters a PDF touched are listed again. Meter objects are looked up by id again after every `await` (a reload or restore during a read replaces `utilityData`; the write goes to the live objects). Written after each PDF and saved with `saveUtilityData(customer)`, so a closed tab loses at most the PDF in progress and the next load continues. The pass runs only when `DB.isReady()` (the IndexedDB-backed store); otherwise it logs `the storage is not ready` and waits for the next load.
- Reading: page text comes from the page cache `bcr_pdftext_<pdfKey>` (`_bcrLoadPdfText`, app/bill-corrections-review.js; the Review Bill Corrections scan writes the same cache), so a page is read, with OCR if scanned, once. Extraction is `extractBillsFromText` (app/bill-analysis.js), the one pipeline the upload queue also calls (`_extractSingleFileForQueue` -> `extractBillsFromPdfBytes` -> `extractBillsFromText`). OCR time is about 12 s per scanned page; cached text runs under 1 s per PDF. The page cache is written by `_bcrLoadPdfText` through `sset` (IndexedDB when `DB.isReady()`, which the pass requires); app/auto-repair.js writes no storage itself. A PDF that is not on this browser marks its bills `waiting` (no version; read on a later load).
- Decisions (computations/bill-repair.js, pure, no DOM): `findRepairMatch` (page, commodity and account; when both the page and the meter carry a meter number they must agree, for one candidate as for many: a page of another meter number is declined `the PDF page holds another meter number`; a page with two candidates must be picked by meter number, then start date; a page the reader holds for review is declined), `repairFieldsFromExtracted` (uses `_extractedToBillRowCosts` and `gasBillSaveTherms`; electric: dates, reads, read difference, kWh; other commodities: dates, total, usage, charge; every candidate goes through the ONE write decision `repairMayWrite`, Matt 2026-10-15: never fill a value that is not printed, held is better than guessed. A stored value is replaced only when (1) the bill itself contradicts it: a check of the bill's own values fails (`repairFailedChecks`: end - start vs difference and difference x multiplier vs kWh through `computePrintedReadMismatch`; charge parts vs total, a missing or negative usage, a missing total, a missing date or a bad period length through the one flag engine; the statistical rules such as a charge against the meter's usual rate or a neighbor's dates are NOT checks here), (2) the fresh value passes every check that judges the field, alone or as a pair with the other value the same check judges (a read difference and its kWh, a charge and its total), and (3) the fresh value is printed on the bill's own page (`repairValueProvenance`: no reader record `_auto_recovered_<F>` or `_auto_corrected_<F>` on the source field, and its digits in the page text of the bill's own pages, `repairPageText` over the stored page range, else the reader's, else the whole PDF when no page is known; OCR forms: a thousands comma read as a dot, a date with a dropped dash). A blank is filled only with such a printed value; a 0 is never proven by the page. Anything else is held: the stored value stays, the reason is in `res.held`, the marker reason and the audit log `pdf_reread_held`), `planMeterRepair` (each repair runs on a copy of the meter and is kept only when no bill of the meter gains a counted flag, or an electric read-chain flag; failed repairs are tried again while the meter improves), `applyRepairChanges`. Never changed: a field the user owns (`billFieldUserOwned`, computations/bill-flags.js: `row._userCorrected[field]`, `row._userCorrected_<Field>` in any spelling or `_chg_` part, or a value retyped over an automatic record; the read-chain rule uses the same test), a value to blank, a stored value to 0 (`repairValueAllowed`, applied in the field builder, the trial and `applyRepairChanges`), a value the bill does not contradict, a value that is not printed on the bill's own page (a read copied from a neighbor page, a computed read, a corrected charge, a converted usage, a 0). Missing bill: a PDF page with no row on the meter, same commodity and account, the same meter number on BOTH sides, a period not within 5 days of a stored one, not held by the reader, is written to the audit log (`pdf_bill_missing`, with page, dates and total) and NOT added: a deleted bill exists only in the audit log, so an added row could bring back a bill the user removed. A PDF whose rows include one without a page number reports nothing. The same PDF page stored in two rows with the same total is written to the audit log (`pdf_duplicate_rows`); nothing is deleted.
- History: ONE store. Each change is written to the existing automatic-correction record `row._auto_corrected_<field>` = { original, corrected, reason: "Re-read from the PDF, page N", source: `PDF_REREAD_SOURCE` ('pdf_reread'), at, page, ruleVersion } (the read-chain rule writes the same record for its reads, marked by reason). `_readCorrectionRecords` / `_billChangeHistory` (app/utility-data.js) show every such record in Edit Billing Period while the cell still holds the value; `clearReadCorrectionRecords` (every edit path) deletes the re-read record of an edited field. Audit log (`logUtilityAudit`, key `en_utility_audit_log`): `pdf_reread`, `pdf_reread_held` (a fresh value that differs from the stored one and was not written: field, stored, read, reason), `pdf_bill_missing`, `pdf_duplicate_rows`, source `auto-repair`. The console line and toast count the held values (`stats.held`, `held: <reason>` in the reasons). The pass does NOT run `correctMeterReadChain` (its reads come from the neighbor bills, not the bill's own page); that rule runs only from `loadUtilityData`.
- Check: `node tools/gate-auto-repair.js` (about 6 s; in the regression gate static list): idempotent, user-corrected fields, never blank, printed values, no flag gained, duplicates kept (no version on an unread duplicate), one history store, one copy of each rule and one accessor, another meter's page never repairs, missing page reported not added, 0 never over a value, every user-edit marker, the write guard in `applyRepairChanges` (with a mutation run), the browser pass with stubs (write after a replaced `utilityData`, missing PDF retried, thrown read capped, second pass reads nothing, storage not ready, a held value in the audit log, a read the chain derives from the neighbor bills is not written and the pass does not call the read-chain rule), no per-PDF sweep of every meter, and check 17, the one write decision: a stored value the bill does not contradict stays when the re-read differs (noise), a contradicted value is replaced by the page value (alone or as a pair), a value only on a neighbor page, a reader-filled or reader-changed value, a 0 and a converted value are never written, every held value carries its reason, one decision function on one write path, and a mutation run without the page test must fail. The run on a real backup is a local check (not in the gate). To check by eye: open Edit Billing Period of a repaired bill and read "Change history".

## Service address on one line, every Water Protection Fee line, joined-address heal (2026-10-22, branch 2026-10-22-bill-read-fixes)

- Utility E service address: `_EVG_ADDR` (app/energy-savings.js, above `_evgAccountsIn`) now matches ONE printed line (`[^\S\n]`, not `\s`). Before, a street number alone on a line plus the next line ("11" then a district name) joined into a fake address and split one bill into two rows in the read.
- City old-format reader: every `WATER PROTECTION FEE` line is summed (one per meter) through `_lbg_mergeMetered` (app/energy-savings.js, above `_lbg_cleanCents`), the same function the new-format reader and the water lines use.
- Heal of stored bills: `EXTRACTOR_RULES_VERSION` is 5. `repairEligibleBills` (computations/bill-repair.js) also lists a bill whose stored `serviceAddress` is joined (`billServiceAddressJoined`). `repairServiceAddressCandidate` writes the fresh clean address only when it stands on the bill's own page and the user does not own the field; else it is held and logged. It needs the stored PDF page text in this browser (the repair pass runs after load, in the browser, no server). A bill whose PDF is not in the browser keeps the old address until the PDF is there.
- Check: `node tools/test-bill-read-address-and-multi-fee.js` (synthetic text).

## No save without a user action (2026-11-03, branch fix/2026-11-03-no-auto-save)

- Rule: a page saves to the shared store only after a person made a change. Every save carries a cause.
- Cause text: `<page>: <action>` for a person (for example `Projects: Save`), `auto: <what>` for a write the code makes itself (`auto: merge of first sync`, `auto: first upload of local data`, `auto: first sign-in guide shown`), `Sync conflict: ...`, `Restore from backup`, `script: migrate-seed`. 
- Where (app/db.js): `_installActionTracker` listens for trusted click, key, input, change, paste, drop and submit events (tab and menu clicks are ignored: `NAV_SELECTOR`, app/db.js; it must NOT match `.ptab`, which is the tab CONTENT panel, or every button in a project tab (Clear All Data, Delete All, Save Edits) counts as navigation and its save stays local; the tab buttons are `.pdt`; check: tools/test-matrix-sync-write-and-view.js; tools/test-matrix-sync-put-bodies.js asserts the PUT bodies in sync mode). A render must never save: with the cause gate open, the Savings tab (app/energy-savings.js `_renderSavingsContent`), Hours (`_getOrInitHoursData`) and Budget (`_getOrInitBudgetData`) keep their defaults in memory and save only from an edit. In app/db.js `_stampOrDropDelete` (one rule, called by `_writeOne` and the queue drain `_drainQueueLocked`), a delete of a key this browser has no stamp for asks the server manifest (one fetch shared for 5 s, so one Clear All asks once; the cache entry belongs to the identity that asked, so a user change ignores it. A row another user creates inside that 5 s window is not seen, so a delete of that key in the window is dropped and the next remote check brings the key back): no row (or already deleted) drops the delete and sends nothing, a row made by another user stamps its version and the delete goes out with it; no answer, an answer that is not a list, or a user change while it waits keeps the delete queued and the drain retries it (never a failed entry); the drain stops its cycle at the first such wait. Check: tools/test-matrix-sync-put-bodies.js (offline Clear All and a manifest timeout, then reconnect). The last such event, for 2 minutes (15 minutes for a file), names the cause (`_currentCause`). `_replicateWrite` gives a write with no cause and no `auto:` name to NO ONE: it stays in this browser only (not queued, not sent), counted in `getAutoLocalCount()`. Tests and code can name an action with `DB.noteUserAction(text)`, or pass a cause as the third argument of `DB.set(key, value, cause)`. When there is no DOM (Node tests) the gate is off.
- No false conflict: a copy that was synced before and has no unsaved user edit gets the server copy silently on load (`_reconcileIncoming`, `syncedBefore`): no archive, no toast, no write. `_hasPendingWrite` counts a queued write or a send now running for the CURRENT sign-in (`_writeBusy[key]` holds the identity epoch + 1). A user edit not yet confirmed is marked in `_userEdited`; it is archived when the server copy replaces it. A copy never synced is archived with `neverSynced: true` (a real set-aside copy).
- Old entries clear themselves: `_purgeFalseConflicts` (app/db.js, runs after each full pull) removes old `hydration-server-wins` archive entries that lack `neverSynced`, for the signed-in user, when that user has no waiting write. The viewer (app/sync-ui.js) is named "Past conflicts" and has no Remove buttons.
- Server: the page sends a `cause` field with each save. kv-sync records it (see the next section, v210). Sync is on only on the stable host and localhost:8888 (app/ch-auth.js `_isNetlifyHost`); the GitHub Pages site never calls kv-sync.
- Check: `node test-no-auto-save.js` (10 tests), `node test-kv-sync-history.js`, `node tools/test-backend-mode-default.js`. In the browser: Network tab, filter `kv-sync`, open a page and leave it: zero PUT. Make one edit: one PUT whose body has `cause`. 

## Cause of every save, stored in updated_by (2026-11-03, branch feat/2026-11-03-save-cause)

- What: kv-sync.js `handlePut` stores the writer as `<account> · <cause>` in the existing `kv.updated_by` column (separator is a middle dot with spaces, `WRITER_SEP`). The cause comes from the `cause` field of the PUT body (the page sends it since the branch fix/2026-11-03-no-auto-save). A missing or non-text cause is stored as `unknown (old page)`. The save never fails for a missing cause. The cause is cut at 160 characters.
- No database change: no new column, no SQL to run. `kv_history.updated_by` gets the same text, because `snapshotHistory` already copies `updated_by` of the replaced row.
- Readers of updated_by: GET and the 409 body (`rowToCurrent`, `rowToConflict` paths, `splitWriter`) return the plain account in `updatedBy` and the cause in a new field `updatedCause`, so app/sync-ui.js (the "who changed it" text) and app/db.js (conflict archive `winningUpdatedBy`) still see an account only. scripts/migrate-seed.js writes its own operator name (no cause) and copies `updated_by` to history unchanged. No code compares `updated_by` with the signed-in account.
- Where it goes live: this is a function change. It goes live ONLY with a Netlify deploy, or on localhost:8888 when `netlify dev` reloads the function from this folder. Sync runs only on the stable host and localhost:8888 (app/ch-auth.js `_isNetlifyHost`). Until then the old function ignores `cause`.
- Check: `node test-kv-sync-history.js` (cause stored, history copy, missing cause saves, GET and 409 return the plain account). In Supabase: `select key, updated_by from kv order by updated_at desc limit 10;` shows `account · cause`.

## Calendar feed on the Project page (2026-10-27, branch feat/2026-10-27-project-calendar)

- What: the Calendar feed panel at the top of the District Calendar tab (app/project-calendar.js). It shows the saved copy of the feed and what changed since the last sync.
- Data: three shared keys per project, `en_cal_cfg_<pid>` (feed address and time zone), `en_cal_snap_<pid>` (last copy, about 1 MB), `en_cal_hist_<pid>` (sync history, newest first, 50 records, merged by id in app/db.js `MERGE_ONLY_CONFIG.calHist`). Class SYNCED in app/sync-classification.js. The feed address lives only in server data. It is not in code, tests or commits.
- When it runs (changed 2026-11-03): only when the user clicks Sync now (or Save and sync the first time). Opening the District Calendar tab now only READS the feed (`pcalAutoSync` in app/project-calendar.js calls `readFeed`, one calendar-fetch call when the saved copy is older than 1 hour, `CalendarDiff.needsAutoSync`). It saves nothing. If the feed changed, the panel says "feed has changes" with a Sync now button. One calendar-fetch call plus two kv-sync PUTs (history first, then copy) per Sync now. A failed read shows the fixed error line in the panel, no dialog. Opening the page (not the tab) makes no call. No timers, no polling.
- Marks (2026-10-29): `CalendarDiff.carriedMarks` builds the New / Changed / Canceled marks from the kept history (`en_cal_hist`), not only the newest record. A mark stays until the event date has passed or 7 days after the sync that first showed it, whichever is first. A sync that finds nothing new does not clear marks. Check: `node tools/test-calendar-auto-sync.js`.
- One comparison: `CalendarDiff.diffSnapshots` in app/calendar-diff.js. A change is a different title, start, end, all-day flag or location. Description, sequence and last-modified alone are ignored. Added and removed count only events whose start is inside the overlap of the old and new sync windows (each snapshot stores its window), so events at the 365-back/730-forward edge never show as false added or removed.
- Limit to know: the reader stops with an error when one repeating event has more than 1,000 occurrences in the window (for example a daily event). The panel then shows its fixed error line and keeps the last saved copy.
- What the tab shows (2026-10-29): `CalendarDiff.listView` (app/calendar-diff.js) is the one function that picks the events of a view (next 14 days from today in the calendar time zone, a month, or before today) and gives each its mark from the newest history record: New, Changed (with old and new time, place or title) or Canceled (removed events are kept in the record with their full row so they show struck through). It makes no call and runs only on tab render. Check: `node tools/test-calendar-upcoming.js`.
- Check it works: `node tools/test-calendar-feed.js` (all must pass). In the browser, sync twice with no feed edit: the second sync must show 0 added, 0 removed, 0 changed. On localhost:8888 the Network list must show one calendar-fetch call per click, one on tab open when the copy is older than 1 hour, and none on page open.

## BAS schedules on the District Calendar tab (2026-10-29, branch feat/2026-10-29-bas-schedule-import)

- What: the BAS schedules card (app/bas-schedules.js, container `bas-panel-<pid>`, drawn by `basRender` from the District Calendar tab in app/core.js) keeps a dated copy of the WebCTRL Effective Schedules and the scheduling groups, and lists what changed and who imported.
- Data: three shared keys per project under the existing `en_bas_` class (app/sync-classification.js). `en_bas_snap_<pid>` is one object keyed by the date the export covers (60 dates kept, oldest dropped; about 55 KB per date for 437 programs). `en_bas_hist_<pid>` is the import history, newest first, 50 records, merged by id in app/db.js (same config as `en_cal_hist_`). `en_bas_groups_<pid>` holds the groups (schedules and members).
- When it runs: only when the user imports a file. The CSV is read in the browser (`emParseEffectiveSchedulesCSV`, `_emScheduleBlocks` in app/equipment-matrix.js, the one reader of Occupied/Unoccupied blocks). The export has no date, so the dialog requires "Date this export covers". No Netlify Function, no network call, no timer, no env var. Opening the page or tab only reads saved data.
- One comparison: `BasDiff.applyScheduleImport` (app/bas-diff.js) picks the earlier copy (the same date, else the latest earlier day of the same type: Mon-Fri, Sat or Sun, else the latest other date), compares only the buildings in the import, merges them into that date's copy and builds the history record. Groups: `BasDiff.applyGroupsImport`. Who: the signed-in name (`currentUser.name`).
- Check it works: `node tools/test-bas-diff.js` (synthetic data; includes a page-text test with quotes and script tags in names). In the browser import the same files twice for one date: the second history line must say 0 changed.

## Space map and the BAS column of the events list (2026-10-30, branch feat/2026-10-30-calendar-p2)

- What: the Space map card (app/space-map.js, container `space-panel-<pid>`, drawn by `spaceMapRender` at the end of the calendar panel render in app/project-calendar.js) says which BAS program(s) serve each calendar space, or Off-site (no BAS action). The events list has a BAS column: Covered, Not covered (minutes short), No BAS schedule for this space, Off-site, Not mapped, No BAS copy for this weekday.
- Data: one shared key per project, `en_space_map_<pid>` = `{ spaces:{ "<normalized space>": { space, programs:["location|program"], offsite, confirmed, by, at } } }`. Class SYNCED (app/sync-classification.js, prefix `en_space_map_`); whole-key last write, like `en_bas_groups_`. Restore groups it under District calendar (app/restore-merge.js).
- One function: `BasCoverage.eventCoverage` (computations/bas-coverage.js) decides each cell: the event space (`CalendarDiff.eventPlace`) -> confirmed map entry -> programs in that date's copy of `en_bas_snap_<pid>` (the copy for the date, else the newest earlier copy of the same weekday, else the nearest later one; none = No BAS copy) -> minutes of the event (calendar time zone) not inside an occupied block. Several programs: the least covered one counts. An event with no end time is checked for 1 hour; an event that runs past midnight is checked to midnight; all-day events show no cell. Serving units (`BasCoverage.isServingProgram`: HW, CHW, boiler, chiller, pump, plant, ERV, DOAS, and an AHU or RTU named only by its tag) are never suggested and never checked.
- When it runs: on every draw of the District Calendar tab, from saved data only. No network call, no timer, no Netlify change. Name suggestions (`BasCoverage.suggestPrograms`) are computed on draw and saved only when the user chooses Confirm. "Import suggestions" reads a CSV in the browser (columns calendar_space, bas_location, bas_program, confirmed; `emParseCSVText`); a space the user already confirmed is kept. The file is never part of the repo.
- Check it works: `node tools/test-bas-coverage.js` (synthetic data). In the browser: confirm a space, then the BAS column of its upcoming events changes from Not mapped to Covered or Not covered.

## What needs to change list (2026-10-30, branch feat/2026-10-30-calendar-p3)

- What: the "What needs to change" card at the top of the District Calendar tab (app/sched-actions.js, container `sact-panel-<pid>`, drawn by `sactRender` from the calendar panel render in app/project-calendar.js). One line per Not covered upcoming event, newest event first, with Done / Open again. A second short table lists BAS programs occupied all day on a saved BAS day while no event of that building is on the calendar. Serving units never make an action or a waste line.
- Data: shared key `en_sched_actions_<pid>` = `{ actions:{ "<event id>": { id, date, text, program, status:'open'|'done'|'closed', openedAt, doneBy, doneAt, closedAt, note } } }`. Class SYNCED (app/sync-classification.js, app/sync-ui.js, app/restore-merge.js). Each save that changes an action also adds one record `kind:'action'` (who, when, what) to `en_bas_acthist_<pid>` (its own 50-record list, merged by id, so actions never push out the BAS import history `en_bas_hist_<pid>`); the BAS schedules history shows both lists, newest first.
- One function: `SchedActions.reconcile` (computations/sched-actions.js) turns the calendar copy, the space map, the BAS copies and the saved actions into the new saved actions. It calls `BasCoverage.eventCoverage` for every check. A Done action whose event is still Not covered after a newer BAS import or calendar sync (`SchedActions.latestImport`) reopens with a note. An action whose event is now Covered closes with "Fixed in BAS on <date>". `SchedActions.wasteFindings` finds the all-day programs.
- When it runs: the list is worked out in memory on every draw of the tab (saved data only, no network call, no timer). It is saved (`sactPersist`) after a calendar sync (project-calendar.js `sync`), after a BAS schedule import (bas-schedules.js `basSave`), and when a person chooses Done or Open again (`sactSet`). No Netlify Function change.
- Check it works: `node tools/test-sched-actions.js` and `node tools/test-bas-coverage.js` (synthetic data). In the browser: an event shown Not covered in the BAS column has a line at the top; choose Done, then import a newer BAS copy that still lacks the time and the line opens again.

## Report period and coverage: getReportCoverage (2026-10-27, branch fix/2026-10-27-report-coverage)

- What: `getReportCoverage(projId, meterIds, reqStartYm, reqEndYm, buildingIds)` (computations/report-data.js) is the ONE rule for which
  months a report covers. The period ends at the last month with a real bill for an included meter. A zero-filled propane
  month (normalization.js `zeroFill`) and the report-date month never extend it. `buildingIds` and `meterIds` are the same selection `collectReportData` reports on. It also returns the latest bill dates
  inside the period, clamped to the last day of the period end (`lastBillDate`, `electricThrough`, `gasThrough`) and completeness (`complete`, `missing`, `waitingText`): a period is
  complete only when every included monthly meter (not propane) has a bill for every month of the request.
  `reportRequestRange(selectedPeriod)` turns a quarter or a custom range into the months asked for.
- Callers: `collectReportData` (app/report-engine.js) sets `data.period.coverage`, drops months after the end and sets `data.period.months` to the month count of the period (no fixed 3 or 12; a cumulative report keeps every month of the coverage); the Generate Report dialog lines "Bills through ..." and "Not ready to present: waiting for ..." (`_rptV2RefreshCoverageWarning`, app side only);
  the file name tag `_rptFilenameWaitingTag` (" - waiting for bills"). Completeness and the through dates change no printed number and print nothing in the client PDF (no "Bills through" line on the cover). A quarter with no bills has no months and no actual bars.
- Charts: a month after the period end has `cur: null` (no actual bar) in `buildFullYear` / `filterThroughCurrentMonth`.
- When it runs: on report build and on each dialog change (browser, local time). Needs: nothing outside the page (no env var).
- Check: `node tools/test-report-coverage.js` (synthetic fixture); open Generate Report for a project with a missing month and read the line.

## Facility Audit tab (2026-10-28, branch feat/2026-10-28-facility-audit)
- New sync key `en_audit_<pid>` (prefix rule `en_audit_` in app/sync-classification.js). Value: `{findings, edits, importedAt}`. `findings` holds records the user added or imported. `edits` holds the user's changes to automatic findings, keyed `auto:<rowId>::<ruleId>`. Set points are never stored in it. No new Function, no schema change, no timer, no env var.
- Automatic findings are recomputed on every read by computations/audit-rules.js (`auditComputeAutoFindings`; rules `setpoint-off-standard`, `sensor-deficiency`, `schedule-extra-hours`). They read the Equipment Matrix functions, they do not copy them. A serving unit (AHU, RTU, ERV or DOAS in a building with boxes or zones, and hot water and chilled water plants) never gets a schedule finding.
- Import of the audit workbook: computations/audit-import.js `auditImportSheets`, called from app/facility-audit.js `faImportFile` when the user picks a file. Rows that match no equipment stay as free text and show in the import review list.
- Check: `node tools/test-audit-rules.js` and `node tools/test-audit-import.js`.

- Coverage view (2026-10-30, default view of the tab): computations/audit-coverage.js `auditComputeCoverage` is the ONE coverage function; `_faCoverageView` in app/facility-audit.js only renders it. It runs on every render of the tab, in the browser, no timer, no env var, nothing stored. It groups equipment by system type plus identical BAS point names (Equipment Matrix), marks a group covered when one member has the data it needs, and lists only real gaps in "Still need". Serving units need no schedule; plumbing (domestic hot water) is out of scope; lighting is never a gap. Check: `node tools/test-audit-coverage.js`.

## Report drafts: one contract total, shortfall line (2026-10-31, branch feat/2026-10-31-lou-report-drafts)

DRAFT for Matt's review; not released. (1) `computeContractProjection` (computations/csc.js) is the one contract-total projection: the current contract year = billed savings so far (`d.contract.quarterlyActuals`, each quarter floored at $0) + the targets still to come; other years = the escalated target (`computeMultiYearCscTotals`). Each row is rounded and each total is the sum of the rows. `_rptContractProjection(d)` (app/report-engine.js) calls it; the Financial Summary "3-Year Total" and "Annualized" and the Contract Projection table, total and chart all use that one result. An annual report now also carries `quarterlyActuals` and `actualsThroughQuarter` (set in `collectReportData` from `billableSavingsWithPresented` per quarter). A presented report (`d.printed`) with no printed total keeps the old all-target total. (2) `savingsNotBilledWithPresented(projId, yms, perBldg)` (computations/savings.js) returns `{shortfall, presented}`: the gap between real rows and the billable total. `collectReportData` stores `totals.shortfallNotBilled`. The Financial building table and the Year-to-Date table print one line "Shortfall not billed (contract minimum $0)"; the Year-to-Date table also prints "Presented quarters (figures locked as presented)" when a presented quarter differs from the current month rows. (3) The cover chart (`rptPageBoardSummary`) charts the floored value of each quarter to date, not months. (4) The annual Contract Projection comparison box now compares the year to date with the annual target. Runs on report render only; no schedule, no env var. Check: `node tools/test-contract-projection-shared.js` and `node tools/test-savings-floor-contract-rates.js`.

## Baseline: the contract method (2026-11-03)

Matt (2026-11-03): the contract method may not change. The 2026-10-31 change (baseline at the weather of the reporting month) is removed. No timer, env var or host change.
- `expectedUsageForMonth(row, ctx)` (computations/savings.js) is the ONE rule for the baseline usage of a meter-month: the baseline-year model value for that calendar month at the baseline year's weather, else the regression, else the baseline average. This holds for every month, for kWh, therms and gallons. kW: the kW-vs-CDD fit at the reporting month's CDD (`computeKwCddRegression`, computations/regression.js). `baselineUsageByCalMo` reads the baseline-year map. Callers: `getMeterSavings`, `buildReportData` in app/report-engine.js (takes `expUsage`, `blExpKW` from the `getMeterSavings` rows, no local copy), the Meter Performance stats pills (app/utility-data.js).
- Missing weather is never read as 0: `regressionBaseline` returns null and `computeKwCddRegression` gives no prediction for a month without degree days. For an Electric meter with a kW fit, a month without CDD is `weatherMissing` in `getMeterSavings` (`incompleteYM`, reason "weather not yet available", $0, left out of usage totals). Gas, propane and water months never wait for the reporting weather, because the contract baseline for them does not use it.
- Report gate: `reportWeatherGaps` and `reportMissingInputs` (computations/report-data.js) test the weather cache directly before a quarterly or annual report is built. Needed: the CDD of each reporting month with an Electric bill (kW baseline) and the HDD and CDD of each baseline month. Missing: no report and an inline message in the Generate Report dialog. Months inside a presented quarter are not tested as reporting months. Check: `node tools/test-report-blocks-on-missing-input.js`.
- Report Electric Demand $ is the sum of `getMeterSavings` `kwCostSav` (`electric.kwCostSaved`); Electric Energy $ is the electric total less that.
- Report EUI: baseline and current use the same meter-months (months with a bill and weather), all fuels, annualized once per building (x12/n). `euiPeriodLabel` is printed on the cover gauge and the EUI page.
- Quarter with a month that has no CDD for a kW fit (`quarterWeatherMissing`, `billableSavingsWithPresented`, `savingsOrWaiting`): it has NO savings figure: left out of `total` (not floored, not billed) and of `byBldg`, named in `weatherWaiting`. Reports and the Dashboard print `Waiting for weather data: <month>`. A presented quarter is never waiting.
- Tests: `node tools/test-expected-usage.js`, `node tools/test-savings-floor-contract-rates.js`. `SAVINGS_CALC_VERSION` is `2026.11.03.contract1`.
- Known, not changed: the expected usage uses calendar days of the month, the actual usage is the bill period. About one day net. Pre-existing.

## Savings Methods Comparison, Project > Performance (2026-11-03, branch feat/2026-11-03-normalized-savings-method)

No timer, env var or host change. Comparison only: no report, export, portal, print or billing code calls it. The contract method governs all billed savings and is unchanged.
- Where: Project > Performance, section "Savings Methods Comparison", below the "Monthly Savings: Projected vs Current" chart. The `perf` branch of `renderUDProjAggPanel` (app/utility-data.js) calls `savingsMethodsHtml(udSelProjId)` (app/savings-methods.js) once. No button, no toggle.
- Shows per year (years come from the bills after the baseline, never fixed), per quarter: "Contract method (billed)" before the $0 floor, "Billed" (the floored or printed figure), and "Bills normalized for weather and dates (comparison)". Year total row. Hover on a figure lists the months, the energy and kW parts, and the formula.
- Contract column: `getMeterSavings` rows, unchanged (baseline-year value for the calendar month; kW fit at the reporting CDD). Billed column: `billableSavingsWithPresented` fed with `getBuildingSavingsByYM`, the same call the pages use.
- Normalized column: `getMeterSavings(..., { normalizedBills: true })` (computations/savings.js; the option is off for every billed caller and bypasses the savings cache). The baseline, the rate and the months are the contract's. Only the bill changes: `normalizedBillsAtBaselineWeather` fits the post-baseline rows (`getNormRows`, each scaled to its calendar month: usage x normDays / days) with `computeMeterRegression` and reads that fit with `regressionBaseline` at the baseline weather of the month (mean of the baseline months of that calendar month). kW is not normalized (the contract kW term is used in both columns). A month with no fit keeps its real bill. Savings = (baseline - normalized bill) x the month's rate, summed (`getMeterSavingsComparison`, `getProjectSavingsComparison`).
- Check: `node test-savings-methods-internal.js` (hand-computed fit, scan that proves no other file reaches the section). In the app, open Project > Performance on a contracted project; the Contract column must equal the project's quarter savings before the floor.

## Quarterly report review fixes (2026-11-02, branch fix/2026-11-02-q3-report-review-findings)

No timer, env var or host change. Runs on report render only.
- Billed to date: `_rptBilledToDate(contract, q)` (app/report-engine.js) is the one sum of `contract.quarterlyActuals` through quarter q. The cover, the compensation table, the contract table and the projection chart call it. `totals.cumulativeSavings` is no longer printed on the quarterly pages.
- Rounding: `_rptApportion(values, total)` gives whole numbers that add to `Math.round(total)` (largest fraction first). `_rptBuildingTarget` uses it for the building targets; `_rptQFacts` uses it for each building's month cells (`f.shown`), and a month total is the sum of those cells.
- Baseline cost: `collectReportData` sets each building `blCost` = amount billed in the period (the `rawBills` amounts, the same ones the bills table prints) + savings; `totals.blCost` is their sum. Every percent (`savingsPct`, the page 2 total percent and its note) reads it. `targetWeight` is the old baseline-year cost, kept only as the share basis for the building savings targets.
- EUI period: `reportEndYM` in `collectReportData` is the quarter end of a quarterly report (else the last report month); EUI months after it are not used, so the label ends at the report quarter end.
- Projection note: page 5 prints the billed-to-date and remaining-target amounts behind Year 1.
- PDF text layer: the app print (window.print, Save as PDF) keeps every digit. A PDF run through the Bluebeam printer driver (Producer "Bluebeam Brewery") lost digit "1" in the text layer; that is the driver, not the app.
- Check: `node tools/test-report-q3-review-findings.js` and `node tools/test-report-pdf-text-digits.js` (headless Chromium + pdftotext).

## Download PDF button on reports (2026-11-03, branch feat/2026-11-03-report-pdf-download)

No timer, env var, host or server change. Runs in the browser on click. No network call except the one-time load of the two libraries below.
- Buttons: "Download Word" and "Download PDF" in the report toolbar (`#rptPreviewToolbar` for Quarterly/Annual/Cumulative/Custom, `#reportOverlay` toolbar for every other report). They call `exportReportToDocx()` (app/report-engine.js) and `downloadReportPdf()` (app/report-pdf.js). There is no Print button: no report opens a print dialog. The old print function `exportReportToPDF()` was removed.
- Method: for each report page (`.rpt-page`, or `.soo-doc-page` for Sequence of Operations) html2canvas 1.4.1 draws the page at 1.25x (120 dpi), jsPDF 2.5.2 puts it on an 8.5 x 11 in page as JPEG (quality 0.6), then adds the words of the page as invisible text at the word positions. Look is the screen page; text can be selected, copied and searched. Text is a picture, not vector. Both libraries were already loaded in energy-department.html (cdn.jsdelivr.net, pinned, SRI).
- Pages: `_rptReportExportPages(data)` and `_rptForceOpenTierPanels(root)` (report-engine.js) give the pages for both Print and Download. A page taller than one sheet is cut between lines of text (`_rptPdfPageCuts`).
- File name: `rptReportFileName(data, ext)` (lib/report-file-name.js) is the one rule for print title, .pdf, .doc and .docx: `<client or project> - <report name> <period or local date><ext>`. Cumulative, custom and current reports now name their first and last month, not "Annual".
- Cost: a 57-page report is about 6 MB and takes about 20 s; an 8-page report about 1 MB and 3 s (headless Chromium, 2026-11-03). html2canvas copies the document on every call, so `ignoreElements` skips every element that is not the page or a parent of it (this cut the time ten times). The page yields to the browser between pages and each canvas is released after use. A toast shows "Building PDF: page n of N".
- Check: `node tools/test-report-file-name.js`. Then open a report, click Download PDF: a .pdf file saves with no print dialog; page count equals the Print page count.
- Word (2026-11-03, branch feat/2026-11-03-report-word-download): `exportReportToDocx()` reads the same pages as the PDF (`_rptReportExportPages`, `_rptForceOpenTierPanels`), so it works from the v2 preview as well as the overlay; hidden pages are left out. It writes the .docx with docx-writer.js (JSZip, already loaded) under `rptReportFileName(data, '.docx')`.
- Report History: each entry has "Download Word" and "Download PDF" (`reexportReport(id, 'word'|'pdf')`). It opens the saved pages in the overlay and downloads the file. The data object is built from the entry, not from the report opened last.
- Utility Data > Utility Audit: "Download Word" and "Download PDF" call `downloadUtilityAudit(projId, kind)` (app/report-engine.js). `printUtilityAudit` sets `_currentReportData = {project, _utilityAudit:true}`; the file name is `<client> - Utility Audit <date>`.
- Check: `node tools/test-report-download-buttons.js` (clicks both buttons on a synthetic report; asserts a .docx and a .pdf download and no print dialog).
- Other documents (2026-11-03, branch feat/2026-11-03-doc-exports): same two buttons, no print dialog, no network. Each runs in the browser on click.
  - Meeting agenda and minutes: modal and list-row buttons call `meetingFileFromEditor(kind)` / `meetingFileById(projId, meetingId, kind)` (app/meeting-export.js). Word: the meeting becomes HTML, then `_docxTranslatePages` and `_docxAssemble` (app/docx-writer.js) write it on the CSC letterhead. PDF: `buildMeetingPDF` (app/csv-import.js, also the live preview). Both names come from `meetingFileName(m, ext)` (lib/report-file-name.js); the date line parts come from `meetingDateParts(m)`. Meeting Report History rows: `reexportReport(id, 'word'|'pdf')`.
  - Building Scorecard (Utility Data, building Scorecard tab): `scorecardDownload(btn, kind)` (app/scorecard.js) sets `_currentReportData = {project:{client}, _scorecard:{rootId}}` and calls `exportReportToDocx()` / `downloadReportPdf()`. `_rptReportExportPages` returns the `.sc-root` element for `_scorecard`. The `.sc-export` class (energy-department.html) gives a white page 816 px wide; light text colours are set to black for the build and put back. The gauge rings and the trend chart go into the .docx as pictures (`_rptRasterizeGaugeRingsForDocx`, canvas copy in `exportReportToDocx`). File name: `<building> - Building Scorecard <date>`.
  - HVAC Load Estimate: `hvacLoadExportWord` (app/calculators.js) now writes a .docx through the same docx path (was a .doc file). PDF stays `hvacLoadExportPdf` (a picture of the page, no text layer). Names: `hvacFileName(building, ext)`.
  - Deleted: `exportReportToWord` (.doc, mso HTML) and `_buildReportCssVarResolver`; they had no caller.
  - Check: `node tools/test-report-download-buttons.js` also covers meeting, scorecard and HVAC; `node tools/test-report-file-name.js` covers the names.

## Q3 2026 quarterly report in the Q1 and Q2 layout (2026-11-06, branch 2026-11-06-q3-expert-fixes)

No timer, env var, host or server change. Runs in the browser when a report renders or downloads.
- Layout: an unpresented quarter (`_rptUnpresentedQuarter(d)`, app/report-engine.js:2151: quarterly and no `d.printed`) renders through `_rptQuarterReportHTML` (:4165) with the same page list as the presented Q1 and Q2: cover, financial, savings performance, environmental impact, observations, operating hours and set points, meter performance with one Building Baseline Data page per building, appendix A and B. Each section starts a new page. Presented Q1 and Q2 keep their own code path.
- Quarter EUI: `computeQuarterlyEUI(qKBtu, sqft)` (computations/eui.js:91) is the quarter kBtu divided by square feet, not annualized. `collectReportData` sets `euiQ` per building and `euiBaselineQ` and `euiCurrentQ` in the totals. Every building page, the hero ring and the usage tables read them; the unit text is "kBtu/ft2 (Q3 2026)". kBtu comes from `computeKBtu` (computations/eui.js). Hand check: kWh x 3.412 + therms x 100 + propane gal x 91.452, divided by square feet.
- Meter name: `_rptMeterLabel(commodity, account)` (:2330) returns "Electricity-Acct#123", "Natural Gas-Acct#123" or "Propane-Acct#123". Every report place that names a meter calls it.
- Hours page: `_rptQHoursRows(d)` (:2184) reads `en_bas_schedhistory_<pid>`; an entry with no end date runs to the quarter end. The page is left out when the project has no record.
- Baseline Data page: `rptBuildSiteBaselineData(b, d)` (:4253) takes the table of the site (`_buildBaselineDataHtml`, app/calculators.js) and restyles it for the report; there is no second builder.
- Baseline Data page: `rptBuildSiteBaselineData(b, d)` (app/report-engine.js) reads the table of the site (`_buildBaselineDataHtml`, app/calculators.js) and cuts it into two standard report tables at 9.5px: Electricity, then Natural Gas / Propane with Total Utility. Columns and cells are the site's own; each has 12 month rows and a bold Total row. There is no second builder.
- Plurals: `_rptPluralize(n, text)` replaces `{s}` with '' when n rounds to 1, else 's'. The Environmental Impact lines (report and Energy Graphics page) and the bill and building counts call it.
- Meter heading: in an unpresented quarter every Meter Performance table heading is `_rptMeterLabel(commodity, account)` + " Performance".
- Observations: for an unpresented quarter `_rptComResultText(list)` names a utility the strongest or weakest performer only when it saved; any other utility gets its dollar result ("Electric used $6,713 more than baseline"). A building with negative savings reads "<name> used $X (Y%) more than baseline".
- Savings rule (Matt, 2026-11-06): savings = (baseline-month use - current-month use) x current-month rate, by month, then added. Cost never enters savings. The baseline cost shown is the cost of the baseline-month bills (`blCostByCalMo`, collectReportData; electric = energy charges `commodityCost`), a display figure only. No year is written in the code: baseline month and current month. `_rptBuildingMonthMap` no longer falls back to baseline cost minus actual cost. Check: `node tools/test-report-savings-and-baseline-cost.js` (synthetic; runs again with all dates moved two years).
- Letterhead and footer: `rptPage` draws the wave footer and the "<period> Quarterly Report / Page N of M" label on every report page (`_waveAll`, :1732). The letterhead is on page 1. The cause of the loss: the wave and label were drawn only for pages with `letterhead:true`, and the quarterly pages passed `letterhead:false`. The same pages feed the screen, the PDF and Word.
- PDF sharpness: `downloadReportPdf()` (app/report-pdf.js) draws each page at `RPT_PDF_RASTER_SCALE` 2.5 (about 240 dpi), JPEG quality 0.75, with invisible selectable text on top. A 27 page report is about 7.6 MB. The old 1.25 scale looked out of focus at 100 percent zoom.
- Word colors: `_docxStampLiveStyles` stamps `data-dx-color` on the clone; `_docxStyleFmtFromEl` (app/docx-writer.js:1619) uses it when there is no inline color, so the red and green of the class rules (`.rpt-g`, `.rpt-r`) reach the .docx. Before, the detached clone had no cascade and the runs were black.
- Word corner text: `exportReportToDocx()` takes the "<client> / <period>" text of the first page and `_docxApplyHeaderText(zip, text)` (app/docx-writer.js:589) writes it, right aligned, into `word/header2.xml` (the default header). It is no longer in the page body.
- Environmental page: `increaseEquivalents` and `_equivalentsOf` (computations/pollution.js) give the same equivalents for an increase as `calculatePollutionCredits` gives for a decrease. A quarter with more pollution is titled "Pollution Added".
- Check: `node tools/test-report-pdf-letterhead-footer.js` clicks the real Download PDF button, renders the saved file with pdftoppm and fails when the letterhead (page 1) or the wave footer (page 1, page 2, last page) is missing or when pdftotext returns no footer text. Also `node tools/test-docx-class-colors.js`, `node tools/test-report-q3-review-findings.js`, `node tools/test-report-schedule-table.js`, `node tools/test-report-word-table-widths.js`, `node tools/test-report-escape.js`.
- Not yet removed: the v235 page functions (`_rptQCoverPage`, `_rptQResultsPage` and the other `_rptQ...Page` functions) and the `qRender` fields of `REPORT_SECTIONS` have no caller in the report; some tests still read them.

## Q3 2026 report review fixes, v2026.11.08.245 (2026-11-08, branch 2026-11-08-q3-v245-review-fixes)

No timer, env var, host or server change. Runs in the browser when a report renders or downloads. Check: `node tools/test-report-q3-review-findings.js` (section 14), `node tools/test-report-word-layout.js`, then download PDF and Word and count 31 pages.
- Rounding: every printed value is `Math.round` of its own exact value; a total rounds the exact sum. A month can differ from the quarter total by $1. `_rptApportion` is not used on month cells of the report pages and `_rptControlledRound` is deleted (app/report-engine.js: `_rptQFacts`, `_rptQShownCols`, Appendix B).
- Percent of baseline cost: `bldgPctBase` = the building baseline cost shown (`collectReportData`). The "actual plus savings" denominator and its note are removed.
- Baseline cost: `_rptBaselineBillCostByCalMo(m, bills, blMonths)` sums `_rptBillAmount` (energy charges only) over the baseline bills by calendar month; `collectReportData` and the Baseline Data page (`_bdBaselineEnergyCost`, app/calculators.js, `_buildBaselineDataHtml(b, projId, { report: true })`) both use it.
- Year to Date: `getMeterSavings` (computations/savings.js) rows carry `formulaSavings` (the contract formula result; 0 for a pinned override row). The month rows of `rptPageYearToDate` print `trueSav`, so a pinned month override does not change the month. The pin itself is not edited.
- Meter table (lib/perf-table.js, `mode: 'report'`): one column set for every electricity meter (Actual kW is the billed kW), kW with one decimal, whole-word two-line headers, and a dollar column total that is the sum of the cent values printed above it.
- Word: `_docxRun` keeps U+2212 in one run (no `noBreakHyphen`). `_rptQNoBreakMinus` turns a hyphen before `$` or a digit into U+2212. `_rptQThSameCase` sets `text-transform:none` on every `<th>`.
- Footer: label and "Page N of M" are both 14px; the page number sits 48px from the right edge, 6px from the bottom.

## Q3 2026 report page layout, v2026.11.08.246 (2026-11-08, branch 2026-11-08-q3-v246-layout)

No timer, env var, host or server change. Runs in the browser when a report renders or downloads. Check: `node tools/test-report-page-packing.js` (it fails on v245), then download PDF and Word and count 33 pages.
- Building summary: `_rptQBuildingSummaryPage` (app/report-engine.js) wraps the whole body in one `data-unit="keep"` block. `_rptQPackRun` packs whole blocks: two to a page when `_rptQBodyFits`, else one. A block is never split.
- Meter Performance heading: the `meterPerformance` row has `leadFirst: true`. `_rptQuarterReportHTML` gives the first meter block the section heading as its lead when it follows other blocks on a page. `_rptQPackRun` then marks that title as used, so the next meter page says "(continued)".
- Electricity by Building header: `Baseline<br>billed<br>kW` and `Actual<br>billed<br>kW` (`_rptQFuelDetailPages`). The unit never breaks.
- Year to Date: `rptPageYearToDate` prints "Q3 2026 savings not billed" (`_shortDisp` from `savingsNotBilledWithPresented`, computations/savings.js) above "Difference from the figures presented to the district". The Savings column adds to "Savings billed to date". Baseline and Actual cost cells are empty.

## Q3 2026 report gas cost, Baseline Data prices, summary spacing, v2026.11.08.247 (2026-11-08, branch 2026-11-08-q3-v247-gas-packing)

No timer, env var, host or server change. Runs in the browser. Check: `node tools/test-gas-cost-single-source.js`, `node tools/test-report-q3-review-findings.js` (section 15) and `node tools/test-report-page-packing.js`. The last two fail on v246.
- Zero therms = zero gas energy cost: `getBillGasCostOrNull` (computations/rates.js, about line 130) returns 0 for a bill with no gas charge and zero therms. Before, it returned the bill total, which is the $23.33 base charge. A bill with positive therms and no gas charge still returns the bill total. Every reader (report engine `_rptBillAmount`, Baseline Data, rates, flags, roll-ups) calls this one function. Savings do not move: the savings rate uses bills with therms above 0 only.
- Baseline Data (`_buildBaselineDataHtml`, app/calculators.js, about line 667): Average $/kWh = kWh cost / billed kWh, Average $/kW = kW cost / billed kW, Average $/therm = gas cost / billed therms, in the month rows and the TOTAL row. The Normalized kWh and Normalized therms columns stay normalized (the gas header said "Therms" before v248; the label changed, no figure). The propane gallons column is delivery-allocated billed gallons (`normalizePropaneDeliveries`), not weather-normalized, so its header stays "Propane Gal". The report cuts this table into Electricity and Natural Gas / Propane and Total Utility by header names (`rptBuildSiteBaselineData`, app/report-engine.js, `firstOther` and `names`): "Normalized therms", "Propane Gal", "Total Utility". Rename a header in `_buildBaselineDataHtml` and change both names there too; `node tools/test-report-q3-review-findings.js` (section 16) checks the pair. A zero divisor prints a dash. A month that has a baseline row prints its normalized therms even when 0 (the weather model is floored at 0 in summer); a month with no baseline row prints a dash.
- Building Summary spacing (`_rptQBuildingSummaryPage`, app/report-engine.js): heading margins 4px 0 2px, EUI gap 4px, and the block class `rpt-q-tight` (energy-department.html) sets table cell padding 1px top and bottom. The lead heading that `_rptQuarterReportHTML` adds for a `leadTitle` section uses margin 4px 0 2px. Two summaries fit one page (814 of 830px in the test).

## 7. How to keep this file current

Any commit that changes these facts must update this file in the same commit.
This includes a new or changed function, schedule, env var name, timer, host, or sign-in rule.

- Utility E bill reading (PDF/OCR import; no schedule, no env var). Account number: `_evgAccountsIn` / `_evgPickAccount` (app/energy-savings.js, near `_EVG_ADDR`) are the one reader used by `extractAll`, `extract`, `_acctForIdx` and `_pageOwnAccts`. They keep only the candidates of the longest digit length, so an OCR-damaged first digit (9 digits) loses to the clean 10-digit line. A candidate is one unbroken digit string (6 to 20 digits); a digit group after a space is not merged. On/Off-Peak kWh: `_decideOnOffPeakKWh` (app/bill-analysis.js, about line 1688) defers to the kWhConsumed-derived path when a leg has no readable rate line, kWhConsumed is not held, it agrees with the charge-basis total within 0.05 kWh, and exactly one leg self-verifies and also passes the strict half-cent check. How to check: `node test-kwh-corroboration.mjs` (expect all assertions pass).

## Compact PDF Storage keeps referenced PDFs (2026-10-08)
- What: `collectReferencedPdfKeys()` in app/site-functions.js scans all stored records (DB.getAll() and live utilityData) for PDF keys. `compactPdfStorage()` runs it after the remap commit. It deletes a non-canonical key only when no record uses it. If the scan throws, it deletes nothing (`scanFailed`).
- When: only when the user clicks the sidebar button "Compact PDF Storage". No timer. No env var.
- Check: run `node tools/test-compact-keeps-referenced-pdfs.js` (gate runs it). It must print 5 PASS.

## Gas supplier scanned invoices (2026-10-08, branch 2026-10-08-woodriver-sites)
- `_wreRebuildMissingCharges` (app/energy-savings.js:7652) runs inside `_parseWRESiteBlocks`, when a gas supplier invoice is read (browser, no schedule, no env var).
- Gas supplier invoice usage (`_parseWRESiteBlocks`, app/energy-savings.js, ship step after `_wreInvoiceRates`; browser, no schedule, no env var). A Sub-Total usage that only failed a "cannot verify" reason (bare digits, unverified low-confidence read, rebuilt from components) is kept and set `_mmbtuUnverified` + `_manualReview`; a usage the math contradicts is still removed. A bare whole-number usage of 100 or more gets 2 decimals (474 -> 4.74); `_wreBlendedInBand` (the one rate-band test, also used by `_wreSubTotalConfirmed`) may confirm it with the invoice-level rate. Safety net after the site loop: a shipped usage with no passing band test is marked for review unless the site usages add up to the invoice total (1% / 0.1 MMbtu). Check: `node test-wre-mmbtu-unverified.js`.
- A scan can cut off the right-hand $ columns. The site then has Mmbtu and Fuel but no charge. The charge is rebuilt as (Mmbtu + Fuel) x Rate. Rate = the most common Index rate on the invoice (at least 2 sites agree). The site is marked `_chargeRebuilt` on the block.
- Skipped (left to the manual-review checks): sites with Trigger or Special Weather rows, Fuel not 0.5% to 4% of Mmbtu, or Index and Sub-Total Mmbtu that disagree.
- A Sub-Total row with a lost label ("b-Total:", "E  -") is read when it is only two numbers (Mmbtu, Fuel) and the block has no Sub-Total yet.
- Check: `node tools/test-wre-scanned-sites.js` (synthetic). In the browser console the line "[WRE] Block parser" shows the site count.

## Constellation correction invoices and Baldwin metered lines (2026-10-08, branch 2026-10-08-constellation-baldwin)
- Constellation `extractAll` (app/energy-savings.js:7068): the site id window runs from the end of the last "Total Current Site Charges" line before the site to the start of the site (not the last 600 characters). A correction invoice (many "Service for" sections per site, one id header) now reads its ids and addresses. A site with no id of its own stays ID-UNREAD. Runs in the browser when a Constellation PDF is read; no schedule, no env var. Check: `node test-constellation-correction-invoice.mjs` and `node test-constellation-account-id.mjs`.
- Baldwin City `_parseMeteredLine` (app/energy-savings.js:11694): a charge comes only from the last token printed with cents (a trailing one-digit number is page noise). Usage is the token after the two reads. A row with usage but no readable charge is not dropped: it becomes a row with a null charge and `_manualReview` set. The split-column path calls the same function. Check: `node tools/test-baldwin-metered-line.js`.

## City utility page-total check (2026-10-09)

- Client A city utility bill reading (app/energy-savings.js, rule with `_extractNew`; browser, no schedule, no env var). The printed lines of one page must add up to the printed Current Bill. `_lbg_pageLineSum` is the one sum, `_lbg_pageTotalCheck` compares it (tolerance one cent, `_LBG_PAGE_TOTAL_TOLERANCE`), `_lbg_flagPageTotalMismatch` holds every bill of a page that does not add up (`_pageTotalMismatch`, `_manualReview`). A page with a held Gas line is not checked.
- `_lbg_labeledAmount` reads both page totals. The Current Bill label is fuzzy (`_LBG_CURRENT_BILL_LABEL`: "Bin", "Bil") and its loose pass stays on one line.
- Repairs, each only when the repaired value makes the page add up to the cent: a Stormwater amount that differs from the one most Stormwater lines in the same file agree on (`_lbg_peerStormCharge`, `extractAll` re-reads the page with `opts.peerStorm`); a Water Protection line whose decimal point was lost (343 for 3.43).
- A Fuel Adjustment worked out as a residual is held when the same account prints a commodity on another page of the file that this page lacks (`opts.missingSlots`, `_lbg_commoditiesByAccount`).
- A Gas charge of 0 beside real therms takes the same reconcile-or-hold path as a missing charge. A letter glued to the front of the therms figure (`usageLeadGarble`) adds a "first digit may be missing" note to the hold reason.
- Gas therms that fail the rate check while the page proves the Gas charge are filled only when two sources agree: meter reads times the meter's therm factor (from its other bills in the file or the saved bills) and the charge; or one-digit repair of the scan plus the charge. The hover text names the method. Otherwise the bill stays held. Check: import a file with a gas page whose therms lost a digit; the hold clears and the therms field shows the filled value.
- One autofix rule for every reader (app/energy-savings.js, 2026-10-20): a value whose read failed its check is filled only when two independent sources agree on it and no other value has as much support; a tie, a disagreement or one source keeps the hold or flag. The one-digit repair of the scanned text only confirms a value (it proposes values only when no other source does). The hover text names the method and what the scan read. Tolerance is the bill own rounding (cent-exact for money, 4 decimals for quantities).
- Utility E (electric) sources, run at the end of the electric reader for every bill: total kWh when reads times multiplier did not match (reads times the printed multiplier, else the multiplier the same account other bills share in the file or in the saved bills; digit repair of the scan; energy charge divided by the one printed rate); billed kW when its charge did not fit (digit repair; printed demand charge divided by the printed demand rate; the printed rate is kept); number of days when the read dates, the billing period and the printed Days differ (read dates, billing period, Billing Date, printed Days digits); one empty charge cell the position engine held (printed Subtotal, else Current Charges minus the lines below it, minus the other lines; digit repair of the cell text quoted in the hold). Check: `node tools/test-autofix-electric.js`; with the real ground-truth files, `node tools/gate-bill-ground-truth.js` (Utility E 692 correct / 0 wrong / 3 held).
- Billing-detail pages: `_findDate` separator class fixed (colon then space now matches) and the July-to-December month filter removed; due and penalty dates are skipped by label instead.
- City bill date row (app/energy-savings.js): `_lbg_readDateRow` is the one reader of the 5-date period row: reads by column, repairs a slash OCR-read as 1 only when one calendar split fits the row order, and leaves an unreadable or unprovable column empty (with only 4 readable dates, a column is assigned only when exactly one unreadable token sits between the first and last date, which proves its place; otherwise start, end and bill date all stay empty); the city-bill post-step in `extractAll` holds the bill for review when days are outside 25-35, the start differs from the account's previous end, or a column was not read. `_lbg_labeledAmount` reads a comma before 2 final digits as the decimal point ("$2,584,70" = 2584.70; "$45,00" = 45.00). Check: `node tools/test-lbg-date-row.js` (synthetic).
- Fuel Adjustment sign (city gas, app/energy-savings.js `_lbg_fuelAdjSign`, browser, no schedule, no env var): the ONE sign rule. A Fuel Adjustment read without a minus is a credit (negative), unless the page lines add up to the printed Current Bill only with it positive (`_lbg_pageLineSum`, one cent). A printed minus (leading, trailing, parentheses, or the cent sign "¢" the scan prints for the minus) is never turned positive. The three old forced-credit copies (the older city readers) call the same function with no page. `_extractNew` runs it before the Water, Stormwater and Water Protection proofs. The old "sign could not be confirmed" hold is gone (a read with no proof is a credit). A page with NO Fuel Adjustment label and a residual that cannot be a Fuel Adjustment (larger than the Gas line, with every other line read) derives nothing: the bill is held by the page-total check, whose reason names the dollar gap.
- Number shapes (same file): a comma before the last 2 digits is the decimal point everywhere (`_lbg_commaCents`, used by `_lbg_cleanCents` and `_lbg_labeledAmount`; "4,00" = 4.00); a charge with 3 decimals ("23.337") keeps its first 2 (`_lbg_pickChargeToken`); a Water amount whose first digits were broken (",254.80" after "12") lists its possible values (`_lbg_brokenThousandsCandidates`) and keeps one ONLY when it makes the page add up to the printed Current Bill to the cent (with the file's flat-fee Stormwater when this page's Stormwater is unread). Marker `_auto_corrected_WaterCharge`. `EXTRACTOR_RULES_VERSION` is 3, so the stored-bill repair pass reads each eligible bill again once; it only writes values printed on the page, so it does not change a stored Fuel Adjustment.
- How to check: `node tools/test-lbg-page-reconcile.js` (expect ALL PASSED); `node tools/test-lbg-printed-gas-line.js`; `node tools/test-lbg-fuel-adj-sign.js` (synthetic; sign rule and number shapes).

## Gas supplier usage checks, site alignment and per-customer site count (2026-10-09, branch 2026-10-09-wood-river)
- Where: app/energy-savings.js, gas supplier rule `extractAll`: `_parseWRESiteBlocks` (7723), `_wreRepairComponentDecimals` (8470), `_wreSubTotalConfirmed` (8490), `_wreComponentsUsage` (8510), `_wreInvoiceRates` (8526). app/bill-analysis.js: `_wreExpectedSiteCount` (counts the saved gas meters for the invoice customer; null means no hold), `_gateWRE_siteCountCheck` (935-950), `_billHasKeyField` (15645). They run in the browser when a gas supplier invoice is read. No schedule. No env var.
- Decimal repair: a component line read as a whole number of 100 or more ("6003") is divided by 100 when that makes the components fit the printed Sub-Total and the whole number does not.
- Usage recovery: when the cross-check fails, the Sub-Total usage is kept if its charge / (usage + fuel) falls inside the printed rate band (a site with no readable rate uses the rate that at least 2 other sites print). Else the usage is rebuilt from the component lines when each passes the rate check and their charges add up to the Sub-Total charge. Else it stays empty and held. Flags: `_wreComponentLineGarbled`, `_wreUsageFromComponents`, `_wreComponentDecimalRepaired`.
- A Sub-Total read as a whole number of 100 or more (no decimal point) is held unless the rate and charge confirm the repaired value (was: only above 999).
- Other OCR pass (consensus merge): rates, component lines and review flags travel with a value taken from another pass, so the same checks run on it.
- Missing "Service Address:" line: a component or Sub-Total line that arrives after the open block already has its Sub-Total opens an empty block (`_stubBlock`, no address, no account). Later sites keep their position.
- Site-count gate: expected count is read at run time from the user's saved gas meters whose account equals the invoice customer number (`_wreExpectedSiteCount`, app/bill-analysis.js). A customer with no saved meters is never held by the count.
- Check: `node tools/test-wre-engine-fixes.js` (synthetic, 35 checks). Also `node tools/test-wre-parser-regression.js`, `node tools/test-wre-scanned-sites.js`.

## Utility E meter-table digit repair (2026-10-09)
- No schedule, no env var. Runs inside bill reading (PDF/OCR import) in the Utility E extract function (app/energy-savings.js, the one that holds `_meterRe`) and `_postExtractionVerify` (app/bill-analysis.js).
- Decimal-point damage: `_evgRepairMeterDecimals` (app/energy-savings.js, next to `_evgAddrNorm`) is the one repair for a decimal point that OCR read as a space ("21,506 4170") or as a comma ("27,0460") on a meter row. It swaps one character for one character, so row offsets stay valid. A bare digit run in the KW, RKVA, Difference and kWh columns gets its point back in the row loop (`_lostPoint`); Difference and kWh are adopted only when Difference x Multiplier equals kWh. The meter-row regex now needs the first date to start a token, so a meter id glued to the date cannot shift the columns.
- No guessing on a tie (`_digitsDiffering`): the kWh column alone does not rewrite Difference and the reads when the opposite explanation (Difference right, kWh and one read each off by one digit) is equally likely. A single misread read blocks the two-read search. Multi-meter kWh uses Difference x Multiplier for a row (`_rowKwhUsed`) when its kWh column is a one-digit misread of it and either the reads agree with Difference, or a one-digit repair of a read explains Difference and none explains the column. A multi-meter sum that includes such a row, or a row whose lost decimal point was restored, is marked `_meterInfo.kwhRepaired`. The kWh check in `_postExtractionVerify` then takes the self-verified printed charge-line total when it is in the same 0.5% bucket; sums of rows read as printed are kept. A bare 5 or 6 digit StartRead next to an EndRead that lost its point gets its point back (a new meter reads a single digit before the point), and reads that agree with the printed Difference skip the EndRead/StartRead ratio check.
- Bill checks (app/bill-analysis.js): `_isMultiMeterBill` is the one multi-meter test; the 500000 kWh ceiling scales with the row count. `_findAltPassBill` matches an alternate OCR pass by account and period, never by position. `_missingCriticalFields` treats Total Amount Due and Total Current Charges as one total. `_canWitnessRead` lets a neighbor bill fill a missing StartRead or EndRead only when the neighbor's own reads prove out and the address matches; the day gap now uses `calcDays`, so the fill runs on slash dates. The ActualKW alignment to BilledKW = TDCkW now corrects any difference above 0.00005.
- Check: `node tools/test-utility-e-digit-repair.js` (synthetic, expect ALL PASSED). Also `node test-kwh-corroboration.mjs`, `node tools/test-utility-e-addr-dates.js`, `node tools/test-utility-e-bill-boundary.js`.

## Kansas Gas Service charge-line check (2026-10-09, branch 2026-10-09-kgs-gas)
- What: a Kansas Gas Service bill whose charge lines do not add up to Total Current Charges is checked in the browser after the bill is read. Where: app/bill-analysis.js `sumKgsComponentCharges` (computations/rates.js, the one sum; list `KGS_COMPONENT_CHARGE_FIELDS`), `_kgsRecoverSuspectLine` (1890), and the Pass B2 block in `_postExtractionVerify` (scope `kgsBills`, about 4686).
