The extractor in app/energy-savings.js lists charge lines that printed but were not read (`_kgsSuspectLines` field; helpers `_kgsUnreadableLabelCount` 6210, `_kgsSuspectLines` 6219, `_kgsFranchiseTotals` 6232).
- Rule: if exactly one line is unreadable or lost its decimal point, the line is the Total Current Charges minus the other lines. A bare-digit read must be within one digit of that value. More than one suspect line, or a bigger difference, changes nothing and the existing sum-mismatch flag (`_sum_mismatch_kgs`) is set. A rebuilt line is flagged `_auto_recovered_B2_<field>` and shown on the review screen.
- Sales Tax (2026-10-27): the extractor (app/energy-savings.js, KGS path, search `SalesTax`) sums every State, City and County Tax line on the page into `SalesTax` (null when no tax line prints, or when one prints with an unreadable amount). It is saved as `salesTax` through the one table `KGS_GAS_SAVED_FIELDS` (app/bill-analysis.js:33), so all save paths, the duplicate merge and the save-field gate get it from one place. `SalesTax` is in `KGS_COMPONENT_CHARGE_FIELDS` (computations/rates.js; `CHARGE_FIELDS_GAS` is that list); `sumKgsComponentCharges` sums it and is the ONE KGS line-sum: the gas sum warning, the GasCharge recovery by subtraction, the total check, Pass B2, the suspect-line recovery and `_kgsAutofixFranchise` all call it. Gas rate math stays on `gasCharge` only; `salesTax` is not read by `getBillTaxCost`. Check: `node tools/test-kgs-sales-tax.js`. Not done: the Utility E electric `SalesTax` is extracted but not saved; `app/scorecard.js` billCost reads `getBillTaxCost`.
- Pass B2 scope: before this change Pass B2 ran only when the file-level rule name equalled the provider name. The rule name is the shared gas rule name, so Pass B2 never ran. It now runs for each bill whose own provider is Kansas Gas Service.
- When: whenever a Kansas Gas Service PDF is read (browser, no schedule). Needs: no env var.
- Check: `node tools/test-kgs-suspect-line.js` (synthetic, 14 checks) and `node tools/test-kgs-total-decimal-drop.js`.
- Delayed Payment Charge (2026-10-28): it is in `_kgsSuspectLines` (app/energy-savings.js, Gas Utility rule), so a value read as bare digits ("127" for 1.27) or unreadable is rebuilt from the total by `_kgsRecoverSuspectLine`. The KGS review layout (`_LAYOUT_KGS`, app/bill-analysis.js) has a Delayed Payment Charge row so the running total equals the line-sum. Check: `node tools/test-kgs-delayed-payment.js` (synthetic; also checks that every `KGS_COMPONENT_CHARGE_FIELDS` entry has a layout row).

## Bill matching and save path (2026-10-09, branch 2026-10-09-match-save)
- Where: app/bill-analysis.js, app/core.js, app/csv-import.js. All run in the browser when a bill is read, saved, checked for duplicates or imported. No schedule. No env var.
- One match result: `findMeterMatch` (bill-analysis.js) now gives every account-number hit a `customerId` (`_stampCustomerId`). Before, an account hit had none, so `autoAssignAllSavedBills` (core.js:3863) found no building and skipped every identity-matched bill (0 of 65 assigned).
- One auto-route rule: `_isAutoRoutableMatch` (bill-analysis.js:9142) = account/meter-number hit or the unambiguous building+commodity fallback, with a real meter. `_resolveBillDestination` and `autoAssignAllSavedBills` both call it. An address-only guess stays in Saved Bills. A period already on the meter is not added twice (Auto-Assign All).
- One address score: `_scoreIdentityCandidates` (5693) scores each same-account meter against the bill service address; `_pickIdentityCandidate` (5721) uses it and returns `matchType: 'ambiguous'` when two scores are within `_IDENTITY_TIE_MARGIN` (0.03) and none is an exact match. `_identityAddressScore` reads a split ("BALL FIELDS") or singular ("BALLFIELD") site tag as the stored one (`_addrTailKeys`).
- Shared by the save path: `_chooseAmongIdentityHits` (5765) is used by `_autoCreateMeterAndSaveBill` (21176) so two meters on one account are told apart by address, not "first found". If address cannot decide it returns null and the bill stays in Saved Bills. `_meterLosesAddressContest` (5774) is used by `_checkDuplicates` (11831): a bill whose address fits a sibling meter better is not a duplicate of this meter's stored bill.
- Duplicate bills (2026-10-20, app/bill-analysis.js): a blank new value is never a difference and never replaces a saved value. `_dupHasValue` is the one blank test; `_dupApplyRow` is the one row copy (Overwrite copies every non-blank new value, Merge fills blank saved fields) used by the save paths; `_applyDupUpdate` (Overwrite, Merge, Apply Selected, Overwrite All, Merge All) uses `_dupHasValue`. `_buildDiffFields` leaves out a blank new value, so a bill whose only difference is a blank counts as 0 fields differ ("Identical (safe to skip)"). A gas bill compares therms (`gasBillSaveTherms`, `resolveGasUsageThermsOrNull`; old gas bills keep therms in `kwh`) and the row is labelled Therms (`_DUP_FIELD_LABELS`). The duplicate list in the top row of the PDF / OCR page names the field with both values ("Therms saved 68.139, new 70.2"; 2 named and "+N more" when several differ) with `_dupDiffSummaryText`. Check: `node tools/gate-dup-compare.js` (in the regression gate static list).
- History: `_historyBillIsSameSite` (491) and `_historyForBill` (504) keep out of a bill's history any saved bill whose meter number or service address differs. Used by `detectStatisticalOutliers` and by the verify loop.
- Page-proved total (2026-10-19): `detectStatisticalOutliers` (app/bill-analysis.js) skips the total-charges outlier warning when `_pageProvesTotal(bill)` is true: the City reader (app/energy-savings.js, after `_lbg_pageTotalCheck`) sets `_pageTotalProved` on each bill of a page whose lines add up to the printed Current Bill to the cent, and the bill is not held (`_gateTripped`, `_manualReview`), the total is not hand-edited (`_userCorrected_TotalCurrentCharges`), and no `_auto_corrected_*` field exists. A real usage swing stays with the Bills-table `usage_vs_same_month` rule. Runs on every extraction (no timer, no network). Check: `node tools/test-evergy-altpass-outlier.js` (section 5, 10 checks). To check by eye: upload a City bill page whose gas total is far from the same month of other years; the Gas period button shows no warning count when the page adds up.
- `_inferBillCommodity` (8841): `_saveBillToMatchedMeter` returns null (held) when a bill with no commodity label has gas, propane or kWh fields that name another commodity than the target meter.
- `_isPlausibleAddressAlias` (6479): Import Building List (`_bldgImportAliasFor`, csv-import.js:5026) adds a service address as an alias only when it is the same street or at least 0.60 alike.
- `_mergeCsvRowIntoBill` (csv-import.js:505): a CSV re-import keeps a value the user corrected by hand (`_userCorrected`); only the typed ERASE clears it.
- Check: `node tools/test-match-save-paths.js` (expect ALL PASS; synthetic).
- Utility E charge-line reader (browser, when a bill PDF is read; no schedule, no env var). `_evgChargeAmounts` (app/energy-savings.js, just above the Utility E extract function) is the one reader of dollar amounts on a charge line; `getAmt` and the missing-charge scan both call it. It takes all decimals of a figure, skips rates (after "at" or OCR "al", before "per kW", under 1 with 3 or more decimals) and skips 1-decimal figures in prose. `_evgRateDigitRepair` (same place) repairs a rate that has a digit 3 read as 8 or 9, or one extra digit, only when the repair makes qty x rate equal the line's charge; it runs in the rate cross-check inside the Utility E extract function for single-part lines within 5% of the derived rate (larger gaps use charge / qty as before). the Utility E extract function sets `Commodity: 'Electric'` on every bill. Check: `node tools/test-utility-e-line-parser.js` (expect PASS 18 of 18; synthetic).

## Charge-field lists, kWh rate sum and OCR canvas ceiling (2026-10-09, branch 2026-10-09-g11-tests-dedupe)

- One list per non-electric commodity: `CHARGE_FIELDS_GAS`, `_WATER`, `_SEWER`, `_STORMWATER`, `_PROPANE_SUM` (app/bill-analysis.js:33-47). The line-item check, both sum pills and the sum banner use them. Electric uses `CHARGE_FIELDS_ELECTRIC_SUM` (app/bill-analysis.js, after `UTILITY_E_COMPONENT_CHARGE_FIELDS`): the Utility E fields plus `ElectricCharge` and `FuelAdjustment`. The sum banner and both sum pills share it, so they agree on every bill.
- The effective kWh rate in the electric extractor (app/energy-savings.js:4626) calls `sumElectricEnergyCharges` (computations/rates.js:285). Any page or test that loads app/energy-savings.js must load computations/rates.js first.
- (2026-10-09, branch 2026-10-09-charge-dedupe-2) The Utility E rate recalculation and `_extractedToBillRowCosts` (app/bill-analysis.js, in `_postExtractionVerify` and above `_extractedToBillRowCosts`) call `sumElectricEnergyCharges` too. The general line-item check uses `CHARGE_FIELDS_ELECTRIC_SUM` for Electric. Check: `node tools/test-bill-save-fixes.js`.
- (2026-10-09, branch 2026-10-09-compsum-dedupe) `UTILITY_E_COMPONENT_CHARGE_FIELDS` now lives in computations/rates.js (about line 282), with `sumElectricComponentCharges(parsed)` (cents-rounded sum of that list). The charge reconciliation in the Utility E extract function (app/energy-savings.js, `_compSum`) calls it, so no hand-written list is left there. Any page or test that loads app/bill-analysis.js or app/energy-savings.js must load computations/rates.js first. Check: `node tools/test-bldgname-needs-review.js`, `node tools/test-bill-save-fixes.js`, `node tools/test-utility-e-line-parser.js`.
- `_clampOcrSuperScale` (app/bill-analysis.js:14069) keeps the OCR supersample canvas under 36 million pixels and 16384 pixels a side (`OCR_MAX_CANVAS_PIXELS`, `OCR_MAX_CANVAS_SIDE`). `_renderPageHQ` calls it. A letter page at the highest pass is about 19.9 million pixels, so normal pages do not change. The scale never goes below the requested scale.
- Check: `node tools/test-ocr-canvas-ceiling.js` and `node tools/test-provider-synthetic-bills.js` (expect all passed; synthetic).

## Utility E billing details: service dates, NumberOfDays, Customer Chg cents, printed totals (2026-10-19)
- Runs in the browser when a Utility E bill PDF is read (no schedule, no env var, no server call). Changed after a one-page bill read null dates, the Facilities amount as the Customer Charge, and the charge sum ($2,411.08) instead of the printed Current Charges ($2,294.81).
- Service period: `_EVG_SERVICE_FROM_SRC` (app/energy-savings.js, above `_evgAccountsIn`) accepts a 2 to 4 digit end year ("to 09/02/20." when the scan cuts the page edge). `_evgServicePeriodFrom(match)` / `_evgServicePeriod(text)` are the ONE reader of the pair: a cut end year is taken from the start year (+1 when the end month is before the start month). The fill is recorded as `_auto_corrected_BillingPeriodEnd` {original "09/02/20", corrected "09/02/2026", derived: true} (the one change history; the Edit Billing Period window and the field popup show it). Every caller goes through it: the extract function (`_svc`), the section splitter (`sfMatches`), the section date keys, and the raw-text viewer in app/bill-analysis.js.
- NumberOfDays: `_evgNumberOfDays(readStart, readEnd, periodStart, periodEnd, billDate, printedDays)` (app/energy-savings.js, same place) is the ONE rule: the meter read dates (Start Read Date to End Read Date, "08/05" to "09/03"), read to read with `calcDays(..., false)` (exclusive, like the printed Days column); the read dates print without a year, which comes from the billing period, else from the bill date (+1 when the end month is before the start month). The read-date count wins (Matt's rule: Number of Days comes from the meter read dates, not the billing period dates). The billing-period count is used ONLY when a valid printed Days value (an integer 1-45) differs from the read-date count and equals the billing-period count (a read date misread by a day); the source then says "billing period (the read dates give N days; the printed Days column equals the billing period)". No printed Days, or an implausible one (for example 3647 or 50), means the read dates. Without read dates the billing period is used. `_evgSetNumberOfDays(result, printedDays, billDate)` is the one writer of `NumberOfDays` and `result._numberOfDaysCheck` = { source, readStart, readEnd, readDays, periodDays, printedDays, printedDaysAgree }; it runs at the end of the extract function (after the read dates are validated against the billing period) and in extractAll after the winning date pair is stamped. A first value computed before `result` feeds only the kW-charge proration in `xRate`. `result._billDate` keeps the page's Billing Date for the year.
- Customer Chg: `NEXT_CHG_LINE` (the stop rule of every next-line amount search in `xChg`, the Tax exempt / Franchise / Sales Tax readers and `_evgOtherChargeLine`) is now built from `_EVG_KW` (the one list of charge-label patterns, moved above it) plus the tiered Energy, Subtotal and Current Charges labels. The old private list required "Fac " or "Facilities " and did not stop at an OCR "Facilties Chg" line, so "Customer Chg ..... $48}" took $165.17 from the next line. `xRate` calls read `_EVG_KW` too. When the Customer Chg line shows dollars but no cents ("$48}", "$48.", "$48.9"), `_custPartial` reads the digits and the cents come ONLY from the printed Subtotal minus the other printed lines above it (`sumElectricSubtotalCharges`, computations/rates.js), and only when that result starts with the read digits; recorded as `_auto_corrected_CustomerCharge` {original: null, corrected, reason, derived: true}. Otherwise `_customerChargePartial` {read, subtotal, othersSum} is recorded and the field stays empty.
- Subtotal: `_EVG_SUBTOTAL_LABEL_SRC` / `_EVG_SUBTOTAL_LABEL` (app/energy-savings.js, same place) is the ONE label pattern ("Subtotal", OCR "Sublotal", "Subtota1", "SUBLOLA"); `subtotalAmount`, `totalDue`, the bill-offset fallback, `_evgOtherChargeLine`, `CHG_STOP` and the missing-charge scan read it. The amount is taken from the Subtotal line itself (the dot leader can OCR as up to 80 letters) or within 30 characters when it wraps.
- Printed totals: `UTILITY_E_BELOW_SUBTOTAL_FIELDS` (FranchiseFee, SalesTax, MiscellaneousCharge), `sumElectricSubtotalCharges(parsed)` and `checkElectricPrintedTotals(parsed)` (computations/rates.js, under `sumElectricComponentCharges`) are the one identity: lines above the Subtotal = Subtotal; Subtotal + the lines below it = Current Charges (tolerance $0.01). The extract function stores it as `result._printedTotalCheck`. Stage 3 of `_postExtractionVerify` (app/bill-analysis.js) treats the printed total as corroborated when the Subtotal equals it OR Subtotal + fees equal it. A computed charge sum is NEVER written over a printed total any more: the former "small, well-provenanced" auto-write (`_totalFromChargeSum`, `_totalSource`) is removed; the sum is proposed in `_correction_pending_TotalCurrentCharges` and the bill is held. The no-op correction cleanup in the same function compares non-numeric originals (dates) as text, so a date fill record is not dropped.
- Check: `node tools/test-utility-e-billing-details.js` (synthetic; expect ALL PASSED). Also `node tools/test-utility-e-detached-amounts.js`, `node tools/test-utility-e-addr-dates.js`, `node tools/test-utility-e-line-parser.js`.

## Provider routing and OCR pass control (2026-10-09, branch 2026-10-09-provider-routing)

- Runs in the browser when a bill PDF is read (no schedule, no env var). `_pickFileRule` (app/energy-savings.js:13243) is the one choice of the rule that owns a file (the rule marked `fileFirst` first, then the first rule whose detect accepts the text). Both read paths and the OCR retry in app/bill-analysis.js call it (:9991, :16250, :16443).
- `_extractFileBills` (app/energy-savings.js:13263) reads the file. A page the file rule does not detect, but another rule claims (`_pageRule`, :13251), goes to that other rule. A page nobody claims (a continuation page) stays with the file rule. A file with no such page is read as `rule.extractAll(text)`, unchanged. A split file gets `_mixedProvider = true` on the returned array. The Gas Utility rule claims a page on a brand name only (`detectPage`), because its file-level detect also accepts any text with "therms" and "bill".
- `_GAS_BRAND_RE` (app/energy-savings.js:6436) is the one list of gas brands (Spire, Laclede, Kansas Gas Service, Atmos, Black Hills). The Gas Utility rule detect, `detectPage` and the OCR provider test read it.
- OCR scoring (app/bill-analysis.js, module level above `extractPDFText`): `_detectProviderEx` (:14434) names the scoring set and says whether the sign is strong. `makeScorePage` (:14482) now builds one scorer per page (:14911). A page scores with its own provider; until it shows one, it uses the last provider seen on an earlier page. Other gas brands use the `gas` signal set.
- `_ocrPageNeedsRetry` (:14511) is the one test for the extra OCR passes: the page text needs an account label, a money amount with cents and a date. No provider has its own test.
- The provider recovery guard on rotated pages counts a second pass as agreement only when that pass has a strong sign (`_detectProviderEx(...).strong`).
- Check: `node tools/test-page-provider-split.js` (expect all passed; synthetic).

## Import buildings, meters and bills from CSV (2026-10-12, branch 2026-10-12-meter-import)

- Runs in the browser when the user clicks a button. No schedule, no timer, no env var, no server call. The buttons are `+ Add Building`, `Import List`, `Import Bills` (`bldgAddImportButtonsHTML`, app/report-engine.js:12690). The same row shows on the sidebar Utility Data page and on the project Utility Data tab.
- `Import List` (modal `#bldgImportModal`). `parseBuildingListTable` (app/csv-import.js:4898) reads a CSV or Excel sheet. A file with a Commodity, Provider, Account #, Meter # or Bill file column is read by header name, one row per meter. A file without them is the older building list: A name, B sq ft, C address, F to H gas (Kansas Gas Service, "/" splits two meters). `_bldgImportAnnotate` (:5066) marks each row against the project (building exists, meter already there, or a problem such as an unknown commodity). `importBuildingList` (:5250) joins the building of the same name in the project, or creates it with `addUDBldg`, then adds the new meters. A meter number is read and stored as text, so a 13-digit number and "a | b" stay exact. The commodity must be one of `ALL_COMMODITIES` (app/core.js:2884).
- One rule for "is this meter the one with this account and meter number": `udMeterMatchesIdentity` (:5044) and `findUDMetersByIdentity` (:5054). Import List uses it to skip a meter that is already there. For a meter already there, Import List still sets its Bill file when the list value is not blank and differs (`importBuildingList`, app/csv-import.js); a blank value never clears it, and no other field changes. The preview says "Bill file updated". Check: `node test-import-list-billfile-dom.js` (synthetic). Import Bills uses it to route a row.
- `Import Bills` (modal `#billsMultiModal`). `planBillFiles` (:5450) sends each file to ONE meter by its file name (it must equal the meter's `billFile`, set from the Bill file column of the meter list), or each row by the Account # and Meter # columns inside the file. A file or row that fits no meter, or more than one, is listed in the preview and is not imported. `importBillsMulti` (:5672) saves the matched files. A start date that is already stored is updated, not added, so the same file twice adds 0 bills.
- The bill reader and the bill save step are shared with the one-meter dialog: `parseBillCsvText` (:197) and `applyBillRowsToMeter` (:719).
- One CSV line splitter: `_csvTextLines` (app/csv-import.js:187) drops a byte-order mark, splits on line breaks, trims and skips blank lines. The bill reader, the list reader and `planBillFiles` all call it. A line break inside a quoted cell is not supported.
- Meter units: Import List stores no `billUnit` / `displayUnit`. `getMeterBillUnit` / `getMeterDisplayUnit` (app/utility-data.js) give the default from the commodity, the same as for a meter made by the meter roster import.
- One CSV with Account # and Meter # columns may also have a Commodity column. It tells an electric account from a gas account with the same number. If the rows then have no kWh (Electric) or therms (Gas) values for the meter they reached, they are listed as not imported. `importBillsMulti` finds each meter again by id when the button is pressed, so a reload of the project data while the window is open cannot send bills to a stale object; a meter that is gone is listed, not written.
- Account and meter numbers compare with spaces and dashes ignored (`_macNormAcct`, app/meter-autocreate.js:34, the roster importer's rule). "G-1" and "G1" are the same number. Import List shows such a meter as "already there"; Import Bills refuses a row when two stored meters fit.
- Text from a CSV, an Excel sheet or a file name is shown through `_escHtml` (lib/formatting.js) everywhere it goes into HTML: the import previews, the building list, the project and building views, the meter header, the Bills table, the Edit and move lists, the HVAC load tab, the PDF assign lists. Toasts use textContent. How to check: `node test-meter-import-xss-dom.js` (synthetic hostile text; expect 0 failed; `window.__xss` stays undefined).
- Check: `node test-meter-import-dom.js` (synthetic data, expect 68 passed, includes real .xlsx input); `node test-meter-import-xss-dom.js`; `node test-import-list-project-tab-dom.js`; `node test-import-list-empty-project-dom.js`; `node tools/test-csv-header-match.js`; `node tools/test-csv-partial-reimport.js`.

## One rate function and cost-only pricing estimator (2026-10-13, branch 2026-10-13-phase1-one-rate-cost-only-pricing)

- Browser only. No schedule, no timer, no env var, no server call.
- ONE owner of the projected-savings utility rate: `computeSeasonalBldgRates` (computations/rates.js:732). It reads BASELINE-PERIOD bills only: each meter's bills whose month is in `m.baseline.months` (`_dashGetBaselineBills`, app/core.js), or the project fallback months when the meter has none (`_udProjBaselineMonthsFallback`, app/utility-data.js). No baseline gives rate 0. Season: Jun-Sep summer, Oct-May winter. It returns kWh, kW and therm rates for summer and winter, plus a flat baseline mean (`kwhRate`, `kwRate`, `thermRate`) used only by the BAS Trends display lookup (`btBldgRate`, app/bas-trends.js:3179). `calcBldgDefaultRates` is deleted; its callers (Energy Savings, report inputs, BAS Savings Calc, ECM Add as Measure) call the owner. Deleted copies: `getProjectRates`, `btGetBlendedRate`, `_pricingGetProjectAnnualElec` (its 0.1 fallback). `resolveMeterRate` (rates.js) stays: it gives a rate for ONE meter and ONE month through the 5-step missing-rate cascade for bill-based actual savings (Project Performance / Meter Performance). It is a different job and does not read the baseline bills.
- A saved measure keeps its own `m.rates` (the user can edit it). New measures, "Add as Measure" and "Reset rates to building defaults" take the owner's baseline-period rates. Saved rates made before 2026-10-13 came from all bills and stay until the user resets them.
- Superseded 2026-10-14 (phase 5, see "Calc templates take their baseline from the HVAC Load Estimate" below): ECM template rate inputs and `ecmPrice` are deleted. A template shows only the matrix pricing of its monthly measure (`ecmMeasureDollars`: `calcMeasureDollars` + `measureRatesNow`).
- The pricing estimator (app/pricing-estimator.js) owns COST only. Removed: fan fraction 0.12, the literature savings % map, the dollar chips, the portfolio savings roll-up, the "Fan energy %" input. The footer line `_pricingSavingsLineHTML` (:599) shows the project's projected savings read from `getProjMatrixSavingsTotal` (app/energy-savings.js:580), or "No savings estimate yet." One pricing rule for a measure: `calcMeasureDollars` (:555), each month's quantity x that month's seasonal rate, summed; the matrix table, footer and the Cost Estimate line all call it.
- Water, sewer and stormwater rates (2026-10-13): the same owner returns `waterRate`, `sewerRate`, `stormwaterRate` (usage-weighted, see "Baseline rates are usage-weighted" below), `waterUnit`/`sewerUnit`/`stormwaterUnit` (the meter's own bill unit from `getMeterBillUnit`, never converted; stormwater is `bill` = mean monthly charge) and `waterMonthly`/`sewerMonthly`/`stormwaterMonthly` (12 calendar months, annual mean when a month has no bill). Meters of one commodity with different bill units give rate 0 and no unit. The Utility Data Baseline pills (`_udBaselineRatePills`) read these. Check: `node tools/test-utility-seasons.js`.
- Monthly pricing has ONE owner path (2026-10-13, phase 1 part 2): `measureRateAt(rates, mo)` (the season rule: Jun-Sep summer, Oct-May winter; gas uses `gasSummer`/`gasWinter`, and a rate set with neither uses its flat `thermRate`, so saved measures price as saved) and `calcMeasureDollars(m, rates, opts)` (app/energy-savings.js, both above `getProjMatrixSavingsTotal`). It returns annual `kwh/kw/gas/propane/total` and `monthly[12]`. `opts.cents` rounds each monthly component to the cent (half up) for the Baseline + BAS Savings Report print rule. Deleted copies: the pricing loops in `getBldgMeasureSavingsByMo` (computations/savings.js, now sums owner months), `solarApplyToMeasure` and `bcApplyToMeasure` (app/calculators.js), the matrix row, quarter, footer and recalc loops (app/energy-savings.js), and Woodland `_wdSeasonalRates`, `wdOptionAnnualDollar`, `wdOptionDollarByMonth` (app/report-engine-woodland.js). New measures take `gasSummer`/`gasWinter` from `computeSeasonalBldgRates`. Typing a flat Gas $/Therm in the matrix clears that measure's `gasSummer`/`gasWinter` (`svUpdateMsrRate`). Project Performance / Meter Performance is not changed: it uses each bill's own rate (`resolveMeterRate`). Saved measure rates are not refreshed.
- The Louisburg restore blocks (two `Idempotent restore` blocks in `_renderSavingsContent`, app/energy-savings.js) are REMOVED (2026-10-13, see the next section). The measures stay in the saved data.
- Gate: `tools/gate-single-rate-source.js` also fails on `SUMMER_MOS.includes(` or a `* rates.<rate field>` multiply outside `measureRateAt`, and on the deleted names above.
- Check: node tools/gate-single-rate-source.js (PASS); node tools/test-ecm-math.js; node tools/test-bas-trends-math.js; node tools/test-pricing-estimator-fixes.js; node tools/test-rate-single-source.js. The gate also fails on `calcBldgDefaultRates` and on any `* inp.elec_rate / gas_rate / demand_rate` (no template has a rate input since phase 5). On a local backup, matrix total and Project Performance total per project are identical before and after.

## Utility E seasons by service date: one season table (2026-10-13)

- Browser only. No schedule, no timer, no env var, no server call.
- ONE season definition: `SEASON_TABLE` (computations/rates.js), keyed by utility + territory (Missouri Metro, Missouri West, Kansas Central, Kansas Metro, and a last row used when the meter names no territory); `SEASON_UTILITY` is the one provider name it tests. Summer = Jun 1 - Sep 30, winter = Oct 1 - May 31. Source: every Utility E general service schedule ("meter reading periods including one or more days in both seasons will reflect the number of days in each season") and the Utility E bill definitions. Readers: `seasonOfDate`, `seasonOfMonth`, `seasonDaysOfPeriod` (same file). A provider with no row has no seasonal tariff (gas).
- `computeSeasonalBldgRates` (rates.js) classifies by SERVICE DATE, not by the bill's month (`billMonth`). A bill that crosses a season edge counts in both seasons, weighted by its days in each. For kWh and kW the season rates come from the bill's own two printed lines per charge (`bill._chargeParts`, `_billSeasonRates(bill, kind, days, provider)`). Which line is summer comes from evidence, never from "higher rate = summer": (1) the printed date range of the line (`from` / `to`, saved by the extractor `xRate` in app/energy-savings.js and `billChargePartsOf` in app/bill-analysis.js, the one builder of `_chargeParts`) when the bill has one: `how = 'label'`; (2) otherwise the line quantities (kW = charge / rate, kWh = qty) against the day split: the assignment within 0.1 of the summer day share, and more than 0.1 better than the other one: `how = 'qty'`. A label must also pass the quantity check. A bill that fails both gives its one blended rate to both seasons, weighted by days (`how = 'days'`). Bills saved before the extractor kept the date range have no label and use the quantities. The result has `straddle: { kwh: {bills, label, qty, days}, kw: {...} }`.
- A provider with no seasonal tariff (gas, propane, or a blank provider name: no season is invented) gets `kwhMonthly`, `kwMonthly`, `gasMonthly`, `gallonMonthly` (12 values: each calendar month's baseline mean, annual mean for a month with no bill). In a building that mixes such a meter with a seasonal meter, the arrays also carry the seasonal meter's bills in their own season months; `gasSummer` = `gasWinter` = the annual mean. `measureRateAt` (app/energy-savings.js) uses the month value when the array is present. `SUMMER_MOS` is deleted; `measureRateAt` and the report engine ask `seasonOfMonth`. `RATE_WRITE_FIELDS` carries the four arrays; a typed flat rate clears the matching array (`writeRateField`).
- Project Performance / Meter Performance is not changed (`resolveMeterRate` uses `seasonOfMonth`, same Jun-Sep result).
- Gate: `node tools/gate-single-rate-source.js` fails on a second `SEASON_TABLE`, a `SUMMER_MOS = [` list, a Jun-Sep month-number list, a month-number range test for a season (`mo >= 6 && mo <= 9`, `mo === 12 || mo <= 3`), or a season edge date ('06-01', '09-30', '10-01', '05-31') outside rates.js. `seasonDaysOfPeriod` counts a service period of any length. Test: `node tools/test-utility-seasons.js` (41 checks, synthetic).
- Not confirmed from a live source: the Kansas Central and Missouri West sheet page numbers. Saved measure rates are not refreshed.

## Rate guard: one check for every rate write (2026-10-13, branch 2026-10-13-phase1-one-rate-cost-only-pricing)

- Browser only. No schedule, no timer, no env var, no server call.
- ROOT CAUSE of the High School "BAS Savings" kW rate 6019.81 ($/kW): the old rate function `calcBldgDefaultRates` (inline in energy-department.html before 2026-05-11, commit d75d41ff) divided the kW charge dollars by the number of BILLS, not by kW. That is dollars per bill. The CSV measure import (measure ids `m_csv_<building id>`) saved those values into `m.rates`. They stayed in the saved data. Two of them (two buildings) were also re-written on every render by the Idempotent restore blocks. The BAS apply (`bcApplyToMeasure`, app/calculators.js) did not write them. It priced with `_svRatesOrCanonical`, which preferred a saved Baseline Rates table over the owner: removed.
- ONE check: `rateGuardCheck(field, value, baselineRates, bldgName)` (computations/rates.js). A rate fails when it is outside the fixed bounds `RATE_GUARD_BOUNDS` ($/kWh 0.01 to 0.25, $/kW 3 to 45, $/therm 0.15 to 2, $/gallon 0.8 to 4) or outside 0.5x to 2x of the building's baseline rate (`computeSeasonalBldgRates`) when that rate exists. Blank and 0 pass. The bounds are half the lowest and twice the highest bill rate in the 2026-10-12 backup: $/kWh 0.0200 to 0.1056 (264 bills), $/kW 6.60 to 22.50 (262 bills), $/therm 0.318 to 0.900 (520 bills), $/gallon 1.60 to 1.94 (only 3 bills, so that bound is wide).
- ONE writer: `writeRateSet` and `writeRateField` (app/energy-savings.js). Typed rates ask first: `rateGuardAccept` / `rateGuardAcceptAll` open the confirm popup (`confirmAsync`) with the value, the baseline rate and the normal range. Cancel saves nothing and the input shows the saved value. Paths: matrix rate cells and the two measure detail panels (`svUpdateMsrRate`), Baseline Rates table (`saveProjBaselineRates`, `_svSaveRatesFrom`), ECM template rate inputs (`_ecmGuardRateInput`, app/ecm-calculators.js; a rate not yet accepted blocks Calculate), report inputs (`wdInputsSetRate`, the setpoint report engine). Program writes are checked and NOT saved when out of range: they are logged to the console and `window._rateGuardLog` and shown in a toast. Paths: Reset rates (`svResetMsrRates`), building change (`updateMsrField`), add measure (`initMeasureRates`: matrix, ECM, solar, BAS), solar and BAS apply (`ensureMeasureRates`), one-time refresh. No import path writes a rate.
- Measure rates follow the owner: `followOwnerMeasureRates` (app/energy-savings.js; runs in `_renderSavingsContent` only; no flag, no schedule). Each measure carries `m.rateSource`: `'owner'` when the program wrote its rates from `computeSeasonalBldgRates` (`writeRateSet` not accepted, `initMeasureRates`, Reset), `'manual'` when the user typed a rate (`writeRateField`) or accepted one from a form (`writeRateSet` accepted). A measure follows the owner unless `rateSource === 'manual'` (`ownerMeasureRates`); there is no id rule and no load-time data fix (`fixMeasureRateSources` is deleted). Every reader prices a measure with `measureRatesNow(projId, m, fallback)` (the matrix rows, footer, quarter views, the project total, the calculators and the Baseline + BAS Savings report), so all views show one number without a render first; `tools/gate-single-rate-source.js` fails on `calcMeasureDollars(m, m.rates)`. An owner measure is rewritten when its rates differ from the owner's current rates, and its `totalDollar` is recomputed. A manual measure is never touched. Totals (`getProjMatrixSavingsTotal`) price an owner measure with `measureRatesNow` (owner rates, no write). Rates the program takes from the bills (every non-accepted `writeRateSet`) get the fixed bounds only (`rateGuardCheckSet(..., absoluteOnly)`), not the 0.5x-2x check: monthly gas legitimately swings. A rate outside the fixed bounds is not saved; `m.rateFlag` {field, value, source} names it, and it is logged (`window._rateGuardLog`) and toasted ONCE. Typed and calculator rates keep the full check. Check: `node tools/test-rate-guard.js` sections 5 and 5b. Utility Data Baseline stat pills (Blended, Summer, Winter $/unit and $/kW) do no rate math: `_udBaselineRatePills(m)` (app/utility-data.js) reads `computeSeasonalBldgRates(projId, bldgId, meterId)` for the meter (optional third argument = one meter), so a pill equals the rate card. All pill labels and values go through `_udBaselinePillsHtml` (app/utility-data.js), which escapes them with `_escHtml`; `tools/test-single-html-escape.js` fails on a raw pill value. Pill units are kWh, Therm, gal (the owner units); gas and propane show the one Blended pill; water, sewer and stormwater have no owner rate and show none. `_udSeasonRateMeans` is deleted. `tools/gate-single-rate-source.js` fails on it and on a cost divided by usage or demand in app/ or computations/. The Sum / Win rate cards (building and meter Stats panes) read `computeSeasonalBldgRates`.
- Contract freeze and savings floor (2026-10-27). (1) `ownerMeasureRates` (app/energy-savings.js) returns null when `projHasContract(projId)` (computations/savings.js, via getProjectContract: sa is set and type is not none), so a contracted project's measures are priced with the rates and quantities saved with the contract; the owner-follow rule above applies only to a project with no contract. Current rates are used only for ACTUAL savings. (2) `billableSavingsWithPresented(projId, yms, perBldg)` (computations/savings.js) is the one function for a savings value to show or bill: each calendar quarter total (`totalSavingsWithPresented`, so a presented quarter keeps its printed figure) is floored at $0 by `floorQuarterSavings`, then added. For all months to date it is the cumulative savings to date (sum of floored quarters; the 60% cap in Agreement 3.1 is for rebates and incentives and does not net a shortfall). Building rows (`byBldg`) and monthly rows stay net. `getProjectSavingsTotal` and the dashboard, Energy Graphics, Utility Data, scorecard and report project totals call it. Runs on every render, no schedule, no env var. Check: `node tools/test-savings-floor-contract-rates.js`. (3) Contract baseline freeze (2026-10-27): for a contracted project each meter's baseline (usage and cost per calendar month, plus the baseline average) is stored once in `m.baseline.contractFrozen` by `freezeContractBaseline` (computations/savings.js, called from `getMeterSavings` and, at load, `freezeContractBaselines` from `loadUtilityData` in app/utility-data.js, which then saves). `buildMoMap` (computations/normalization.js) returns the stored map through `getContractFrozenBaseline`, so a bill edited or added later does not move it; a record for other baseline months is ignored and replaced. A project with no contract still recomputes. Not frozen yet: the kW weather normalization (`computeKwCddRegression`) and degree days when no weather was loaded at freeze time. Check: `node tools/test-contract-baseline-freeze.js`. (4) Contract measure rate inputs (app/energy-savings.js `RATE_LOCK_ATTRS`) are read-only with the hover text "Contract rate - fixed by the agreement"; `svUpdateMsrRate`, `svResetMsrRates` and the building change in `updateMsrField` leave the saved rates alone for `projHasContract`. `quarterMissingBills` costs about 0.2 ms per call on the largest project, so it is not cached.
- Contract freeze (2026-10-27): a project with a contract (`projHasContract`, computations/savings.js) keeps its measure `rates`, `rateSource`, `rateFlag` and `totalDollar` exactly as saved. `ownerMeasureRates`, the building-change write, `svUpdateMsrRate` and `svResetMsrRates` skip it, and `calcProjSavingsMatrix` and `svRecalc` (app/energy-savings.js) show the saved `totalDollar` and do not rewrite it. Check: `node tools/gate-contract-rates-frozen.js` (about 20 s; in the regression gate static list; headless Chromium, synthetic project; runs 14 write paths (the Savings tab, owner follow, building change, rate input, reset, matrix, recalc, writeRateField, writeRateSet, writeMeasureTotal, ensureMeasureRates, solar apply, BAS apply, setpoint options apply) and fails when any field of a contracted measure changes; a no-contract control must change, so the test cannot pass empty). The one rule is `measureRatesFrozen(projId, m)` (app/energy-savings.js): contracted (`projHasContract`) and `totalDollar` not 0. A measure with `totalDollar` 0 is a new measure (every add path creates it so) and may take its own rates and first dollars, then it is frozen too. The lowest writers call it: `writeRateSet`, `writeRateField(..., projId)`, `writeMeasureTotal` (the one program write of `totalDollar`), `initMeasureRates` (rateSource) and `ensureMeasureRates` (the solar and BAS apply stop before they write quantities). `wdApplySetpointOptions` (app/report-engine-woodland.js) skips a frozen option measure. `calcProjSavingsMatrix` and `svRecalc` still skip the whole contracted project. Data already overwritten before v176 is not repaired by code (no server history read exists in the browser).
- Gate: `node tools/gate-rate-write-guard.js` fails on any rate write outside the two writers, a `writeRateField` caller without `rateGuardAccept`, an unmarked `writeRateSet(..., true)`, a rate input handler that does not pass its input, and a measure built with a `rates:` literal. Test: `node tools/test-rate-guard.js` (includes a gate-fails-on-a-new-path check).

## Baseline rates are usage-weighted; mismatched bills are left out (2026-10-13)

- Browser only. No schedule, no timer, no env var, no server call. Cost: about 0.7 ms per `computeSeasonalBldgRates` call (runs the flag engine once per meter, no cache); `getProjMatrixSavingsTotal` for all 5 projects 48 ms on the 2026-10-12 backup.
- Method (`computeSeasonalBldgRates`, computations/rates.js): every derived baseline rate (kWh, kW, therm, gallon, water, sewer, stormwater; per season, per calendar month, annual) = sum of the charges / sum of the usage over the bills in that bucket. It is NOT the mean of per-bill rates. Charge and usage of one bill come from `getBillRateParts(bill, type)` (rates.js), the same function `getStoredRate` and `getStoredKwRate` divide. A straddling Evergy bill with printed split lines gives each season its line rate weighted by that line quantity (`_billSeasonRates` returns `summerQty` / `winterQty`: kWh of the lines, or kW = charge / rate). A straddling bill without split lines gives its usage prorated by its days in each season. A bill with a stored rate but no cost or no usage has no weight and gives nothing. Stormwater has no usage: each bill counts once, so its rate is the mean monthly charge.
- Bills left out: `computeMeterRateExclusions(meter, building)` (computations/bill-flags.js) is the ONE predicate. It returns the baseline-bill ids that `computeMeterFlagSummary` flags with a rule in `BILL_RATE_EXCLUDE_RULES` = `charge_vs_usage` (total vs usage x the meter's usual rate, 3x) or `usage_charge_mismatch` (water and sewer usage charge vs usage, 2x). The thresholds stay in `BILL_FLAG_THRESHOLDS`. A flag the user dismissed does not exclude. The flags use ALL bills of the meter for the usual rate, so a short baseline does not change them. Bill data is never changed. No new per-commodity $/unit check was added: on the 2026-10-12 backup these two rules already flag the water and sewer bills with a wrong usage (one with usage 291 for $313, one with usage 4,303,400 gal for $700).
- `computeSeasonalBldgRates(...).excluded` lists the left-out bills: `{ meterId, commodity, billId, start, end, ym, usage, charge, rules, reason }` (one per bill that would have fed a rate). `formatExcludedBillsLabel(excluded)` (rates.js) writes "2 bills excluded: Nov 2025, Dec 2025". Utility Data > Baseline stat pills show it as a "Left out of the rates above" pill next to the rates (`_udBaselineRatePills`, both pill strips in app/utility-data.js).
- Effect on the 2026-10-12 backup: sewer and water rates fall from a 10x-too-high value to about $0.01 per gal; electric kWh and kW rates move by under 1.5% on the buildings checked; a gas annual $/therm rate rises 8% to 28% where usage is winter-heavy (weighted by therms), and the month-by-month gas array does not change when each month has one bill. Project Performance is identical before and after; the Energy Savings matrix total moves under 0.1%.
- Gate: `node tools/gate-single-rate-source.js` fails on any read of a measure's `.rates` (also destructured `rates`, `['rates']`, `'rates'` name) outside `measureRatesNow`, `writeRateSet` and `writeRateField`. A read that is not pricing is marked `// rates-ok`.
- Check: `node tools/test-rate-usage-weighted.js` (30 checks, synthetic); `node tools/test-utility-seasons.js`; `node tools/test-bill-flag-rules.js`; `node tools/gate-single-rate-source.js`. The list of excluded bills on a local backup: run `computeSeasonalBldgRates(projId, bldgId, meterId).excluded` for every meter.
- Month rate (2026-10-13): savings per meter per calendar month use that month's own bill rate. When a month holds several bills, `getMonthBillRate(bills, type)` (computations/rates.js, type kwh, kw or gas) returns sum of charges / sum of usage from `getBillRateParts`; it is not the mean of the per-bill rates. A bill with no charge or no usage is left out; with none computable the first bill's stored rate is used. Callers: `getMeterSavings` (computations/savings.js, electric kWh, kW and gas) and the Meter Performance demand and gas cost maps (app/utility-data.js, `perfDemByYm`, `gasCostByYm`). `tools/gate-single-rate-source.js` fails on a `.reduce(... + rate ...) / rates.length` mean. Check: `node tools/test-rate-usage-weighted.js`; `node tools/gate-single-rate-source.js`.

## Physics constants and the one fan power rule (2026-10-13, branch 2026-10-13-phase2-fan-affinity-constants)
- ONE home: `computations/physics.js` (loaded first of the computations files in energy-department.html). No schedule, no env var, no host behavior. Three base figures, every other factor is derived from one of them: `BTU_PER_KWH` 3412.14 (EIA), `BTU_PER_GAL_PROPANE` 91452 (EIA), `BTU_PER_HR_PER_BHP` 33475 (boiler horsepower); plus `BTU_PER_THERM` 100000, `KW_PER_HP` 0.746, `FAN_AFFINITY_EXPONENT` 3. Derived: `KBTU_PER_KWH`, `THERMS_PER_KWH`, `KWH_PER_MMBTU` (293.071), `KWH_PER_BTU`, `THERMS_PER_BTU`, `THERMS_PER_MMBTU`, `THERMS_PER_GAL_PROPANE` (0.91452), `KBTU_PER_GAL_PROPANE` (91.452), `THERMS_PER_HR_PER_BHP` (0.33475). Sources: https://www.eia.gov/energyexplained/units-and-calculators/british-thermal-units.php. Functions: `hpToKw`, `fanPowerFraction`, `ductStaticResetPowerSavings`.
- Propane is ONE figure (2026-10-13, Matt decision): 91,452 Btu per gallon (EIA). Bill conversion (gallons to therms, 0.91452) and the EUI factor (91.452 kBtu per gallon) both come from `BTU_PER_GAL_PROPANE`. They were 0.9153 and 91.5 (two sources). Effect: gallons-to-therms is 0.085 percent lower; propane EUI is 0.05 percent lower.
- Fan rule: power fraction = fixedShare + (1 - fixedShare) x max(speed, minSpeed) ^ exponent. Defaults: exponent 3, minSpeed 0, fixedShare 0 (ideal cube law). Every caller used the pure cube law with no floor, so no value changed. Callers: `vfd_savings`, `pump_vfd`, `vav_reheat`, `fan_wall` (app/ecm-calculators.js) and the duct static pressure reset estimate in BAS Trends (`btEstimateSavings`, app/bas-trends.js). Duct static reset: speed = sqrt(1 - pressure reduction), saving = 1 - fanPowerFraction(speed). A 20 percent pressure cut saves 28.4 percent of fan power with no fixed losses (the audit text said 49 percent; the code gives 28 percent). The fan model and the BAS report use a 10 percent fixed-loss share (`FAN_FIXED_POWER_SHARE`): 25.6 percent, ONE answer through `fanResetSavingsFraction(0.2)` (computations/fan-savings.js). 2026-10-14 review fixes: `fanPowerFraction` takes `opts.curve`: 'affinity' (default, as above) or 'ashraeG', the ASHRAE 90.1 Appendix G part-load fan power curve of a variable-air-volume fan with a drive and fixed duct static pressure: 0.0013 + 0.1470 x PLR + 0.9506 x PLR^2 - 0.0998 x PLR^3 (`FAN_CURVE_ASHRAE_G`; source: NREL openstudio-standards, lib/openstudio-standards/hvac/components/fan.rb, `fan_variable_volume_set_control_type`, 'Multi Zone VAV with Fixed Static Pressure Setpoint', Appendix G baseline of ANSI/ASHRAE/IES 90.1-2016, read 2026-10-14). The fan model default is 'ashraeG' (`FAN_DEFAULT_PART_LOAD_CURVE`): its power is at or above the affinity curve with 10 percent fixed share from 30 to 99 percent flow, so it gives the lower savings. Check: `node tools/test-fan-savings.js` section 7c. The pricing estimator no longer computes savings, so it holds no third copy.
- Value change: the Calculators gas share of a kWh heat calibration used 0.03413; it now uses `THERMS_PER_KWH` (0.03412), a 0.03 percent change in that share (app/calculators.js, the pctGasHeat line).
- Removed duplicate: the `toKBtu` fallback at the top of app/report-engine.js (computations/rates.js always loads first).
- One site-energy sum: `computeKBtu(kwh, therms, propaneGal)` (computations/eui.js, accepts numeric strings). `toKBtu` (computations/rates.js) is deleted; every caller in app/ now calls `computeKBtu`. `UNIT_TO_BASE` Btu and MMBtu factors (app/utility-data.js) come from `KWH_PER_BTU`, `KWH_PER_MMBTU`, `THERMS_PER_BTU`, `THERMS_PER_MMBTU`. `_gasMaxThermHr` (app/calculators.js, HVAC Load Estimate, Reverse Analysis, Gas tab) uses `THERMS_PER_HR_PER_BHP` (0.33475); it used 0.03346, 10 times too low, so boiler horsepower equipment now shows 10 times more therms per hour (100 BHp: 3.346 to 33.475). The `lbs/hr` factor was 0.001, about 10 times low. It now uses `THERMS_PER_LB_STEAM` (0.0097029, from 33,475 Btu/h per boiler hp / 34.5 lb/h per boiler hp). `UNIT_TO_BASE.DTh` comes from `THERMS_PER_MMBTU`. ECM formula labels (app/ecm-calculators.js) build the Btu-per-therm and Btu-per-kWh text from `BTU_PER_THERM` and `BTU_PER_KWH`. `UNIT_TO_BASE.MLbs` (10) is a separate steam factor and is not changed.
- Gate: `node tools/gate-single-physics.js` (in the regression gate static group). It fails on `Math.pow(x, 3)`, `x ** 3`, `x * x * x`, `Math.sqrt(1 - ...)`, a second definition of the three functions, any hardcoded copy of a figure above (0.746, 0.7457, 3412, 3412.14, 3413, 3.412, 3.41214, 3.413, 0.03412, 0.0341214, 0.03413, 293.071, 0.000293071, 0.9153, 0.91452, 91.5, 91.452, 91452, 33475, 0.33475, 0.03346, or 100000 as a Btu-per-therm divisor, the label text "100,000", "3,412" or "3,412.14", or a typed DTh/MMBtu/BTU factor in UNIT_TO_BASE), a base constant in physics.js that is not the one literal or a derived figure that is not computed from it, any use of `toKBtu`, or a second `computeKBtu`, in app/, computations/, extraction/, lib/, netlify/ or a root file. Test and gate files are exempt. Test: `node tools/test-physics-single-source.js` (56 checks, includes a gate-fails-on-a-second-copy check for each rule). Tests that load app files in a vm must load computations/physics.js first, and computations/eui.js when they call a function that uses `computeKBtu`.

### District steam unit (2026-10-13)
- `computations/physics.js`: `BTU_PER_LB_DISTRICT_STEAM = 1194` (ENERGY STAR Portfolio Manager Thermal Energy Conversions: 1 lb district steam = 1.194 kBtu) and `THERMS_PER_THOUSAND_LB_DISTRICT_STEAM` (11.94). This is delivered-steam energy. It is not `BTU_PER_LB_STEAM` (970.3, boiler output latent heat, used by `_gasMaxThermHr`). Keep both.
- `UNIT_TO_BASE.Mlb` in app/utility-data.js uses the 11.94 constant. Mlb means thousand pounds (M = 1,000, as in MCF). The old key `MLbs` (factor 10) is removed. Menus show "thousand lb (Mlb)" through `unitLabel()` (app/core.js meter edit, app/csv-import.js meter dialog).
- Check: `node tools/gate-single-physics.js` fails on a typed number in the Mlb factor, 1194 or 11.94 outside physics.js.

### HVAC Load Estimate: quick annual entry and one data resolver (2026-10-13)
- Entry: HVAC Load Estimate tab, "Data Source" box (`_hvlRenderSourceCard`, app/calculators.js). The user types annual kWh, annual Therms, optional propane gallons, optional peak kW and the year. `hvacQuickAnnualSave` stores it on the building as `b.quickAnnual = {year, kwh, therms, propaneGal, peakKw}` (one place; saved with the Utility Data building through `saveUtilityData(projId)`). `hvacQuickAnnualClear` deletes it. `getHvacQuickAnnual(b)` is the only reader.
- Resolver: `resolveHvacLoadData(projId, b)` (app/calculators.js) is the only function the HVAC Load Estimate reads data through (`computeHvacLoadEstimate`, `hvacLoadCalc` and `_hvlBuildExportData`). Order: baseline bills (full baseline = `computeHvacEnduse` says 6 or more populated months; fewer bills still win when no annual entry exists) -> annual entry -> nothing. It returns `source` ('bills', 'annual', 'none') and a plain label ("From 12 months of baseline bills", "From annual totals you entered (2025)"). When bills win, the entry is kept and shown as not used.
- Annual path: the same Rules of Thumb percentages (45% of kWh, 55% of peak kW, 80% or 15% of Therms; editable on the tab) are applied to the annual totals. No degree-day model is built from annual data. No monthly arrays are built: the estimate's `monthly` is false, the monthly arrays are null, the results table and the Excel/Word/PDF exports skip the monthly and reconciliation sections.
- Check: `node tools/test-hvac-quick-annual.js` (resolver order, annual path, and a static gate that `hvacLoadCalc` and the export read no bill data and that `quickAnnual` appears only in the resolver, its reader and the entry box). It runs in the regression gate by glob. Run time: on tab open and on every edit (no timer, no network).

### HVAC Load Estimate per building, one data path, one demand function (2026-10-13, phase 4 foundation)
- Storage: the estimate is stored on the BUILDING, `b.hvacLoad = { savedAt, est, inputs }` (app/calculators.js `hvacLoadSave`, saved with the building through `saveUtilityData(projId)`; a building is shared by every project of the customer). Only the Save button writes it. A live calculation is kept in memory (`_hvlLive`) and writes nothing, so the last building calculated can no longer overwrite another one. `hvacLoadStoredFor(projId, bldgId)` is the only reader. The project roll-up `hvacLoadProjectRollup(projId)` adds up the buildings' estimates every time and is never stored; the tab shows it in one line ("Project total: N of M buildings have an estimate").
- Data move: `migrateHvacLoadToBuildings` (app/calculators.js) runs at the end of `loadUtilityData` (app/utility-data.js), on every page load, and acts only on a project that still has `hvacLoadEst`, `hvacLoadSavedAt` or `hvacLoadInputs`. The old estimate never recorded its building, so each building of that project with utility data is calculated again from the resolver (`computeHvacLoadEstimate`) with its own saved inputs, else the Rules of Thumb start values (`_hvlThumbDefaults`). Saved inputs move onto the building. The three project fields are deleted. A building whose meters are all excluded from the baseline gets no estimate. It writes a console line `[HVAC load move]` with one row per building. It also keeps typed percentages: `_hvlKeepTypedPercents` sets `kwhPctOverride`, `kwPctOverride` and `gasPctOverride` to true for a saved value that differs from the default `_hvlThumbDefaults` gives now (and to false for one that equals it), for the moved inputs and for buildings an earlier version already moved whose thumb inputs have a value but no flag; the console row says `typed percentage kept: <fields>`. A second run finds no unflagged value and does nothing. No flag (self-deactivating), no sync-classification entry. Check: open the app on a browser that holds an older backup, then look at the console line and at the tab.
- One data path: `resolveHvacLoadData` is the only reader of bills for HVAC work. It adds every INCLUDED meter of a commodity (`isBaselineExcluded`, by `m.id`; the old tab used only the first meter and ignored exclusions). A month no bill falls in is `null`, not 0 (buildMoMap puts a whole bill in the month `billMonth` gives it). It returns the 12-month arrays `kwhArr`, `kwArr`, `facArr`, `gasArr` (gas plus propane at `THERMS_PER_GAL_PROPANE`), `billRates`, `enduse` (`computeHvacEnduse`) and `demand`. Readers moved onto it: `_hvlGasHeatShare` and `hvacComputeElecCoolKwhForBuilding` (BAS Calc autofill), the Energy Graphics HVAC End-Use card (app/graphics-setpoints.js), `_wdBaselineHvac` (Baseline + BAS Savings report page 4 and the setpoint options; its signature is now `(projId, b)`, and so are `_wdComputeHvacSplit` and `wdComputeSetpointOptions`) and the Reverse Utility Analysis actuals. `_hvlEnduseForBuilding` is deleted. `_hvlMonthlyBaseline` is called only by the resolver and the Baseline Data table. Follow-up (2026-10-13): (1) superseded 2026-10-14 (phase 5): the BAS Calc cooling kWh, heating kWh and gas therms autofill read `getEndUse` only (the `useSavedEst` fork and `hvacCompute*ForBuilding` are deleted). (2) `_hvlMeterMoMap` marks a month as billed with `billMonth` (computations/normalization.js); it has no month rule of its own. (3) A propane month with 0 gallons is `null` in `gasArr`, never a real 0 in the 3-lowest-month baseload. (4) The setpoint report gate (`wdCheckReportInputs`, `wdUpdateReportButton`) reads `_wdHvacFullYear`, which uses the resolver (all included meters, exclusions honored; 12 months with a bill). `_wdBldgBaselines` is gone; `_wdRawBillMeters` (first Electric and first Gas meter) feeds only the report's raw bill pages. (5) The static gate in tools/test-hvac-per-building.js (section 5g) fails when an HVAC, baseline or setpoint function in the report engine or Energy Graphics file reads bills outside the resolver. Listed exceptions: the raw bill pages (`_wdBuildMeterBaseline`, `_wdRawBillMeters` and the report data collector) print one account's bills as they are on paper and compute no baseline value; the Baseline Data table (`_buildBaselineDataHtml`) shows weather-normalized kWh and Therms, a different number by design, from the same month maps (`_hvlMonthlyBaseline`). Check: `node tools/test-hvac-per-building.js`.
- Demand: `computeHvacDemand(billedKw12, facKw12, billKwRecords)` (computations/hvac-enduse.js) returns monthly billed kW, its SUM (the demand-charge baseline) and its PEAK with the month, monthly Facilities kW, its sum and highest value, and `facSetYm`, the month of the bill that set the highest Facilities kW. `_hvacPeakOf` is the one place the peak is decided (`computeHvacEnduse` uses it too). Facilities kW follows the highest billed kW of the last 12 months: on the 2026-10-12 backup 141 of 151 bills with a full prior year match the rule exactly (the rest differ at the edge of the window). Consumers: the HVAC Load Estimate uses the monthly series and its SUM (savings are priced every month) and shows both bases plus Facilities kW in the "Electric Demand" table; the setpoint options use the monthly series (month by month, summer months); the Energy Graphics card uses the PEAK (cooling demand = peak minus winter base); the annual path has only the entered peak.
- Check: `node tools/test-hvac-per-building.js` (129 checks: two buildings in turn, roll-up, resolver, demand, data move, static gates that no code reads the old project fields, bills are read only by `_hvlMeterMoMap` and `_hvlMonthlyBaseline`, and every HVAC reader goes through the resolver). It runs in the regression gate by glob.
### Calc templates take their baseline from the HVAC Load Estimate and send monthly quantities (2026-10-14, phase 5)
- Reader: `getEndUse(projId, bldgId, endUse)` (computations/end-use.js:86) is the ONE function that answers "how much cooling kWh, heating Therms, HVAC kWh does this building use". End uses today: `cooling`, `heatingElectric`, `heatingGas`, `hvacElectric` (cooling + electric heating, fans and pumps are inside it) and `nonHvacElectric` (lighting, plug and other together). `fans`, `lighting` and `plug` answer `modeled:false` (not modeled separately yet). It returns annual and monthly (Jan..Dec, null for annual-only data: no month is ever made up), the kW of the same end use, and a source: `source` ('bills', 'annual', 'none'), `estimate` ('saved' = a person saved inputs for this building, 'default' = nobody did), `basis` ('measured', 'rule', 'override', 'method') and a plain `sourceLabel` that names the method. It asks `hvacEstimateForBuilding(projId, bldgId)` (app/calculators.js:167), which calculates the estimate NOW from `resolveHvacLoadData` and the building's saved inputs; it never reads the stored snapshot `b.hvacLoad.est` (a moved-over or old copy must not freeze a split over the bills; the project roll-up uses the same function). Method rule (2026-10-14): with monthly baseline bills the end-use split (cooling, electric heating, gas heating, HVAC total, non-HVAC) is the MEASURED 3-lowest-month baseload split (`computeHvacEnduse`, computations/hvac-enduse.js, carried by the resolver's `enduse`): HVAC kWh = bills above the base load, months add up to the annual number. Rules of Thumb percentages apply only to annual-only data (quick entry) or when a person types a percentage over the shown default (saved as `kwhPctOverride`, `gasPctOverride`, `kwPctOverride` in `b.hvacLoad.inputs.thumb`; a saved value that is only the shown default is not restored). The electric heating share and the demand (kW) split stay rules of thumb and are labelled so. `computeHvacLoadEstimate` returns `est.basis` (per number) and the tab, the Excel/Word/PDF export and every template hint print it in plain words (`hvlBasisLabel`). No other file may read the estimate's end-use fields (`coolKwhTotal`, `monthlyHvacGas`, ...): `tools/gate-ecm-templates-no-dollars.js` fails on it.
- Monthly cooling and electric heating (2026-10-14): with monthly bills each end use has its OWN measured profile from `computeHvacMonthlyProfiles` (computations/hvac-enduse.js): the month's bill above the base load goes to heating when the month's heating degree days exceed its cooling degree days (`resolved.hddArr`/`cddArr`), else to cooling; a month without weather uses the calendar (Oct to Apr = heating). Each profile is scaled to that end use's annual total, so cooling has no January. `hvacElectric` monthly stays the measured bill above the base load. Check: `node tools/test-hvac-per-building.js` (section 4a). kW templates: `kw` fields are PEAK kW; the month value is peak kW x month weight / highest month weight.
- Shaping: `getEndUseWeights` (end-use.js:155) gives the month weights (monthly / sum) of the first listed end use that has a positive monthly profile. `spreadEnergyByWeights` lays a yearly kWh or Therms over them; `spreadDemandByWeights` lays a kW reduction over them (full in the end use's peak month, in proportion in the others). Annual-only data has no profile, so no measure can be built from it (plain message).
- Templates (app/ecm-calculators.js): a template (`ECM_TEMPLATES`) computes yearly QUANTITIES only. Gone: every dollar and payback output, `ecmPrice`, the rate inputs (elec, gas, demand, btm, nm), the cost inputs (repair, equipment, vfd, installed, install, incr, total, per watt, ITC) and the net-metering switch of Solar Production. `ECM_MEASURE_FIELD_MAP` (ecm-calculators.js:1983) maps each result field to a matrix column (kwh, gas, kw) and to the end use that shapes it. `ecmBuildMeasure` (2074) builds the monthly measure; `ecmMeasureDollars` (2140) prices it with `calcMeasureDollars` + `measureRatesNow` (baseline-period rates, month by month), the only dollar a template shows ("Savings value"); `ecmAddAsMeasure` (2154) pushes the measure into the Energy Savings matrix (no more annual / 12; no building = no measure). `getProjectEcmTotal` (1892, the dashboard "ECM Projected" line) prices the saved ECM records the same way. Payback and cost lines: see "Measure cost lines and ONE payback" (phase 7).
- Prefill rule: ONE function, `endUseFill(eu)` (computations/end-use.js): fills only when the end use is modeled, has data and its annual quantity is above 0. The BAS Calc (`openBASCalc`) and the ECM form (`renderEcmCalculator`) both call it.
- Prefill: a template input with `endUse` (today RTU Replacement: existing cooling kWh = `cooling`, existing heating Therms = `heatingGas`) is filled by `renderEcmCalculator` (2221) from `getEndUse` through the shared `chResolveCalcField` rule (app/calc-autofill.js): a typed value stays typed, then the load estimate (marker "from HVAC Load Estimate (...), <data source>"), then the shipped default flagged "not from building data". The form has a Building choice; changing the building refills the untouched inputs. BAS Calc Section D (cooling kWh, heating kWh, gas Therms) in `openBASCalc` (app/calculators.js:4294) reads `getEndUse` only: the saved-versus-measured fork, `hvacComputeGasThermsForBuilding` and `hvacComputeElecCoolKwhForBuilding` are deleted.
- Not modeled yet: lighting and plug loads as separate end uses (lighting templates are shaped by `nonHvacElectric`). Fans are modeled since phase 6 (next section).
- Runs: on opening a template and on Calculate; no timer, no network. Time zone: none.
- Check: `node tools/test-end-use.js` (getEndUse sources and basis, weights, template map, gate fails on a copy), `node tools/test-ecm-math.js`, `node tools/gate-ecm-templates-no-dollars.js` (in the regression gate static group): fails on a dollar, payback, rate or cost input or divide by 12 inside a template, on a second reader of the estimate end-use fields, or on a return of the BAS fork.
- Solar Production (2026-10-14): `ECM_MEASURE_FIELD_MAP.solar_production` has `monthlyFrom: 'solar'`; `ecmBuildMeasure` takes the month weights from `solarMonthlyWeights(projId)` (app/calculators.js), which reads `solarMonthlyProduction(p, manual)`, the ONE owner of the Solar calculator's monthly production (sum of the array rows, else the manual row). The same function feeds the Solar calculation, Add as Measure and Apply to Measure. With no monthly production in the Solar calculator the button stays off and the message says so.
- Escaping (2026-10-14): every value from a building, bill, meter, measure or typed input in `renderEcmCalculator` and `renderEcmResults` (app/ecm-calculators.js) goes through `_escHtml`; `chCalcFieldHintHTML` (app/calc-autofill.js) escapes its hint (source labels). `node tools/test-single-html-escape.js` (section 5) renders both functions with a hostile building name, source label and typed value and fails on markup or an attribute break-out.
### Fan end use and the Fan Savings template (2026-10-14, phase 6)
- What it is: `getEndUse(projId, bldgId, 'fans')` (computations/end-use.js `_fanEndUse`) answers the building's supply-fan kWh from its Equipment Matrix rows (equipment types ahu, rtu, doas, mau, fan that have a fan point: a supply fan status, speed, amps or airflow point, a High/Low enable pair, or any point named "fan" (`_fanRowHasFanPoint`); duct, damper, water detection and zone sensor rows of an AHU are not fans; the rows of the building by `_emNormBldgNameForJoin`). It has the same shape as every other end use, with `source: 'matrix'`, plus `units` (one model per row), `capFactor`, `capAnnual` and `levels`. It runs when a template opens or calculates; no timer, no network, no new storage key.
- Unit model: ONE function, `fanUnitModel` (computations/fan-savings.js). Fan kW: trend running amps (else the matrix amps point, else the amps field) x the voltage and phase asset fields (`motorKwFromAmps`, physics.js), else supply fan airflow x W per cfm (`fanKwFromCfm`; `FAN_W_PER_CFM` low 0.774, mid 0.851, high 0.928; mid is used), else horsepower (the Horsepower/Tons column is read as HP for ahu, doas and mau only; motor efficiency 0.9, load 0.8), else amps with an ASSUMED 208 V three phase (the low end of common supply voltages, so a missing voltage never overstates; labelled default). A unit with none of these gets a stated Default estimate from `fanModelUnits` (computations/fan-savings.js), the first rule that applies: (a) the median kW of the sized units of the same equipment type in the same building, (b) the same type across the other buildings of the project, (c) NOT available: the Equipment Matrix has no link from a VAV box to its AHU, (d) the national average ventilation share of the building's TOTAL baseline electricity (`fanVentilationShare` and table `FAN_VENTILATION_SHARE_CBECS`, computations/physics.js; source EIA CBECS 2018 Table E5, https://www.eia.gov/consumption/commercial/data/2018/ce/xls/e5.xlsx, billion kWh: Education ventilation 19 of 128 total = 14.8 percent, used when the building's own type, else (when the building has none) the project type, names a school; the label says which type was used; All buildings 213 of 1,196 = 17.8 percent, used otherwise), less the fans already sized, split across the units still unsized by tons when the matrix row has them (a unit without tons takes the mean of the others), else equally (kWh per sq ft shown; label "Default: national average ventilation share for <type> buildings (EIA CBECS 2018)"). Each is tagged Default with its basis in `kwHow`; the unit model carries `estimate` ('sibling', 'project', 'area'). With no sibling and no total baseline electricity the unit stays not sized and the template says why. Run hours: BAS trend run hours (fan status, else High/Low enable, else amps, else speed) x 365; else a matrix read with the fan on while the schedule point says unoccupied (8,760 h); else 3,640 h. Speed drive: a speed trend that moves means a drive; else the unit field Has variable speed (yes/no/unknown; Equipment Matrix column `hasVfd`, stored once in `data.edits['<rowId>::hasVfd']` by `emSetAssetEdit`, app/equipment-matrix.js, and set per unit in the template picker); else any point of the unit named like a drive is evidence of a drive: `fanDrivePointName` (computations/fan-savings.js, the one rule) takes the first point name with VFD, VSD, Drive, Inverter, Hz, Frequency or Speed, except a High/Low speed enable, wind or air speed, a diagnostic, an energy recovery wheel, and a point of another fan (exhaust, return, relief, condenser, compressor, pump); the model says "Drive found from point: <point name>" (data level: Equipment Matrix), and Has variable speed = No set by the user wins over it. No evidence means the drive status is UNKNOWN (not assumed constant speed). `driveStatus`: 'has', 'none' (Has variable speed: No, or a speed trend that stays in one range) or 'unknown'. A unit with a drive and no moving speed trend has no speed profile and counts at its full-speed power (a speed trend that moves gives the profile and the part-load power). Unit fields (voltage, phase, amps, HP, Has variable speed) are read from `data.edits` of the matrix record (an earlier read of the key `en_eqmatrix_edits_<projId>` missed every user edit). Two-speed fan: a High/Low enable trend, or both enable point names in the matrix row; Low share from the enable hours, else 55%. Every value carries a data level: `trend` (measured trend), `matrix` (Equipment Matrix value) or `default` (stated assumption, constants at the top of fan-savings.js). A better level replaces a worse one on its own when data arrives.
- Cap and months: fans run all year, so their energy sits in the bills' baseload, not in the HVAC electric above baseload. The sum of the sized units is capped by the building's total baseline electricity (`_euTotalElectric`, computations/end-use.js, the HVAC Load Estimate `totalKwh`) less its measured cooling and electric heating end uses: `capFactor = cap / sum` when the sum is larger, else 1; with no total there is no cap and the label says so. Double-count rule: `getEndUse(.., 'nonHvacElectric')` is that same remainder LESS the fans (annual, 12 months and kW series, never below 0; its source label says so), so cooling + electric heating + non-HVAC + fans never add up to more than the building total. `hvacElectric` is unchanged (cooling + electric heating). Monthly = annual x days in month / 365 (a fan runs all year), so the Energy Savings measure for a fan saving is shaped by days in month, not by cooling. The estimated units are inside the sum, so the cap applies to them too.
- Template: `ECM_TEMPLATES.fan_savings` (app/ecm-calculators.js, category HVAC Equipment). Quantities only (no cost, no payback). Inputs: the unit picker (type `fan_units`, drawn by `ecmFanUnitsFieldHtml` with the data level of each input and "what would refine this estimate"), three measure switches, the fan power curve (default ASHRAE 90.1 Appendix G), the static pressure cut (default 20 percent), and a "Size is right" tick on each unit sized by the national average. COUNTED vs POSSIBLE (2026-10-14 review fixes): a drive or a two-speed replacement is counted in `savings_kwh` (and so in Add as Measure) only when the unit's drive status is known "none"; units of unknown drive status show `possible_drive_kwh` ("Possible if no drive is installed ... confirm drive status to include") and a unit sized by the national average shows `possible_size_kwh` until its Size is right box is ticked; neither is in the total. Measures from `fanSavingsForUnits`: (a) drive or efficient motor on a constant-speed fan (fan kW x hours x the mid of a published range: `FAN_VFD_SAVINGS_RANGE` low 25, mid 35, high 45 percent, "25-45% reduction compared to constant-volume belt-drive systems" from the ASHRAE Handbook fan chapter, the PNNL plug fan study and ENERGY STAR fan guidance; the template shows low, mid and high, counts the mid; label `FAN_VFD_SAVINGS_LABEL` "Default: typical savings range for adding variable speed to a constant-volume fan, 25-45% (ASHRAE Handbook fan chapter; PNNL plug fan study)"; the drive-name rule `fanDrivePointName` also serves the Audit Estimate "Fan speed/VFD" control concept (app/audit-estimate.js), and reads camelCase and underscore keys as words; the monthly HVAC load profile `fanSpeedProfile` and the lowest airflow input were removed because no source stood under them: they gave 74 percent on one building, above published ranges; a unit with a moving speed trend already has a drive and gets no drive saving), (b) drive in place of a two-speed motor (High hours only: Low hours stay at Low speed; mean power over Low to High), (c) duct static pressure reset on a fan that has a drive (`ductStaticResetPowerSavings`; share of fans it works on: setpoint trend flat = 1, moving = 0, no trend = 70 percent). `calculateEcm(templateId, inputs, ctx)` now passes `{projId, buildingId}` to `calculate(inp, ctx)`. Add as Measure sends `savings_kwh` and `savings_kw` shaped by the fan end use (`ECM_MEASURE_FIELD_MAP.fan_savings`); dollars come from `calcMeasureDollars` at baseline-period rates. A projected estimate, never called measured or verified.
- Trend import (app/bas-trends.js): new point types `fanhienable`, `fanloenable`, `fanamps` (plus the existing `fanstatus`, `fanspeed`, `staticp`, `staticpsp`), matched by point name. `btFanPointExtras` adds small per-day numbers to the existing daily summary in `en_bas_<projId>` (run hours; amps running mean; hours and mean speed in four speed bins): no raw rows are added. MEASURED size (synthetic file, `node tools/test-fan-savings.js` section 7c): one unit, 365 days, six fan points (status, amps, speed, static setpoint, High enable, Low enable) = 376,346 characters (368 KB) of `en_bas_<projId>`. `btCheckStorageSize` counts 2 bytes per character, so one such unit-year counts 752,692 bytes and the 3 MB console warning (`btWarnIfStorageLarge`) is reached after about 4 unit-years of full fan points (about 8 by character count). `btMergeSummaries` adds a point type that an old day lacks and, on a fan point type the day already has (fanstatus, fanspeed, fanamps, fanhienable, fanloenable), the fan facts it lacks (run hours, mean amps, speed bins), so a re-import upgrades old days; it never overwrites a stored value. The trend is joined to the matrix row by building and equipment tag (normalized name). Fixed in the same change: `btImportCSV` typed each row twice and threw a TypeError in `computeSummaries`; it now passes the raw rows to `btComputeOneDaySummary`.
- BAS Trends report (2026-10-14 review fixes): `btEstimateSavings` (app/bas-trends.js) takes fan kW from `fanKwFromHp` (nameplate HP, motor efficiency, load 0.8), run hours from `fanDefaultRunHours` (3,640 h, was 2,400 h) and the duct static reset fraction from `fanResetSavingsFraction` (25.6 percent for a 20 percent cut, was 28.4 percent). Effect on that report: the assumed fan kW per air handler is 3.28 kW (was 4.10), so the SAT reset and after-hours estimates fall about 20 percent and the DSP reset estimate changes by the hours and fraction. Fan rule gate: a second HP-to-kW, run-hours number or reset call outside the owners fails `tools/gate-single-fan-model.js`.
- Plain limits: the speed is read as a fraction (a maximum above 1.5 is taken as percent; Hz is not converted); run hours from a short trend are scaled to a year; the estimate cannot see a drive that has no speed point; Hi/Lo units are two-speed only when both enable names exist.
- Gates: `node tools/gate-single-fan-model.js` (in the regression gate static group) fails on a second fan run-hours rule, a second unit model, a direct read of the fan units, the fan constants outside their owners, fan math inside the template, a fan kW from HP (`hpToKw` on a fan line), a `ductStaticResetPowerSavings` call, or a fan run-hours number outside the owners, or when the BAS report stops calling the fan owner. `node tools/gate-single-physics.js` also bans `Math.sqrt(3)` and the W per cfm figures outside physics.js. Test: `node tools/test-fan-savings.js`.

### Equipment Matrix Counts view and estimated HP (2026-11-04, item 6715c224)
- What it is: Equipment Matrix tab > `Counts` button beside Summary (`emSetCountsView`, `emRenderCountsView`, app/equipment-matrix.js; view mode `counts`). Two tables count EQUIPMENT rows, not motors, phantom rows left out (`emIsPhantomRow`): by type and by building. Columns: motor speed (variable, single, not known), carbon dioxide sensor yes/no, humidity sensor yes/no, estimated HP. Totals row, hover text on every header, a dash where a column does not apply. A click on a count opens an inline list with the point that proves it; a click on a name opens the equipment details; a click on a building sets the building filter. No timer, no env var, no stored data, no network.
- Per-row answers: ONE function, `emRowFeatures(row, projId, edits)` (computations/equipment-features.js, loaded by energy-department.html after fan-savings.js). It also owns the concept list `EM_FEATURE_CONCEPTS` (moved out of app/audit-estimate.js; the Audit Estimate sample groups call `emRowFeatures` and keep only the concepts flagged `audit`, so its group counts do not move; `Humidity` is Counts-only). Motor speed: a person's Has variable speed answer wins, else a drive point from `fanDrivePointName(names, equipName, 'motor')` (computations/fan-savings.js; the default kind is unchanged for the fan savings), else a fan or pump point means single speed, else not known (a refrigerant-flow unit is not known). A bare Fan Speed point, a selector or a manual speed point is not a drive.
- Estimated HP: `fanEstimateHp` (computations/fan-savings.js) takes the first source that applies: supply fan airflow divided by the speed fraction (speed 35 percent or more; a fixed speed fan with no speed point is full speed) through `fanHpFromCfm`; else the drive kW point (`supplyFanPower` column) scaled to full speed; else the amps of a single speed motor through `motorKwFromAmps` at `FAN_HP_AMPS_VOLTS` 460 V, `FAN_HP_AMPS_PHASE` 3 and power factor 0.85. VFD amps are never converted. A reading of 0, or amps under `FAN_HP_MIN_AMPS` 0.5, is not known. `fanHpFromKw` is the one inverse of `fanKwFromHp`; it rounds UP to a standard size (`FAN_STANDARD_HP_SIZES`). It is an estimate, never a nameplate value. The 460 V is for this estimate only: `fanUnitModel` keeps `FAN_DEFAULT_VOLTS` 208 for the fan savings (unchanged; see the 208 V note in the item 6715c224 report).
- The three HP-only points (DOAS supply fan airflow, drive kW, exhaust fan amps) are read from the raw point names in `emRowFeatures` (`EM_HP_RAW_POINTS`), NOT mapped to Equipment Matrix columns. A new column would change the ASHRAE 36 scored/additional counter of the Audit View; test-audit-point-totals.js guards it.
- Check: `node tools/test-equipment-features.js` (unit), `node test-equipment-matrix-counts-dom.js` (headless browser, synthetic rows, needs `CH_TMP_ROOT`), `node tools/gate-single-fan-model.js` (no second drive-name rule), `node test-audit-concept-groups.js` (audit groups unchanged). On a real project backup, the Counts type total equals the number of non-phantom rows in the Raw View header.

### Measure cost lines and ONE payback (2026-10-14, phase 7)
- What it is: each Energy Savings matrix measure holds its cost as `m.costLines` (stored with the measure in `en_projects`, `savingsData.measures`). A line is `{id, kind, label, vendor, hours, amount, note}`. Kinds: `thirdParty` (price from a third party, vendor name optional), `labor` (internal labor: hours x the hourly rate), `parts`, `incentive` (taken off the cost). The old measure fields `implCost` and `incentive` are gone (the 2026-10-12 backup held no value in them).
