## Seasonal on-peak / off-peak energy lines (2026-10-23, branch 2026-10-23-seasonal-tou)
- A Utility E bill that crosses the Jun 1 / Oct 1 season edge prints up to four energy lines: On Pk Sum, On Pk Win, Off Pk Sum, Off Pk Win. The extractor reads them ONCE: `_evgTouRawLines` and `_evgTouFromText` (the printed amount of every charge line is read by `_evgChargeScan`, the same function `xChg` uses) (app/energy-savings.js, called at the Energy charges step of the Utility E reader) make raw lines; `touResolveLines` (computations/rates.js) gives each line its season (the printed Sum / Win word, else the printed date range, else the season of the period start through `seasonOfDate` / `SEASON_TABLE`), recovers a missing or garbled kWh, and places a line whose label OCR lost. `touApplyToBill` is the ONLY writer of the per-line bill fields `OnPkSum`, `OnPkWin`, `OffPkSum`, `OffPkWin` plus `KWh`, `Rate`, `Charge` (12 fields, list `TOU_FIELD_NAMES`) and of the totals `EnergyOnPeakCharge`, `EnergyOffPeakCharge`, `OnPeakKWh`, `OffPeakKWh` (each total is the sum of its lines; a kWh total is written only when every line of that side has a kWh). `touRatesEntry` writes `_rates.EnergyOnPeakCharge` / `EnergyOffPeakCharge` with one part per line; each part has `season` ('Sum' | 'Win'); `billChargePartsOf` (app/bill-analysis.js) copies `season` into the saved bill `_chargeParts`, and `_billSeasonRates` (computations/rates.js) reads that label first.
- kWh recovery order, per line: printed quantity x rate = charge to the cent; thousands digits repaired from the last 3 digits and 4 decimals; the one open line = kWh used minus the other lines (accepted only if it fits its own charge); charge / rate (accepted only if round(kWh x rate, 2) = charge). A clean printed quantity and rate whose product is not the charge stays as printed (the charge is the doubtful value; the older charge checks decide it; `touSyncFromRates` then writes the decision onto the line). A rate worked out from charge / quantity is used only for a quantity of 1,000 kWh or more.
- Hold: `touHoldOf` (called in `_applyExtractionGates`) adds ONE warning, "On-peak and off-peak kWh do not add up to kWh used. Check the PDF.", when a line has no kWh or the line kWh do not add up to kWh used (or to the ECA / EER / PTS charge-basis total from `_chargeBasisTotal`). When the lines were read, the old "On+Off peak sum" kWh witness and the old incomplete-tier warning in `_decideOnOffPeakKWh` are not used (`b._touLines`).
- Display: `renderPDFFields` (app/bill-analysis.js) shows one row per line from `touLinesOfBill`: "Energy On-Pk Summer", "Energy On-Pk Winter", "Energy Off-Pk Summer", "Energy Off-Pk Winter", each kWh, rate, charge (the charge cell edits the line field; `_pdfApplyFieldEdit` calls `touApplyToBill` again). An older saved bill has no line fields: `touLinesOfBill` derives the season from its `_chargeParts` (printed dates, else the quantity share against the day split) for display only and changes no stored value. The Utility Data charge detail row (app/utility-data.js) prints Summer / Winter for a part that has a season.
- No timer, no network, no env var. Check: node test-seasonal-tou.js (exit 0); node scripts/regression-gate.js.
- Printed charge wins (2026-10-23): `touKeepsPrintedCharge(bill, prefixOrField)` (computations/rates.js) is true for a 'read-unverified' line (clean printed quantity and rate whose product is not the printed charge). The gap check in `_postExtractionVerify` (Strategy B, app/bill-analysis.js) skips such a charge, and `touSyncFromRates` never replaces the line charge. Check: node test-seasonal-tou.js (case 'printed charge wins'); node tools/gate-correction-formulas.js.
- City bill, all OCR pass texts and printed usage cell (2026-10-23, computations/bill-layout.js `textLineCandidates`, `readBestPass`; app/energy-savings.js `_lbg_buildGasBill`; browser, no schedule, no env var, no network): the second source for a row (the printed text line, R3) now reads the text of EVERY OCR pass of the page (`read.pageTexts`), not only the winning position pass; the candidates are joined and the page sum still decides. `_lbg_buildGasBill` takes the Gas usage cell from the page text (`_lbg_textLayerGasUsage`) before the usage x rate check when the position read of the cell is lost or does not fit the printed Gas charge and the text cell does (marker `_auto_corrected_NaturalGasTherms`). A held gas bill still shows no Gas charge when the charge is not proven by the page (`_lbg_heldGasBill`, unchanged). How to check: node tools/test-city-all-pass-texts.js and node tools/test-city-repair-rules.js (exit 0).
## Equipment Matrix Raw view: BAS review columns (2026-10-23, branch 2026-10-23-bas-review)
- What: four columns in the Raw view, defined in `app/equipment-matrix.js` (`emGetColDefs`): Heating Type, Points, Screenshot, Review.
- Heating Type = `emRowHeatingType` (calls `_emDeriveHeatingType`; the Setpoint & Schedule export calls the same function). Points = `emRowPointCount`; the hover list comes from `emRowPointNames` (also used by the All Points drawer). Review = `emReviewReasons`, with the sample set from `_emSampleMap`. All three are read through `emRowCalc`, set up by `emPrepareRowCalc` before each draw or export.
- Column dropdown filter: `emFilterByCols` and `emColFilterOptions`; the list starts with "(non-blank)".
- Screenshot tick: `emSetScreenshot` writes `data.edits[rowId::screenshot]` through `emSetAssetEdit`, the same path as row Notes.
- Runs: on every table draw in the browser. No timer, no env var, no host change.
- Check: `node test-em-bas-review-checklist.js` (synthetic rows) prints "fail 0".

## Release v2026.10.23.161 (2026-10-23, GitHub site only)
- Merged: grey-text sweep (573274c7), PDF / OCR layout (92c18c5d), seasonal on-peak / off-peak lines (4ae5fcea), City gas bill all-pass-texts (94b4a3e1), Equipment Matrix BAS review (8e54865d). Pushed to `main` only. Not promoted to `stable`: Netlify has no deploy credit until 2026-11-02.
- Conflicts kept both sides: `energy-department.html` theme tokens (pure #fff / #000 from the grey sweep, plus `--ink` from the layout branch set to the same pure value) and three OPERATIONS.md sections (each branch appended at the end).
- Check: `node scripts/regression-gate.js`, `node test-seasonal-tou.js`, `node test-em-bas-review-checklist.js`, `node tools/test-city-all-pass-texts.js`, `node tools/gate-correction-formulas.js`, `node tools/test-no-grey-text.js`.
- Text color fixes found by the 3-engine check on this release: `.btn` now has a base fill (`--s3`) and text (`--text`), so a plain button no longer takes the browser's own colors; the PDF / OCR Save button (`.pdf-top-btn.primary`) has white text in Light, where its fill is dark teal.

## Bill reader repairs from the page, the meter's last saved bill and the bill's own numbers (2026-10-27, branch fix/2026-10-27-reader-mistakes)
- All of this runs in the user's browser while a PDF is read (no time zone, no timer, no server, no env var, no network). Check all of it with `node test-bill-reader-repairs.js` (synthetic fixtures, exit 0), `node test-seasonal-tou.js`, `node tools/gate-read-chain-correction.js` and `node scripts/regression-gate.js`.
- Last saved bill of the meter: `_priorSavedBillOf(extracted)` (app/energy-savings.js, search the name) is the ONE reader of the saved bills for the readers. It matches the account number, the commodity and the meter number, takes the saved bill whose end date is the latest on or before the extracted start date (`calcDays`), gives nothing when two bills tie, and returns it in the shape of an extracted bill. `_priorSavedBillsOfFile(bills)` (app/bill-analysis.js, before `_postExtractionVerify`) lists it for every bill of a PDF file. A PDF file of ONE bill is now checked too: step 0 of `_postExtractionVerify` (`correctExtractedReadChain`, computations/bill-flags.js) runs for any bill count and receives these bills as its previous bills (sixth argument). It never changes a saved bill; it only reads it.
- Misread read, rule e / e0 (computations/bill-flags.js `computeReadCorrection`): when the read difference D is proven by the usage (`_rcUsageProves`), a start read that is a 1-2 digit misread of the previous end read AND an end read that is a 1-2 digit misread of the previous end read plus D are both set from that chain (rule e with a next bill that agrees; rule e0 with no next bill, only when the previous bill is itself clean). Reason `READ_CHAIN_REASON.e`; e0 is provisional like a0 (`READ_CHAIN_PROVISIONAL`) and `_rcRevertProvisional` puts it back when the meter's later bills disagree. Check: `node tools/gate-read-chain-correction.js` (about 1 minute).
- Gas start read that lost its leading digit, rule f: `computeShortStartRepair` and `correctShortStartRead` (computations/bill-flags.js). A start read with fewer integer digits than the end read is replaced by the saved previous end read when that read has the digit count of the end read, ends with the printed start digits, is not above the end read and has the same decimals. Called from `_extractFileBills` (app/energy-savings.js) for every Gas bill before `_lbg_applyGasUsageAutofix`, so the therms follow the repaired reads. A field the user owns is never changed. The record is `_auto_corrected_StartRead` with the reason `READ_CHAIN_REASON.f`.
- Meter multiplier (app/energy-savings.js, block "MULTIPLIER REPAIR" in the Utility E reader): when the printed multiplier does not give the printed kWh Used, and kWh Used / Difference is a whole number (within 0.001) that is ONE confusable digit from the scanned multiplier, the whole number is the multiplier; kWh Used is the product when it is exact or one confusable digit away. The records are `_auto_corrected_MeterMultiplier` and `_auto_corrected_kWhConsumed`. In `meterKwh`, Difference x Multiplier and (End - Start) x Multiplier count as ONE vote (`_sharesMultiplier`): they share the multiplier.
- Seasonal energy lines (app/energy-savings.js `_evgTouRawLines`, computations/rates.js `touResolveLines`): the quantity of a line is the trailing number that ends at the 4 decimals, so a chart noise word before it is not glued to the quantity. The one open line with no printed rate AND no readable printed quantity gets `charge / quantity` (the quantity is kWh used minus the other lines) rounded to 5 decimals, accepted only when `_touVerifies` shows that quantity x rate gives the charge to the cent; a line with a readable printed quantity is never given a rate this way. `_EVG_ADDR` accepts a letter right after the house number ("105E5S ST"); a digit run alone is not an address.
- City gas row (app/energy-savings.js `_lbg_textLayerGasLine`, `_lbg_leadingOne`; computations/bill-layout.js `textLineCandidates`): when the position rows hold fewer than two reads of 100 or more for the Gas row, the same row of the text layer is read by `parseMetered`, and its reads and usage are taken when it gives two reads of 100 or more; the charge stays the position read's. A lone I or l before a thousands group reads as 1 (`_lbg_leadingOne`, used by the usage cell and the reads). In `textLineCandidates` the amounts of a label stop at the next label of the template on the same line, and a hit with no amount left is not a hit, so a chart header with the same word does not make a label look like it prints twice. Check: `node tools/test-bill-layout.js`, `node tools/test-city-all-pass-texts.js`, `node test-bill-reader-repairs.js`.
- `EXTRACTOR_RULES_VERSION` (app/energy-savings.js) was NOT changed by this branch: it is 5. Raising it makes the automatic repair read every stored bill again once; that is a release decision.
## Scanned-bill reader: same values in every browser (item efc38a78, 2026-10-27, branch fix/2026-10-27-browser-parity)
- OCR time budget is a hang guard only (app/bill-analysis.js, `OCR_PER_PAGE_ALLOWANCE_MS` 1200 s a page, `OCR_ABSOLUTE_CEILING_MS` 120 min a file). It used to be 120 s a page. Firefox runs the same Tesseract wasm about 5.7x slower than Chromium (one 2.5x pass 36 s against 6.3 s), so the old budget made Firefox leave the page before the position read (`BILL_LAYOUT.readPage`, marker `%%POSITION_READ%%`) and read different values. Slowest honest page measured: Firefox 215 s. The early returns that test `_ocrBudgetExceeded` stay, but only a stuck engine reaches them. Per-call limits (`OCR_TIMEOUT_MS` 90 s, `PDFJS_AWAIT_TIMEOUT_MS` 30 s, `LANCZOS_RESIZE_TIMEOUT_MS` 60 s) still stop one hung call. Check: `node tools/test-ocr-budget-hang-guard.js`; a Firefox run of nine scanned bill pages must give the same bills as Chromium (about 3 minutes a page in headless Firefox, about 40 s in Chromium).

## Meter match by the meter's own address, label addresses (2026-10-27, branch fix/2026-10-27-meter-match)
- `findMeterMatch` (app/bill-analysis.js) has a new last pass before the Pass 2 commodity adopt result is chosen. It runs only when identity, building-address and commodity passes found nothing and the bill has an account. `_meterAddressHits` finds same-commodity meters whose own `maddr` is the bill address (exact, or same street and same unit, `_meterOwnAddressIs`, `_addrUnit`). One hit returns matchType `address` with `accountMismatch: true`. Two or more return `ambiguous`. `address` and `ambiguous` are never auto-saved (`_isAutoRoutableMatch`).
- The building-address pass no longer takes the first same-commodity meter when the bill has an account. With 2+ meters it picks the one whose own address matches, else `ambiguous`.
- `_addrIsLabel` (app/energy-savings.js) sets `ServiceAddress` to null for label text ("Statement Date", "Account Number"). It is applied once in `_extractFileBills`. Stored label values are not repaired.
- Check: `node tools/test-meter-match-address.js` (synthetic data). No schedule, no env var.

## Automatic weather refresh (2026-10-27, branch fix/2026-10-27-weather-refresh)
- What: monthly HDD, CDD and average temperature (stored per ZIP in DB key `en_wdd_<zip>`, rows `{ym, hdd, cdd, avgTemp}`) fill themselves. The one owner is computations/weather-refresh.js (`wxRefreshZip`, `wxMergeAppend`, `wxFetchRows`). The browser calls `api.weatherdatadepot.com` directly (it answers `access-control-allow-origin: *`; tested from a page origin, HTTP 200). No Netlify function, no timer, no schedule, 0 Netlify invocations. CSP `connect-src` of energy-department.html lists the host.
- When: `wddRefreshProjectWeather(projId)` (app/utility-data.js) runs from ONE place, `udSelectBldg` (a building opens). It takes every distinct 5-digit ZIP of the project's buildings plus the project ZIP. A ZIP is fetched when it has no stored month or its newest stored month is older than last calendar month (local time of the browser). Once per ZIP per page load. A failed fetch is silent (stored months stay).
- Rules: stored months are never changed; only months newer than the newest stored month are appended (frozen baseline months cannot move). The current calendar month is never saved. A month missing from any of the 3 source series stays absent (never 0). A ZIP with no stored month gets all months from 2020. HDD and CDD come as running yearly totals (balance point 60 F); a month is the total minus the month before; a month whose predecessor is missing is left out. Average temperature is already monthly.
- Reports: `collectWeatherData` (app/csv-import.js) gives `null` (not 0) for a month with no weather row. The Weather appendix table (`rptPageAppendixWeather`) prints "No weather data" and the weather chart (`_apbWeatherHddCddChartSVG`, fed by `rptPageAppendixBaseline`) prints "No weather data" with no bars. The regression calculation table in `rptPageAppendixBaseline` also prints "No weather data" (no predicted or saved value) for a month with no weather row.
- Removed: scripts/fetch-weather.js, scripts/zips.json, weather-data/66053.json and the static-file fallback `wddPrefetchFromServer`. There is no manual weather script now. CSV upload (`wddHandleFile`) still works and still replaces the cache.
- Degree-day base (2026-10-31, branch fix/2026-10-31-dd-base-label): HDD and CDD are base 60°F. The source is api.weatherdatadepot.com; the base is the constant `WX_DD_BASE_F` (computations/weather-refresh.js, used in the two `balancePoint` URLs and in the HDD/CDD column headers of the meter weather table and the Import Weather Data preview in app/utility-data.js). A CSV import must be base 60°F too (help text says so). Check: grep `balancePoint` shows only `WX_DD_BASE_F`; the headers read "HDD (base 60°F)".
- Fetch on Generate Report (2026-11-02, branch fix/2026-11-02-q3-report-review-findings): `generateReportPreview` (app/report-preview.js:13) calls `wxEnsureMonths` (computations/weather-refresh.js:173) with the project ZIPs and the report months, WAITS for the fetch, then builds the report. It runs on each Generate Preview click, only for a ZIP whose stored months do not reach the newest report month that the source has published. It needs no env var and no Netlify call. If the fetch fails, the report is built and `totals.weatherWaiting` prints the reason in the preview note (`#rptPreviewNotes`). Fetch timeout: each request ends after 20 s (`AbortSignal.timeout`, app/report-preview.js), and the preview note then says "could not be loaded: <reason>". Savings cache: the key in `getMeterSavings` (computations/savings.js) includes `getWeatherFingerprint(projId, bldgId)` (app/utility-data.js, memoized on the stored weather array with a WeakMap, so a cache hit costs one lookup), so a saved month shows at once with no reload. Merged findings and contract page: each part is a `data-subsection` block with its own sidebar row (`_setSectionVisible`, app/report-preview.js); the page hides when every block on it is hidden. Preview-only note: with no `en_bas_schedhistory_<projectId>` record, the quarterly preview says the operating hours page is left out (not in the PDF). Check: `node tools/test-weather-refresh.js` (3 `ensure:` checks, synthetic).
- Check: `node tools/test-weather-refresh.js` (synthetic; includes the frozen baseline check: appending Sep 2026 changes no baseline-period row or regression, and the contracted project check: a weather fill, also an empty ZIP back-filled from 2020, writes only the ZIP key and changes no stored `m.baseline`). A meter whose saved baseline has no weather coefficients (status 'no-weather') takes the new weather on its next Save Baseline; on 2026-10-27 no contracted meter was in that state for any new ZIP. Real check: refetch 2020 to 2026 for 66053 and 66062 and compare to the stored rows (all 157 months equal on 2026-10-27).

- Page header row (2026-10-27, energy-department.html `.topbar`, app/core.js `_syncHeaderTabs`): the page has ONE header row, at most 40 px tall when the tabs fit on one line. On the left (`#hdrTabs`): nothing when no project is open (the department name shows only in the sidebar), or the open project's tabs (`#pdTabBar`, text only, built by `_getProjTabHTML`, drawn by `renderDetail`) when a project is open. Tabs wrap to a second line when the row is full; there is no scroll, no arrow and no hidden tab. The active underline is the bottom border of the label (`.tab-lbl`). On the right: bell, the user email (`#topName`, from `currentUser.name`), Sign Out (`signOut`). `_syncHeaderTabs()` is the ONE place that decides whether the project tabs show. It runs from `sv`, `showList` and `renderDetail`. The page fills to the bottom of the window. app/sync-ui.js `updateCornerRow` adds the class `ch-corner-row` to `body` (a 38 px bottom margin on `.content`) only while the Conflict history or Held drafts pill shows. No timer, no network, no env var. How to check: open a project and look at the header at 1280 px and 1920 px wide; the bottom of the page must touch the bottom of the window.
- Short terms and one-row project bar (2026-10-28, app/core.js project info bar in `renderDetail`, `_phcFullTitle`; energy-department.html `.phc-info`; tools/test-no-ui-abbreviations.js): Matt's standing rule: on INTERNAL screens the standard energy terms (EUI, SA number, kBtu, CBECS, DCV, VAV, AHU, RTU) may be short when a hover tooltip (`title`) gives the full words. Invented or unusual short forms stay banned; reports, PDFs and client documents stay spelled out. The gate allows EUI only in `app/core.js` and `app/graphics-setpoints.js` (`BANNED_WORD_FILE_EXCEPTIONS`); EUI stays banned everywhere else, reports included. The project bar shows `SA277` and `Site EUI` with tooltips. The info items sit in `#phc-info-<id>`. At 1280 px and wider the info text and the four project buttons stay on ONE row: the info text is cut with an ellipsis (its tooltip holds the full text), the buttons never wrap. Under 1280 px the items wrap as before. No timer, no network, no env var. How to check: open a project at 1280 px and 1920 px (one row); `node tools/test-no-ui-abbreviations.js` passes.


## CSC Price List view in the Cost Estimate tab (2026-10-29, branch feat/2026-10-29-csc-price-list)
- What: a read-only table of the imported price list (`en_pricing_catalog`). Open it with the "Price list" button next to the "Price list: N items, imported <date>" label (Estimate type bar, shown for every estimate type: BAS Audit, Full Facility Audit, Retrofit, unset; rendered once by `_pricingEstimateTypeBarHTML`). Code: `_pricingImportStatusHTML`, `_pricingPriceListPanelHTML`, `_pricingPriceListRows`, `_pricingPriceListFilter` in app/pricing-estimator.js (after `_pricingFmt`).
- One price rule: CSC price = `_pricingUnitPriceFor(entry, {priceBasis:'contract'})` (40% of list, `COST_CONTRACT_PCT`), the same function the estimate rows call. The header line reads `COST_CONTRACT_PCT` and `_pricingGetConfig().hourlyRate` live. Net = the ALC net column (what CSC pays).
- Open state, search, category and sort are in memory only. Nothing is stored. No network call, no timer.
- How to check: `node test-pricing-price-list.js` (21 pass, includes every estimate type); open any project > Cost Estimate > Price list.

- BAS schedule import fills saved Set Points zones (2026-10-30; app/equipment-matrix.js emFillSavedSetpointSchedules, called by emAttachParsedSchedules). Runs when Save is pressed on the District Calendar import. It sets the Schedule of saved Set Points zones whose schedule is blank, "?" or from an earlier import; a typed schedule stays. Needs no env var. Check: node tools/test-bas-first-use.js.

- Equipment Matrix Edit Mode speed (2026-10-30; app/equipment-matrix.js:7249, :7734). Cell edits use one delegated `focusout` listener on document (`_emAttachNavDelegatedListeners`, attached once per page load) instead of an onblur attribute on each cell. The first render batch is smaller in Edit Mode (`CELL_BUDGET` 8000 instead of 30000), so the table shows sooner. No timer, no network call, no env var. How to check: `node test-equipment-matrix-render-performance.js`; turn Edit Mode on, edit a cell, click away, and the value saves.

## v189 to v191 entries (2026-10-31; why each behavior exists)
- PDF/OCR "Create building + meter" (v190; app/bill-analysis.js `_mbNewBldgName`, `_bldgKey`, `_mbSetCreateTarget`, `_mbLinkSameMeter`, `_autoCreateMeterAndSaveBill` last argument `newBldgName`). Why: a bill with no building match used to land on "Unmatched Bills", which hid the building from the project. Now a bill with no match and a service address gets the button "Create building + meter" (the review bar and the bill box). No building is made when the button is pressed; the building is made at save time. The name and address are the service address with spaces cleaned. Bills of the same file with the same address (compared by `_bldgKey`, lower case, letters and digits only) are linked to the same create target, and at save the building is found by name or address before one is made, so they share ONE building. The new building goes into the project scope. A bill with no service address keeps "Create meter on Unmatched Bills". No timer, no network, no env var. Check: `node tools/test-ocr-create-words.js`.
- PDF/OCR Extracted Values full width (v190; energy-department.html `.ef-grid`). Why: the old container-query column steps left empty space at the right at some window widths. Each cell now has a minimum of 190 px and grows to share the row, so every row fills the pane at every width. CSS only. Check: `node tools/test-extracted-values-grid.js`.
- PDF/OCR review bar (v189; app/bill-analysis.js `_mbReviewBarHtml`, `_mbReviewMax`, `_mbToggleReviewList`). Why: the user must see the counts, the issues and Save All while reading the values, and the bar must not cover bill rows. It stays on screen under the values. On a narrow window it shows 1 issue link and a "+N more issues" button (Show less to fold). A bill with no building shows "Create building + meter" in the bar. Check: `node tools/test-ocr-review-bar.js`.
- District Calendar change list grouping (v190; computations/sched-actions.js `computeActions`, `mergeSpans`, `whenText`, `reconcile`). Why: one line per event gave many lines for one BAS fix. Rules: (1) the unit key is the BAS scheduling group when the program has one, else the program, so the line names the thing that is edited in the BAS. (2) Per unit and per day, uncovered spans of all events merge when they overlap or touch. (3) Days with the same merged spans join into one line ("Mon/Wed Nov 3 - Nov 24 (6 dates)"). (4) The line shows only the uncovered span (`BC.eventCoverage` gaps), never the whole event time. (5) An event inside the main building schedule is covered, so it gives no line. (6) A line stores `eventIds`; reconcile uses them, because the line id is now unit plus spans and no longer one event id. A line closes as "Fixed in BAS" when every event is covered, "Merged into another line" when an event is still short under another line, "Event removed from the calendar" when none remain. The id changes when the spans change, so the old line closes and a new line opens. Check: `node tools/test-sched-grouping.js`, `node tools/test-sched-actions.js`.
- Weather refresh on load (v189, base label v191; app/utility-data.js `wddRefreshAllProjectsWeather`, `wddRefreshZips`; computations/weather-refresh.js `WX_DD_BASE_F`). Why: new months of weather must appear without a manual script. On the event `dbFullPull` (first full data pull of the page load) each ZIP of every project is tried once; no call is made when the newest stored month is the last complete month. Stored months never change; only newer months are added. Source: api.weatherdatadepot.com, called from the browser. Degree days use base 60°F because the contract baselines were computed on 60°F; `WX_DD_BASE_F` is the one value for the fetch URLs and the HDD/CDD headers, and a CSV import must be base 60°F. No timer. Check: `node tools/test-weather-refresh.js`.

## Quarterly report layout (2026-10-31, branch feat/2026-10-31-baseline-reporting-weather)
- What: a quarterly report that is not a presented (frozen) one is built by `_rptQuarterReportHTML` (app/report-engine.js, block starting at `_rptQuarterLayout`). `generateReportHTML` calls it first. It makes 8 pages: cover, results, energy use and weather, findings, contract position, energy use intensity, meter results and bills, method. Presented reports and annual reports keep the old page list.
- Shared values: `_rptBuildingTarget` (building share of the quarter target), `_rptResultOf` (result words and colour from the sign of the saving), `_rptEnergyResultPositive` (the environmental page shows only when energy use fell), `_rptBuildingMonthMap` (monthly cost by building). Totals still come from `billableSavingsWithPresented`, `savingsNotBilledWithPresented`, `computeCscSplit` and `_rptContractProjection`.
- Runs: when the user builds a report (browser, user time zone). Needs no env var.
- Check: build a third quarter 2026 report for a project from a backup that has weather for July to September; it must show 8 pages, "Page N of 8", and a total that equals the sum of the building rows.

## Quarterly report operating hours and set points table (2026-11-02, branch feat/2026-11-02-q3-schedule-table)
- What: the quarterly report (`_rptQuarterReportHTML`, app/report-engine.js) prints one page, "Operating Hours and Set Points" (`_rptQSchedulePage`, section key `basSchedule`, right after the Meter Results and Bills page, same section toggle), with one row per building: baseline weekday occupied hours, quarter weekday occupied hours, weekend occupied hours, occupied heating and cooling set points, each with date ranges.
- Data: the project's BAS schedule history record, store key `en_bas_schedhistory_<projectId>` (loaded by `_rptBasHistoryLoad`, put into `collectReportData` as `basScheduleHistory`). It is a synced key (prefix `en_bas_`, app/sync-classification.js). Shape: `{version:1, buildings:[{building, baselineWeekday:[{text}], weekday:[{text,from,to}], weekend:[{kind,text,from,to}], setPoints:[{kind,text,from,to}]}]}`. `from` and `to` are YYYY-MM-DD; an entry with no dates is the baseline. `building` must equal the building name in the report. No client value is in code. The code clips each dated entry to the report period and drops entries outside it (`_rptBasCellLines`, `_rptBasScheduleRows`).
- No record, or no record for any report building: no page and no placeholder.
- Load a record: Settings, Restore from backup file, mode Add, with a JSON file of the form `{"en_bas_schedhistory_<projectId>": {...}}`. Add mode adds the key and changes no other key.
- Runs: when the user builds a quarterly report (browser, user time zone). Needs no env var.
- Check: `node tools/test-report-schedule-table.js` (synthetic data); build the report for a project with a record and look for the page.

## Audit log import fills the schedule history record (2026-11-02, branch feat/2026-11-02-bas-audit-log-import)
- What: the BAS schedules card (app/bas-schedules.js) has an "Import audit log" button (`basPickAudit`, file picker `bas-afile-<pid>`, several files at once). It turns the WebCTRL audit log export (columns Date, Description, Details, Location, Operator) into the record `en_bas_schedhistory_<pid>` that the quarterly report table reads. No hand-built record is needed.
- Code: app/bas-audit-log.js (`BasAuditLog.parseRows`, `mergeStore`, `storeEvents`, `analyze`, `apply`, `resolveNames`; pure, no storage). CSV split: `baSplitCSV` (app/bas-alarms.js). Building of a row: `BasDiff.buildingOf` (app/bas-diff.js). The one write is `DB.set('en_bas_schedhistory_<pid>', ...)` in `basPickAudit`. Script tag is in energy-department.html after bas-diff.js.
- Rules: a change at time T on date D applies from D; a day shows its end-of-day state. Hours: Add, Update and Delete schedule rows at the building root location, type Weekly; hours of a day are the union of the schedules that list it. Set points: lines `.../occupied_heating|occupied_cooling changed from "X" to "Y"` in Details (any Description); a zone is the Location; the value of a day is the most common heating and cooling pair over zones that have both points; the baseline set point is the most common value before each zone's first change. A value with no source is printed as an em dash in the report. Nothing is invented.
- Event store (2026-11-02): the import does not build the record from the picked files alone. `BasAuditLog.mergeStore` keeps every schedule and set point event it has read in `en_bas_auditevents_<pid>` (`{version:2, lastDay, locs:[...], events:[[iso, kind A/U/D/S, locIndex, text, operatorHash]]}`; each event once, key = date+location+text+operator; alarm acknowledgements and other rows are not stored; schedule rows keep only the lines the reader uses, set point rows only `H|C from to`). `analyze` then rebuilds the history from ALL stored events (`storeEvents`), so 90 one-day files from the scheduled Location Audit Log report, in any order and overlapping, give the same record as one 90-day export. Columns are found by header name; 5 columns (manual export) or 6 (scheduled report, adds Reason) are accepted. The key is in the sync status labels (app/sync-ui.js), the restore group `BAS schedule history` (app/restore-merge.js) and the `en_bas_` note of app/sync-classification.js. Size (measured on 6 real logs, 12 months, 2325 events): 630 KB with full events, 178 KB compact, so about 180 KB a year. After a write, if the blob is over 1 MB (`STORE_WARN_BYTES`, about 5 years; the browser store holds about 5 MB for the whole site) the card message adds the size and a warning. Nothing is pruned.
- Merge: log building names are matched to project buildings by `BasAuditLog.resolveNames` (one function for both imports). An exact name wins. A prefix match ("X Elementary" for "X Elementary School") is accepted only when it is the only candidate and no other log building in the same import could map to the same project building. Otherwise the building is skipped and the card message lists it ("Not imported, no single matching building in this project"). With no project list the log names are kept. The last run of every building ends on the latest event day of the whole export (`lastDay` of the store), so a state still in effect is not cut short. The days inside a building's window are replaced; the same files twice give the same record. An auto baseline carries `windowStart` (first event day of its log); a later import replaces it only when its window starts earlier (an equal start also replaces, because every import rebuilds from the full store), so the order of imports does not matter. A baseline entry without a source mark (typed by a person) is kept, also beside an auto entry. Other fields of the stored building entry are kept. One import runs at a time per project (`_importing` in app/bas-schedules.js); a second pick shows "An import is already running."
- Runs: when the user picks files (browser, user time zone). No network call, no timer, no env var. Sync: the key is already in the synced `en_bas_` class (app/sync-classification.js), the restore groups (app/restore-merge.js) and the sync status labels (app/sync-ui.js).
- Check: `node tools/test-bas-audit-log.js` (synthetic data; also drives the button in headless Chromium). In the browser pick the same audit log files twice; the report table must be the same.
- Fallbacks in the audit log import (2026-11-02): `BasAuditLog.analyze(events, pointsList)`. (1) Baseline: when the record has no baseline for a cell, it writes the earliest values in the log (old values of the first change of each schedule; the most common value before each zone's first set point change), with `src` = `BasAuditLog.AUDIT_SRC`. (2) A building with no occupied set point row in the log takes the set points of the stored WebCTRL points list: the most common occupied heat and cool pair over equipment that has both, labelled with the type when all are the same type; it is written as one dated entry for the log window and as the baseline, `src` = `BasAuditLog.POINTS_SRC`. The points list is the Equipment Matrix (`en_eqmatrix_<pid>`, imported by `emHandleImport` -> `emParseCSVText`, `emDetectColMap`, `emExtractEquipmentGroups`, `emGroupToMatrixRow`); `window.basPointSource` reads it with `emGetNormalizedPoints` and `emFormatEquipTypeLabel` (no second parser). The Set Points sheet import replaces entries with either mark; an entry typed by a person is never replaced.
- Baseline import (2026-11-02): the card's "Import baseline (Set Points sheet)" button (`basPickBaseline`, picker `bas-bfile-<pid>`) reads the Optimization Strategy Sheet "Set Points" sheet saved as CSV, in the format the Equipment Matrix Set Points export writes (`EM_SETPOINT_EXPORT_HEADERS`, app/equipment-matrix.js; columns found by name). Code: app/bas-baseline.js (`BasBaseline.parseRows`, `apply`; pure). It writes only the baseline entries (`baselineWeekday` and the undated weekend and setPoints entries) of the same record `en_bas_schedhistory_<pid>`; no new key. Per building: the most common occupied hours Mon-Fri, weekend value and occupied heating and cooling pair; another hours group held by 15% or more of the rows is added in brackets ("Area G ..." when all its rows are in one area, else "some ..."). Building names are matched to project names by `BasAuditLog.resolveNames` (a log building with no single match is skipped and listed in the card message). Each entry carries `src:"Optimization Strategy Sheet (Existing)"`; a baseline entry without that mark (typed by a person) is kept; dated entries are never touched. The same file twice, or either import first, gives the same record. Runs when the user picks a file (browser). No network, no timer, no env var. Check: `node tools/test-bas-baseline.js` (synthetic).

## Notices and links never cover the page (2026-11-03)

- Nothing that stays on screen may be `position: fixed` over the header, the tabs or the page, and nothing may reserve layout space with padding or margin. The page (`main.content`) ends at the window bottom.
- Notices (offline, signed out) go in ONE stack in the normal page flow: `#ch-sync-banner-stack`, first child of `.app-col` (`ensureStack` in app/sync-ui.js, also `window.chNoticeStack`). The new-version notice is NOT in that stack: `_showVersionUpdateBanner` (app/report-engine.js) shows a plain sidebar footer link "New version: reload" (`#ch-ver-update-link`, made by `window.chSidebarLink` in app/sync-ui.js; slim rail text "Reload"). A click saves the page state (`_savePageStateForVersionUpdate`) and reloads. It shows only when the live version differs from the loaded one. It takes no space in the main column.
- The login page has no sidebar, so it shows no Sync status and no sidebar links.
- Past conflicts has NO sidebar link (removed 2026-11-05). It opens from the Sync status panel (`_renderArchiveEntry` in app/sync-ui.js). `ensureSidebarLink` still serves the page-update link.
- Kept fixed on purpose: dialogs and panels the user opens, the transient toast (below the header row, goes away alone), the Customize toolbar and the feedback button (modes the user turns on).
- Check: `node test-no-overlay-layout.js <outDir>` (1920x1080 and 960x1080; Dashboard, Utility Data Normalized, Equipment Matrix, Cost Estimate).

## Report is not built with missing input (2026-11-03)
- `reportMissingInputs(projId, meterIds, buildingIds, startYm, endYm)` (computations/report-data.js) is the ONE gate for a quarterly or annual report. Bills missing = `getReportCoverage().missing`; weather missing = `reportWeatherGaps` (reporting-month CDD and baseline-month HDD and CDD, tested directly). `_rptGeneratePreviewNow` (app/report-preview.js) calls it after the weather fetch (`wxEnsureMonths`). If anything is missing it builds NO report and writes the reason in the inline box `#rptV2BlockedMsg` of the Generate Report dialog (set up in `openReportModalV2`, app/report-engine.js). The box clears when Year, Quarter or type changes. Runs on each Generate Preview click; no schedule, no env var.
- Report pages never print a status. The earlier "Waiting for weather data" cells, the cumulative/period `weatherWaiting` totals, `_rptWaitingYMs` and the " - waiting for bills" file name tag are removed from app/report-engine.js. The in-site pages (Dashboard, Project Performance, Energy Graphics, Utility Data) keep their own internal "Waiting for weather data" text; it is not part of a report.
- Check: `node tools/test-report-blocks-on-missing-input.js` (missing month -> no report + message; complete -> report built) and `node tools/test-report-q3-review-findings.js` section 5 (no status words on the report pages).

## Project tab sections (2026-11-03, branch feat/2026-11-03-tab-moves)
- What: a project tab can hold labeled sections (pill buttons at the top of the tab). Equipment Matrix tab: Equipment Matrix, Facility Audit, Set Points, BAS Trends, BAS Alarms. Dashboard tab: Overview, Contacts, Hours, Documents. Cost Estimate tab: Cost Estimate, Budget. These eight were top-level tabs; the old tabs are gone. The ONE list is `PROJ_SUBTABS` (app/core.js, above `PROJ_TABS_DEFAULT`). `projTabResolve(id)` maps any tab id (old or new) to `{tab, sub}`; `sPTab(tab, el, sub)` (app/core.js) uses it, so session restore, `?tab=` links (energy-department.html, report "Edit" links), `ch_lastProjTab_<pid>` and `sPTab('setpoints')` or `sPTab('hours')` open the section. `projSubIsActive(projId, tab, sub)` says if a section is on screen (used by app/bas-alarms.js to refresh after an import).
- When it runs: in the browser, on a click on a pill or on `sPTab`. No timer, no network, no env var.
- Stored: `ch_lastProjSub_<pid>_<tab>` in localStorage (the last section used; not synced). Pane ids: the first section's pane is `ptab-sub-<id>`, others keep the old id `ptab-<id>`; body ids (`ptab-facility-audit-body-<pid>` and so on) did not change.
- Check: `node scripts/regression-gate.js` (section checks in the project loop) and `node tools/test-header-tabs-and-scroll.js`. Manual: open a project, Equipment Matrix tab, click each pill.

## District Calendar views: Day / Week / Month / Year (2026-11-03, branch feat/2026-11-03-calendar-views)
- What: the District Calendar project tab (containers `pcal-top-<pid>`, `sact-panel-<pid>`, `pcal-panel-<pid>`) shows ONE period: Day, Week (Monday to Sunday), Month or Year. Default = the current week, starting today (no past days). The user steps back and forward with the arrow buttons; Today returns. A one-time event outside the period is not listed. Search still reaches every date. Year shows one line for each month; a month button opens that month. This replaces the old "Next 14 days / This month / Show past" buttons.
- One function each: the period = `CalendarDiff.periodRange` and `CalendarDiff.shiftAnchor` (app/calendar-diff.js); the events and their marks = `CalendarDiff.listView` (`from` and `to` days; the old `mode` and `month` options are gone); the mark word New / Moved / Changed / Canceled = `CalendarDiff.markLabel` (Moved = the day or time changed). Marks come from the kept sync history (`en_cal_hist_<pid>`, `CalendarDiff.carriedMarks`, 7 days). No second store.
- BAS needs of the period: `SchedActions.periodView` (computations/sched-actions.js) = `periodNeeds` (the existing `computeActions` lines, kept when at least one date is in the period; a weekday with 3 or more dates is one "Every Thu ... Add once as a repeating schedule" line, grouped by BAS group as in v190) + `BasCoverage.checkStatus` (computations/bas-coverage.js, built on `eventCoverage`). "In the BAS now" = the occupied times of that program in the copy of the date (`BasDiff.blocksText`).
- Cannot compare: the card never says "Nothing needs to change" unless every checkable event was checked. With nothing checked it says "Cannot tell yet" and lists the reasons: no BAS copy imported; N places not matched to a BAS space (N events); days with no BAS copy (N events cannot be checked, copies exist for Thu, Fri only); N events in a space with no BAS schedule.
- Which BAS copy a date uses (2026-11-03): `BasCoverage.snapshotForDate` (computations/bas-coverage.js), the one owner. The copy of the date itself; else the nearest copy of the same weekday; else, for Monday to Thursday only, the nearest copy of another Monday to Thursday day (the BAS runs one schedule Mon-Thu). Friday, Saturday and Sunday never borrow. `BasCoverage.sourceText` names the copy used, in the BAS column hover and the "In the BAS now" column (for example "Mon-Thu schedule (Thu Oct 29, 2026 BAS copy)"). A day with no copy says so in the card. Check: `node tools/test-calendar-views.js`.
- When it runs: on every draw of the tab and on every view change, from saved data only. No network call, no timer, no new stored key, no Netlify change. The view and the anchor day are in memory only (`_st` in app/project-calendar.js).
- Check it works: `node tools/test-calendar-views.js`, `node tools/test-calendar-upcoming.js`, `node tools/test-calendar-auto-sync.js`, `node tools/test-sched-actions.js`, `node tools/test-sched-grouping.js`. In the browser: open a project with a calendar feed, District Calendar tab; the week starts today; Month then the next arrow shows next month; a December one-time event is not in a November week.

## Energy Graphics: kWh vs kW analysis card (2026-11-03, branch feat/2026-11-03-energy-graphics-analysis)
- What: a card right below the Monthly Electric kWh / kW charts on the project Energy Graphics tab (`_egfxEaCardHtml`, app/graphics-setpoints.js, container `egfx-ea-<projectId>`). A building selector (`egfxEaSelect`) picks one building. A reading line for each building says only what the numbers show (`eaReadingText`). The two newest years of electric bills are shown month by month with kWh, peak kW, load factor, full-load hours, kWh per cooling degree day and therms per heating degree day, and compared over the months that have a bill in both years. Every value has a hover text with its arithmetic.
- Math: `computations/energy-analysis.js` (loaded after hvac-enduse.js in energy-department.html) is the ONE place for load factor (`eaLoadFactor`, kWh / (peak kW x hours in the bill period)), equivalent full-load hours (`eaFullLoadHours`, kWh / peak kW), use per degree day (`eaPerDegreeDay`; null, shown as a dash, when the month has fewer than `EA_MIN_DEGREE_DAYS` = 50 degree days; those months are also left out of the same-months ratio) and baseload per day (`eaBaseloadPerDay`, the lowest month in kWh per day, picked with `_hvacLowestNAvg` n = 1; the HVAC End-Use Estimate baseload is a different figure: the average of the 3 lowest months in kWh per month). `getEnergyAnalysis(projId, b)` reads the bills: a bill goes into a month with `billMonth`, hours = `calcDays` x 24, gas therms from `resolveGasUsageTherms`, degree days from `getWeatherForBuilding`; meters excluded from the baseline are skipped (the rule the charts above use). With two electric meters the kW add and the load factor denominator is the sum of (meter kW x meter hours). The baseline kW column is `resolveHvacLoadData(...).kwArr`. The older load factor in the Utility Data Performance tab (app/utility-data.js, kWh / (kW x 24 x days)) and the Scorecard (app/scorecard.js) are separate copies of the same idea; they are not moved onto this function yet.
- Runs: on every Energy Graphics render and when the building selector changes (browser, user time zone). No network call, no timer, no env var, no stored data.
- Check: `node tools/test-energy-analysis.js` (synthetic data); open Energy Graphics for a project with two years of bills and hover a Load factor value: the text shows kWh / (kW x hours).

## Energy Graphics: "Where the energy goes" table (2026-11-03, branch feat/2026-11-03-energy-graphics-analysis)
- What: the "HVAC End-Use Estimate" box of each building on the Energy Graphics tab is now a plain table titled "Where the energy goes - <building>" (`_hvacEnduseCardsHtml`, app/graphics-setpoints.js) with one reading line (for example "83% of gas goes to heating. 22% of electricity goes to cooling. Cooling adds 199.4 kW to the summer peak."). Columns: Heating / Cooling, Year-round, Total. Rows: Gas (therms), Electric (kWh), Peak demand (kW). Each figure shows part and percent of the total; the hover shows the arithmetic (part divided by total).
- Same computation as before: `resolveHvacLoadData(projId, b).enduse` = `computeHvacEnduse` (computations/hvac-enduse.js). Year-round = average of the 3 lowest months (`_hvacLowestNAvg`) x months, i.e. the sum of min(month, baseload); heating (gas) and cooling (kWh) = the sum of max(0, month - baseload); cooling kW = peak kW - winter level (average of the December, January and February billed kW). `computeHvacEnduse` now also returns `totalKwh`, `totalGas` (the real sums of the baseline bills used), `yearRoundKwh` and `yearRoundGas`, so the table's Total is never back-calculated from a percent. Total gas includes propane at therms per gallon (as `gasArr` does). With several baseline years the monthly values are the calendar-month averages (`_hvlMonthlyBaseline`), so the total is the average baseline year.
- Runs: on every Energy Graphics render. No network call, no timer, no env var, no stored data. The table CSS (`_EGFX_TBL_CSS`) is shared with the kWh vs kW card above.
- Check: `node tools/test-energy-analysis.js` (section 8, synthetic data); on a project backup the High School table total gas must equal the 2025 gas in "Annual Summary by Building".
- Follow-up: CO2e uses IPCC AR5 GWP (CH4 28, N2O 265) as the EPA Hub does. The report and the Energy Graphics box show the signed NET value of each gas (no clamp to 0); equivalents (cars, trees) show only when net CO2e is positive. Sign rule: the displayed figure is the CHANGE compared with baseline, plus = more emissions, minus = fewer (`emissionsChangeText` flips the avoided value; note text `EMISSIONS_SIGN_NOTE`). Equivalents show only when net CO2e is avoided.

## Quarterly report page flow and detail tables (2026-11-04)
- What: `_rptQuarterReportHTML` (app/report-engine.js) builds each REPORT_SECTIONS page. The core pages (cover to Observations) each start a new page. Every later section (`flow: true` in REPORT_SECTIONS) is packed (2026-11-06, branch 2026-11-06-pack-pages, v243): `_rptQPackRun` cuts each rendered page body into units (`_rptQUnits`: a heading with its notes and its table, or a block marked `data-unit="keep"`) and puts the units of one sidebar group on pages one after the other while `_rptQBodyFits` (against `RPT_Q_PAGE_BODY_PX`, measured in the DOM by `_rptQMeasureH`, estimate `_rptQEstH` where there is no DOM) says the page still fits; a unit that does not fit starts the next page, a new group always starts a page. Each unit sits in a `data-subsection` block of its section (the preview sidebar toggles all blocks of a key, `_setSectionVisible` in app/report-preview.js, and hides a page with no block left). Page titles come from the first unit; a later page repeats it with "(continued)"; REPORT_SECTIONS `packTitle` and `leadTitle` name a section whose source pages have different titles (Building Summaries). Meter Performance puts the meter tables of all buildings first, then the Building Baseline Data blocks (`_rptMeterPerformancePages`). Check: `node tools/test-report-page-packing.js` (no page leaves room for the first block of the next page of its group; none under half full unless it is the last of its group). `_rptQBillGroups` groups the quarter's bills by meter (a meter with a baseline and no bill shows 0 bills). `_rptQFuelDetailPages` builds the electric, gas and propane detail pages (SVG chart from `_rptQMonthChartSVG`, which the Word export turns into a PNG). `_rptQAppendixDPages` builds Appendix D by bill month with row-budget paging.
- Runs: in the browser when a quarterly report preview is built (user time zone). No timer, no env var.
- Check: `node tools/test-report-q3-review-findings.js` (section 6b is the report gate: banned text, every meter in the tables, month totals, electric detail footing, Word footer fields). To see the pages, build the report and download the PDF.

## Quarterly report Word layout (2026-11-05)
- What: the Word export (`exportReportToDocx` in app/report-engine.js, `_docxTranslatePages` in app/docx-writer.js) lays a report out like the PDF. A page with stamped report tables (`data-dx-border`, set by `_docxStampLiveStyles`) is a "report": (1) `_docxFitColumnWidths` and `_docxColumnMinWidths` raise each table column to its longest word (numbers with units and no-wrap cells stay whole when the table has room); table text is the live page size, 9pt or more, cell margins 50 twips; (2) `_docxTightenReportXml` gives every paragraph an exact line of 1.15 times its font, 10pt body text, 4pt after, smaller space around headings, Heading 3 at the margin, and keeps a note with the table under it; (3) a heading takes its size and alignment from the live page (`data-dx-fs`, `data-dx-align`); (4) `_docxAssemble` gets `compactLetterhead: true`, so page 1 starts 152pt from the top; (5) `_docxApplyPageNumberFooters` writes "Page N of M" in Arial 10pt at the right, with an exact-height empty paragraph under it so the number is above the letterhead wave. The "Year to Date" page body has no second heading. (2026-11-05, branch fix/2026-11-05-q3-word-defects) Three Word-only fixes: (6) a minus sign is U+2011 in the report HTML (`_rptQNoBreakMinus`, the one marker for the site and PDF); `_docxRun` writes each U+2011 as Word's own `<w:noBreakHyphen/>`, because Word draws the U+2011 character in Arial as a raised bar; (7) `exportReportToDocx` passes `pageCount: pages.length` to `_docxAssemble`, and `_docxPageNOfMRuns` writes it as the cached NUMPAGES result (a viewer that does not recalculate shows it; the field stays live). Word shows a stale "of 3" on page 1 only when a script moves the view (GoTo, zoom) before it finishes laying out the pages; a plain open shows the true total; (8) no table or picture is wider than the text area (10080 twips): `_docxHoldTableWidth` cuts a table whose word-whole minimums exceed it, `_docxRegisterImage` shrinks a wider picture with its proportions kept.
- Runs: in the browser when the user clicks Download Word on a report (user time zone). No timer, no env var.
- Check: `node tools/test-report-word-layout.js`. Real check: export the .docx to PDF through Word and compare it with the PDF; a 6-building quarterly report is about as long as the PDF (+1 page) with no near-empty page and no word broken inside a cell.
- Table headers and units (2026-11-06, branch 2026-11-06-q3-word-and-headers): `_rptQTable` (app/report-engine.js) centers every header cell, horizontally and vertically, and `_rptHeadKeepUnits` keeps a parenthesized unit on one line. `rptPage` runs `_rptProtectHeaderUnits` on every page body: each unit in a `<th>` (kWh, kW, kBtu/ft2, MMBtu, $/kWh, $/kW, $/therm, $/gal) goes in a span with `text-transform:none;white-space:nowrap`, so it keeps its real case inside the capitalised header. `_docxStyleFmtFromEl` (app/docx-writer.js) reads `text-transform:none` and drops the capitals for that run. The Word footer shows the period label ("Q3 2026 Quarterly Report", `_rptFooterLabelText`, the same text as the PDF footer) at a centre tab, with the PAGE / NUMPAGES fields at the right tab (`_docxApplyPageNumberFooters`). Runs in the browser at report render and Word download; no timer, no env var. Check: `node tools/test-report-word-table-widths.js`, `node tools/test-report-word-layout.js`; export Word, open it in Word and compare with the PDF (header centered, no unit split, footer label).

## Quarterly report: method text, billed kW formula, one rounding (2026-11-05)
- What: `_rptQWeatherMethod` and `_rptQSavingsFormula` (app/report-engine.js) are the single statements of the baseline method; page 1, page 2, Appendix A and Appendix B print them. kWh and therms baseline = the 2025 weather model at baseline-year weather. Billed kW baseline = `fitKwCdd` (computations/regression.js), a fit of billed kW on cooling degree days, evaluated at the cooling degree days of the month reported. `getMeterSavings` (computations/savings.js) returns the fit as `blKwFit`; Appendix B prints it per meter (`_rptQBilledKwBlock`). No number changed.
- Rounding: `_rptQFacts` keeps month figures unrounded; each cell rounds once when printed. Appendix D prints `rawBills.billDatePrinted` (the bill record's own bill date, M/D/YYYY), never a service date. Appendix B packs buildings per page by measured height (`_rptQBodyFits`).
- Emissions: `_rptQEmissionChange`, the Environmental Impact page and the board sentence call `emissionsChangeText`, `energyUseChangeLine`, `EMISSIONS_SIGN_NOTE` and `emissionsSourceLine` (computations/pollution.js).
- Runs: in the browser when a quarterly report preview is built (user time zone). No timer, no env var.
- Check: `node tools/test-report-q3-review-findings.js` (sections 6b and 11). On a Q3 project backup, Appendix B Billed kW formula x cooling degree days must give the Baseline billed kW column.

## Quarterly report final fixes (2026-11-05)

No timer, env var or host change. Runs in the browser when a quarterly report preview is built (user time zone).
- Rounding: `_rptControlledRound(values2d, rowTotals, colTotals)` (app/report-engine.js) rounds a table so every row and column foots (max-flow). `_rptQFacts` uses it for the building by month summary; the quarter total is the true total rounded.
- Shared text: `_rptQTerms` (definitions, Appendix A and page 1), `_rptQChangeRule`, `_rptQSavingsRule`, `_rptQNoBreakMinus` (U+2011 minus in every number, applied in `_rptQuarterReportHTML`), `_rptContractSpan/ProgressPct/ProgressText/PeriodText` (contract period and progress from the contract dates).
- Layout: board summary is part of page 1 (no separate page); Appendix D column "Billing period" (start to end from the bill record); Appendix B uses "Change" = actual minus baseline; propane chart removed; Operating Hours and Set Points prints compact on one page.
- `emissionsSourceLine` (computations/pollution.js) prints the state name (Kansas), not the code.
- Check: `node tools/test-report-q3-review-findings.js`, `node tools/test-pollution-factors.js`, `node tools/test-report-schedule-table.js`.

## Storage fallback (2026-11-06)

- IndexedDB open (app/db.js `_open`, `_openOnce`): if the first open fails, it waits 300 ms (`IDB_OPEN_RETRY_MS`) and tries once more. A browser with no `indexedDB` object is not retried. Two failures run `warmCache` fallback mode (`_usingFallback`), and the page shows the existing one-time toast "Couldn't load saved data from storage".
- Fallback mode (app/db.js `_fallbackSave`, called from `_rawSet` and `set`): all data stays in memory (`_cache`); the server holds it on the synced site. A key whose JSON is over `FALLBACK_LS_MAX_CHARS` (200000) is not written to localStorage; its older localStorage copy and its `ch_rv::` stamp are removed (the current value is in memory and on the server), so the next load pulls it from the server. `ch_sync_queue` and `en_conflict_archive` are always tried, whatever their size. A failed localStorage write shows the existing `TOAST_SAVE_FAILED`, at most once per 30 s (`FALLBACK_TOAST_GAP_MS`). No banner. The conflict history always stores the full losing value.
- Check: `node tools/test-storage-fallback-archive.js` (IndexedDB forced to fail with 12 MB of data and a 5.2M-char localStorage quota). Also `node test-sync-golive-blockers.js` and `node tools/test-conflict-auto-resolve.js`.

## Quarterly report: model fit, baseline wording, entered months, contract totals (2026-11-06)
- What: Appendix B prints R2, the number of baseline months and CV(RMSE) after every baseline formula (`_rptQFitText`, app/report-engine.js; kWh, therms and billed kW). CV(RMSE) = sqrt(sum of squared residuals / (n - parameters)) / mean monthly use, computed ONCE by `cvRmse` (computations/regression.js:4). `modelCvRmse` (same file) applies it to a degree-day model over the baseline rows; `collectReportData` stores it in `regrCoeffs.cvrmse` with `r2` and `n`. `fitKwCdd` now returns `r2` and `cvrmse` with intercept, slope and n and calls `olsRegression` (the same fit as before, same coefficients). NMBE is not printed: the bias correction in `computeMeterRegression` makes the sum of predicted use equal the sum of actual use, so NMBE is 0 by construction. No coefficient, formula or savings figure changed.
- Wording (`_rptQTerms`, `_rptResultKind`, `_rptQContractBody`): the baseline is "a weather model of the use of the meter ... evaluated at the 2025 weather" (billed kW: the weather of the month reported); "Savings target" is "Allocated target" everywhere. The contract page is "Contract Totals": billed to date plus the contract targets, and the 3.5% annual energy cost escalation is attributed to the Agreement (Savings, Projected Savings). The benchmark note prints "2012" and the ENERGY STAR Portfolio Manager U.S. national median table for the K-12 value 48.5 only.
- Entered months: `collectReportData` sets `bd.baselineEntered` (calendar month index to fuels) for each baseline month whose bill has no bill file (`billHasPdf` false, month by `billMonth`). `_rptQBaselineDataBody` prints "<month>, <fuel> entered" (no note line). Overrides in `meter.baseline.overrides` (typed baseline use for Jan to Mar 2025) are not read by this table.
- Runs: in the browser when a quarterly report preview is built (user time zone). No timer, no env var.
- Check: `node tools/test-report-q3-review-findings.js` section 13 (hand-checked CV(RMSE), kW fit coefficients, Appendix B text, wording, entered marks). `node tools/test-report-word-table-widths.js` (the Results table grid is pinned with the new column name).

## Report page footer zone (2026-11-06, branch 2026-11-06-appb-footer-overlap)
- What: `RPT_Q_PAGE_BODY_PX` (app/report-engine.js, next to `_rptQBodyFits`) is the tallest page body of a quarterly report page: 830px. The footer line (page number and label) is 922px from the page top; the body starts at 72px (60px header + 12px padding); 20px stay clear. It was 900, so a full page ran into the footer. `_rptQBodyFits` is the one test every packing step uses (Appendix B `_rptQAppendixBPages`, ). A table that does not fit moves to the next page whole, with its heading and formula line.
- Runs: in the browser when a quarterly report preview is built. No timer, no env var.
- Check: `node tools/test-report-table-fit.js` builds Appendix B for 9 synthetic buildings and fails when any page content is within 16px of the footer or a page ends with a heading. Also look at every page of the real PDF and Word export (release v2026.11.06.241).

## Savings figure colour, quarterly report (2026-11-06, branch 2026-11-06-status-savings-color)

`_rptSavVar(v, dark)` in app/report-engine.js is the one colour of a savings figure: green above $0, red below $0, page text at $0 (sign by `_rptQSavCls`, the same rule as the report tables). The Building Status cards on page 2 and the Target vs Actual savings figure on page 1 call it. The status words (On Track, Below Target) keep the colour of `_rptResultVar`. Runs when a report is built; no schedule, no env var. Check: `node tools/test-report-q3-review-findings.js` (a card with savings above $0 and Below Target shows the savings green and the status red).
- Quarterly report footer and Observations page (2026-11-06): the "<period> Quarterly Report" label and "Page N of M" sit BELOW the wave at the bottom edge on every page (`rptPage`, `RPT_PAGENUM_DIV` bottom 10px right 24px, label bottom 10px with z-index 1 so the wave does not cover it; the approved placement is commit 34c919bd). `rptPageObservations` measures each building paragraph in the DOM (`_rptMeasureHtmlH`) and reserves the Weather and Next Quarter height on the last page (`_rptPaginateWithTail`, budget `RPT_Q_PAGE_BODY_PX`), so the page breaks only when the content does not fit. The old code assumed 150px per building. No timer, no env var. Check: `node tools/test-report-observations-one-page.js`, then look at the Observations page of the real PDF.
- No explanation under report tables (2026-11-06, branch 2026-11-06-v242-combined): the sign lines ("Change = ...", "plus means ..."), the chart colour key and the Appendix C "Bold rows ..." line are removed from `_rptQuarterReportHTML` pages (app/report-engine.js: Cover, Results, Energy Use, fuel Use Detail, Building Summary, Year to Date, Appendix B intro, Appendix C). `_rptQChangeRule` and `_rptQSavingsRule` are deleted. Kept: the Contract Totals basis note (names the Agreement escalation) and the Appendix A/B method formulas. Check: `node tools/test-report-q3-review-findings.js`; search the real PDF text for "plus means".
## Quarterly report: every checked section prints a page again (2026-11-06, branch 2026-11-06-restore-sections)
- What: `_rptQuarterReportHTML` (app/report-engine.js, about :4232) loops over `REPORT_SECTIONS` (about :13621) in table order, which is the order of the preview sidebar. A row prints its page when its checkbox is on (`s[key] !== false`, or the row's `qOn`). The row's `qRender(c)` returns a page string, `{ html, pageCount, tagged }` or null; `qEmpty(d)` or `qNone(d)` give the plain "No ... in Q3 2026." page when there is nothing to print. v239 (bf068dbf) replaced this loop with a fixed page list, so 11 sections had no page. Sections: cover (also the Board Executive Summary row, `sameAs: 'cover'`), financial, savings performance, environmental impact, observations, approved changes, operating hours and set points, per-building summaries (`_rptQBuildingSummaryPage`, baseline Q3 EUI against actual Q3 EUI from `b.euiQ`), meter performance with baseline data, electricity / natural gas / propane use detail (`_rptQFuelDetailPages`: baseline cost is `b.<fuel>.costBl`, the sum of `blCostByCalMo` the Financial Summary uses; the meter table names meters with `_rptMeterLabel`), site EUI benchmark, year-to-date trend, contract totals, appendices A to C (D is off by default). `_rptQStdPage` puts a standard-report page in the quarterly table style.
- Preview sidebar (app/report-preview.js): a row with `sameAs` shows the page, number and checkbox of the named row (`_rptPageKeyOf`). Unchecking a section hides its page and the continuation pages that follow it (`togglePreviewSection`).
- Runs: in the browser when a quarterly report preview is built (user time zone). No timer, no env var.
- Check: `CH_TMP_ROOT=<scratch> node tools/test-report-all-sections-render.js` (every default-on section prints a page, in table order; unchecking removes the page; meter label; baseline cost). Real check: build the Q3 2026 report of a real project, read the sidebar page numbers, export PDF and Word, look at every page.

## Quarterly report: rounding, actual cost, meter names, kW separators, propane baseline price (2026-11-08, branch 2026-11-08-q3-word-review-fixes)

No timer, no env var, no host change. All of it runs in the browser when a report preview is built.

- One rounding rule: `_rptQFacts` (app/report-engine.js:2805) rounds every dollar value from its own unrounded value. `f.parts[id]` = electricity, gas, propane, `total`, `elecEnergy`, `elecDemand` of a building; `f.partsTotal` = the same for the site, each the rounded sum of unrounded values. No printed value is a total less another printed value, so a row can differ from the sum of its parts by $1. `_rptQBillGroups` (:3846) rounds each meter from its own amount; `_rptQBilledOf` (:3897) returns the exact sum and the table rounds it once. Appendix D rounds each bill and each month total from the exact amounts. Savings are unchanged: only the print rounding changed. Month cells of the savings-by-month table still use `_rptControlledRound` (each cell is its floor or ceiling).
- One actual cost (Matt, 2026-11-08: energy charges only): `_rptBillAmount(bill, commodity)` (app/report-engine.js:44) = electric kWh + billed kW + facilities dollars (`getBillKwhCost`, `getBillKwCost`, `getBillFacKWCost`), gas the gas charge (`getBillGasCost`), propane the delivered cost. Customer charge, fees and tax are never in it. `collectReportData` sums it for electric, gas and propane actual cost (`costCur`, `curCost`, monthly `curCost`) and sets `rawBills.amount` from it, so the meter tables, Appendix D, the percent base and Building Performance all print the same basis as the baseline cost and as the presented Q1 and Q2 reports. A building's Building Performance actual cost = the sum of its commodity costs on the Use Detail and meter pages. No bill-total path is left.
- Meter names: `_rptMeterTd` (:2424) writes `white-space:nowrap`; the .docx writer reads that as `data-dx-nw` and widens the Word column. `_RPT_Q_W_METERS` (:2553) = 28/34/9/16/13 percent.
- Baseline Data kW: `_buildBaselineDataHtml` (app/calculators.js:686 `$k`) prints kW with thousands separators.
- Propane baseline price: `_rptPropaneBlCostMap` (:52) fills a baseline month that has gallons and a $0 cost with gallons x the usage-weighted all-in $/gal of the baseline-year deliveries (`getMonthBillRate(..., 'propane')`). The stored contract baseline is copied, never written. No priced delivery = no rate and no change. Display only: `getMeterSavings` does not read it.
- Check: `node tools/test-report-q3-review-findings.js` (section 14, synthetic data). In the browser: Building Summary and the "by Building" table print the same savings for a building; Building Performance actual cost = the sum of the Use Detail actual costs; the Natural Gas Meters names are on one line.

## Quarterly report: display fixes from the v249 review (2026-11-08, version v2026.11.08.250)

No timer, no env var, no host change. All of it runs in the browser when a report preview is built. No stored bill value and no savings figure changes.

- Year-to-Date table (`rptPageYearToDate`, app/report-engine.js:8132): the Savings column and the Baseline and Actual columns total the rows shown. "Q3 2026 measured savings (not billed)" is a memo row after the total (red when negative). The difference row = unrounded measured Q1+Q2 minus the presented total.
- One header case: `_rptQThSameCase` (:4408) prints every table header in sentence case ("Baseline cost", "Propane gal", "$/gal") on the final HTML of the quarterly report.
- Meter tables (lib/perf-table.js `buildMeterPerfTableHTML`, report mode): kWh $ and kW $ cents are apportioned so they add to the Total $ of the row (:112); font 11 px in the page (about 8 pt printed); header "billed kW" with a no-break space; a gas or propane month with usage shows `$0.00` instead of a dash.
- Baseline Data (`_buildBaselineDataHtml`, app/calculators.js, report mode only): billed kWh, facilities kW cost and billed therms columns; Average columns divide by the billed value shown; header names "kWh cost per kWh" and "kW cost per billed kW"; the Total Utility column is dropped when the building has no gas or propane (:759). `rptBuildSiteBaselineData` (:4502) sizes each column from its longest value.
- Appendix A (`_rptQTerms` :2728, `_rptQMethodPage` :4175, `_rptQMedianText` :3530): the baseline is "the 2025 use in the same month"; terms for Facilities kW, Normalized use, Load factor, quarter and annual EUI; the contract total, annualized EUI and national median text moved here from the contract and EUI pages.
- Environmental Impact (`rptPageEnvironmentalImpact` :7021): equivalents say "Equal to the CO2 of ..." with the EPA basis (one year); `_equivalentsOf` (computations/pollution.js:194) uses `Math.floor` for tanker trucks, railcars and power plants, so a value below 1 is not shown.
- Check: `node tools/test-report-q3-review-findings.js` (section 17), `node tools/test-report-page-packing.js` (31 pages), `node tools/test-gas-cost-single-source.js`. In the browser: Reports > Quarterly report, page 23 totals add; page 6 shows no tanker, railcar or power plant line.

## Quarterly report: Q3 2026 report fixes (2026-11-08, version v2026.11.08.252)

No timer, no env var, no host change. All of it runs in the browser when a report preview or a Word export is built. No savings formula, stored bill value or cost basis changes. A presented (frozen) report keeps its old branches.

- Printed months (`_rptMonthsShown`, app/report-engine.js:2668): the months of one quarter are apportioned (`_rptApportion`, largest fraction first) to the quarter total, so the months on pages 1 and 23 add to the printed quarter (Q3 -$6,496 = $8,048 - $5,294 - $9,250). This replaces the "each month is its own round" rule of v245 for the unpresented quarter.
- Use change (`_rptUseChange`, :2694): the rounded exact difference (actual minus baseline). Building Summary, fuel detail and Appendix B call it, so one use prints one change on every page.
- Cost column names (`_rptCostHeads`, :2701): the rate year is in the name ("Baseline cost (2025 rates)", "Actual cost (2026 rates)", "Savings measured (2026 rates)"). Pages 3, 4, 5, 21, 22 and 23 call it. No second copy of these words.
- Propane headers: `_rptQThSameCase` (:4475) writes "Propane delivered (gal)" for any "Propane Gal" header (Building Summary, Baseline Data, fuel detail).
- Equivalents (computations/pollution.js:155): `EQUIV_PER_MT_CO2E` holds the factors of the EPA Greenhouse Gas Equivalencies Calculator "Calculations and References" page (updated August 4, 2026); `EQUIV_SOURCE` is the text printed on page 6. The count is the metric tons of CO2e times the factor, rounded up (existing rule).
- Fuel detail widths: `_RPT_Q_W_FUEL`, `_RPT_Q_W_FUEL_ELEC`, `_RPT_Q_W_METERS` (:2578-2580) and the Word widths `_RPT_Q_WDX_*` give the Building column room. `tools/test-report-word-table-widths.js` pins the Word widths.
- Page 23 bottom rows: Measured Q1+Q2, Measured Q3, Total measured, then billed to date, difference (measured minus billed) and Savings billed to date.
- Appendix A: Terms add the units, set point, Acct# and Site EUI (`_rptQTerms`, app/report-engine.js). The text does not spell out "kilowatt" (a standing rule of `tools/test-report-units-no-next-steps.js`).
- Check: `node tools/test-report-q3-review-findings.js` (section 19), `node tools/test-report-page-packing.js`, `node tools/test-report-word-table-widths.js`, `node tools/test-report-table-fit.js`. In the report preview: 31 pages, page 22 holds the gas and propane tables, page 1 months add to -$6,496.

## Quarterly report: use change equals the printed numbers (2026-11-08, version v2026.11.08.253)

No timer, no env var, no host change. It runs in the browser when a report preview or a Word export is built. No savings formula, stored bill value or cost basis changes. A presented (frozen) report keeps its printed figures.

- Use change (`_rptUseChange`, app/report-engine.js:2696): the printed actual minus the printed baseline, each as `Math.round` of its own value (the same rounding as `_rptQN`). Building Summary, the fuel total line, the kW table and Appendix B call it, in month rows and in Total rows (pass the total baseline and total actual). This replaces the v252 rule (rounded exact difference), which could differ by 1 from the two numbers beside it.
- Meter tables (lib/perf-table.js, report mode, not a presented report): the saved column (`kWh saved`, `Therms saved`, `gal saved`) is printed baseline minus printed actual per month; the Total row is the printed total baseline minus the printed total actual. The percent stays from the exact values. The tab (site) table keeps the exact value.
- Year-to-Date (app/report-engine.js, Total measured block): the billed figure is one row, "Savings billed to date (Q1+Q2, presented to the district)", under "Difference (measured minus billed)".
- Appendix A Terms: "Contract time elapsed". Degree Days table: an empty cell is an em dash. Summary by Building (page 5): fixed layout with widths (colgroup and `data-dx-widths`), Period cells do not wrap.

In the preview: an example month of gas: baseline 536, actual 809, change +273; an example Total row: 167,676, 229,993, +62,317.

No timer, no env var, no host change. It runs in the browser when a report preview or a Word export is built. No savings formula, stored bill value or cost basis changes. A presented (frozen) report keeps its printed figures.

## Quarterly report: totals add from the rows shown (2026-11-09, versions v2026.11.09.256 and .257)

No timer, no env var, no host change. It runs in the browser when a report preview or a Word export is built. No savings formula, stored bill value or cost basis changes. A presented (frozen) report keeps its printed figures. Every total keeps its true value (the rounded exact sum); only a row can print one unit (0.1 kW in the meter tables) different from its own rounding.

- One display rule: `_rptShownRows(values, dp)` (lib/formatting.js:51) apportions the rows of one column to the rounded exact sum with `_rptApportion(values, total, dp)` (lib/formatting.js:20, moved from app/report-engine.js; largest fraction first, `dp` decimals). Callers: `_rptQShownCols` (app/report-engine.js:9402, the Monthly Use table of Building Summaries, and Appendix B kWh and therms via the same function, so a month reads the same on pages 9-14 and 26-31 and the Utility Bills use is split from it), the Billed kW table of Appendix B (`_rptQBilledKwBlock`, :4812), the by-building tables and month charts of the fuel detail pages (`_rptQFuelDetailPages`, :3995), the meter tables (lib/perf-table.js:129, report mode of a period not presented: kWh, therms, gal and the two 0.1 kW columns), and `_rptBldgCostShown`.
- Building costs: `_rptBldgCostShown(d, b, key)` (app/report-engine.js:2653) prints the baseline cost or the actual cost of a building apportioned across the buildings, for a report not presented (`_rptUnpresentedQuarter`). Financial Summary, Summary by Building (page 5) and the building sentences of Observations call it; the Financial Summary target cost uses `projShown` (:5915). A figure that appears in a by-building table and a by-fuel table can differ by 1 between the two tables, because each table adds to its own total.
- Use change colour: `_rptQChangePctTd` (:2572) is the one cell for a use change percent (red above baseline, green below, plain at 0.0% and dashes). Pages 5 (EUI cell has its own text), 9-11, 21, 22 and 23 call it.
- Appendix A (`_rptQTerms`, :2782): Terms add the contract share (`share`) and the Baseline Data columns (`baseCols`), carry one definition of the allocated target (the Method bullet), and state the Site EUI split (baseline = weather-model use, actual = billed use). The Method and Terms lists use line-height 1.3 so Appendix A stays on one page.
- Check: `node tools/test-report-totals-add.js` (scans every total row of a generated report), `node tools/test-report-q3-review-findings.js`, `node tools/test-report-word-table-widths.js`, `node tools/test-presented-printed-values.js`. Not covered: gas savings by building (exact dollar savings, +/-$1) and the all-buildings rows of the by-building tables against the building pages (each adds to its own total).

## Quarterly report: one printed value for each figure (2026-11-09, version v2026.11.09.258)

No timer, no env var, no host change. It runs in the browser when a report preview or a Word export is built. No savings formula, stored bill value or cost basis changes. A presented (frozen) report keeps its plain rounding.

- `_rptShown(d)` (app/report-engine.js) computes the printed whole numbers once for each report data object (WeakMap memo). Use series: portfolio, buildings, months. Cost: portfolio, buildings, fuels. Each level adds to its parent (largest remainder, `_rptApportion` in lib/formatting.js).
- Every page and chart reads the registry (`_rptShownUse`, `_rptShownMonthUse`, `_rptShownCost`, `_rptShownCostColumn`). The Baseline Data pages (`_buildBaselineDataHtml`, app/calculators.js) and the meter tables (`buildMeterPerfTableHTML`, lib/perf-table.js) take the registry months through `opts.fixed` and `opts.shownUse`.

- Check: `node tools/test-report-totals-add.js` (section 3b: the tiers add, and a figure on two pages is one value), `node tools/test-report-word-table-widths.js`, `node tools/test-report-table-fit.js`.

No timer, no env var, no host change. No savings formula, stored bill value or cost basis changes.

- `_rptYear1Target(d)` (app/report-engine.js) is the one Year 1 contract target (sum of the four quarterly targets). The Contract to Date table row and the Appendix A Contract totals bullet both read it.
- Appendix A Terms (`_rptQTerms`): `target` is the allocated target definition again, `measured` has the billed kW term, `negative` names the rates of the month reported. Passenger cars on Environmental Impact use 4.29 t CO2e per vehicle (`EQUIV_PER_MT_CO2E.carsRemoved`, pollution.js); 34.135 t gives 8.
